## V A Chorus Girl at Sebo’s / 五 塞博俱乐部的歌舞女郎

===Original===
Sebo’s Club was crowded, for it was the dinner hour and Sebo’s is the most extensively patronized of the dining clubs. Here, all that was beautiful, all that was smart, all that was famous and brilliant in the world of society, letters and the drama met on common ground﻿—the inherent and universal desire which humanity has for careless comfort. A Cabinet Minister and his party sat at the next table to that presided over by a great revue actress; the owner of a Derby winner sat back to back against a famous Radical satirist. The editor of a great London daily could look across his table and without shifting his eyes could count in his field of vision the pretty dancer from the Empiredrome, a royal physician, a peer of the realm and a ragtime singer.
The big dining hall blazed with lights, the little tables were crowded together so as to leave scarcely room for the waiters who, by some mysterious dispensation of Providence, seemed able to thread their ways through impossible spaces. The noisy coon band kept up its rhythmic pandemonium in one corner of the room, but did not drown the rippling laughter and the buzz of lighthearted talk.
In the little vestibule a young man, very tall and very thin, paced the tesselated floor with that evidence of resignation which tells so eloquently the story of the Unpunctual Guest. He was very fair and very pink. His countenance was vacant and the vacancy was by no means relieved when he screwed a gold-rimmed monocle into his right eye.

===Chinese===
塞博俱乐部里人满为患——正是晚餐时分，而塞博是宾客最盛的餐饮俱乐部。举凡社交界、文坛、戏剧界一切美丽的、时髦的、知名的、耀眼的人物，都在此汇聚一堂，理由人皆有之——人类天性都贪图安逸的享乐。一位内阁大臣和他的随员，就坐在某位当红歌舞剧女主角的邻桌；一位德比赛马冠军马主，与著名的激进派讽刺作家背靠背而坐。伦敦某大报的主编连眼珠都不必转，视线之内便能数出帝国游艺剧场来的俏丽舞女、一位御医、一位国会上院贵族，还有一位唱拉格泰姆的歌手。
宽敞的大餐厅灯火辉煌，小餐桌挤得密密匝匝，几乎没给侍者留下立足的空隙；可他们凭着老天爷某种神秘的安排，竟能从根本通不过的缝里穿行自如。喧闹的黑人乐队在厅堂一角敲着狂乱的节拍，却盖不住银铃般的笑声和轻快的谈话声。
小小门厅里，一个又高又瘦的青年在镶嵌地砖的地板上来回踱步，那副逆来顺受的架势，分明在无声诉说“客人迟迟不至”的故事。他肤色白皙，脸蛋红润，神情茫然；待他把一片金边单眼镜旋进右眼，那份茫然丝毫未见减轻。

===Original===
Presently the glass doors swung and a girl came hurriedly toward him, holding out her gloved hand.
“I am awfully sorry I am late, Reggie,” she said with easy familiarity.
“If you were an hour late or five hours late or a day late,” said the young man with gentle ecstasy, “I should be content to wait, Miss Flemming.”
She flashed a dazzling smile at him.
“I shouldn’t be horribly shocked if you called me Vera,” she said.

===Chinese===
不一会儿，玻璃门一转，一位姑娘匆匆向他走来，伸出戴着手套的手。
“真抱歉我来迟了，雷吉。”她熟不拘礼地说。
“您就是迟到一个钟头、五个钟头，或者一整天，”青年带着温柔的痴迷说，“我也甘愿等，弗莱明小姐。”
她朝他投来灿烂的一笑。
“您要是叫我维拉，我也不至于吓着。”她说。

===Original===
The young man went pinker than ever, coughed, stuttered, ran his gloved finger inside the high upstanding collar about his thin throat, dropped his eyeglass, retrieved it and did all this in the space of four seconds, thereby betraying his perturbation and his gratitude.
“You have a table, I suppose?” said the girl when she had returned from depositing her coat.
“Rather!” said the young man, and added after a second’s thought, “Rather!”
He fussily shepherded her through the mass of tables where his own attenuation enabled him to emulate the deeds of the agile serving man and brought her to a corner table which was smothered with rare flowers. Heads were turned, sharp eyes focused the couple, some smiled, though for the girl the glances held nothing but admiration or cold-blooded appraisement, according to the sex of the observer.

===Chinese===
青年的脸涨得更红了：咳嗽、结巴、拿戴手套的手指去抠细脖子上高高竖起的硬领、单片眼镜掉下来又捞起来——这一切统共只费了四秒钟，把他心里的慌乱和感激一齐暴露无遗。
“您想必订好桌位了吧？”姑娘寄存完大衣回来问道。
“那还用说！”青年说，想了一秒钟又添一句，“那还用说！”
他手忙脚乱地领她穿过重重餐桌——亏得自己身形清瘦，才学得了那些腿脚伶俐的侍者的本领——把她带到一张被名贵鲜花堆满的角落餐桌。人头纷纷转过来，锐利的目光聚在这对男女身上，有人莞尔一笑。不过落在姑娘身上的视线，除了爱慕便是冷冰冰的估价，全看旁观者是男是女。

===Original===
“Reggie Boltover!” said one young man.
“Who is Reggie Boltover?” asked his companion.
“A human being loosely attached to a million,” was the laconic description.
The girl was radiant, the smile hardly left her face and the eyes which glanced shyly up to her tall companion were full of wonder and delight.

===Chinese===
“雷吉·博尔托弗！”一个青年男子说。
“雷吉·博尔托弗是谁？”他的同伴问。
“一个松松垮垮拴着一百万的人。”回答言简意赅。
姑娘神采飞扬，笑意几乎没有离开过她的脸；她羞怯地抬眼望向高个子同伴时，那双眼睛里满是好奇与欢喜。

===Original===
“So this is Sebo’s,” she said. “Isn’t it a dreadfully wicked place?”
Reggie Boltover’s face creased alarmingly﻿—he, too, was smiling.
“My dear Miss﻿—my dear Vera,” he said boldly, “should I bring you to a wicked place, now I ask you; should I bring you to a wicked place, should I?”
His conversational powers were not brilliant but his heart was pure. He was not really a wicked young man about town and his chief wickedness lay in his implicit belief that he was. He had met the girl one night by accident. A more daring friend of his, and nearer approaching Reggie’s own ideal of doggishness, had induced him (he protesting feebly) to call at a stage-door where he was meeting a charming friend to take her to supper. The charming friend in the generous large-hearted way of chorus girls had introduced *her* friend, Vera Flemming, a newcomer to the ranks of the chorus, and they had all supped together and Vera had been very charming to Mr. Reggie Boltover and he had asked her to go with him up the river and had serious thoughts, because of her evident refinement, of introducing her to his mother, which shows that Reggie had reached the most dangerous stage of infatuation. There was really nothing wrong about Reggie Boltover and nothing remarkably terrible about this strangely initiated friendship.

===Chinese===
“原来这就是塞博。”她说，“这地方坏得吓人吧？”
雷吉·博尔托弗的脸起了吓人的褶子——他自己也在笑。
“亲爱的弗莱明小姐——亲爱的维拉，”他壮起胆子说，“您说，我会带您去不良场所吗？我问您呢；我会带您去不良场所吗，会吗？”
他的谈吐说不上高明，心地却纯正。他其实并非当真是城里寻欢作乐的恶少；他最大的恶，在于笃信自己就是。他和这姑娘是一天晚上偶然相识的。他有个朋友胆子大得多，也更接近雷吉自己心摹神追的“浪荡”理想，撺掇着他（他还软塌塌地推让了几句）到一处舞台后门去——那位朋友要在那儿接一位可爱的朋友去吃夜宵。那位可爱的朋友本着歌舞女郎慷慨大方的心肠，介绍了自己的朋友维拉·弗莱明——歌队伍里的新丁——于是四人同吃了一顿夜宵；维拉对雷吉·博尔托弗先生非常亲切，他便约她同去河上游玩，还因为她显然知书达礼，认真考虑把她引见给自己的母亲——可见雷吉已到了痴迷最危险的阶段。雷吉·博尔托弗实在没有半点不轨，这段起因古怪的友谊也没有什么了不得的可怕之处。

===Original===
Chorus girls are merely shopgirls with a taste for caviar and peaches. They are no more sinful than their sisters in the same social strata and the only difference between them is that, whilst they are exposed to similar temptations, the chorus girl has a larger field to pick from and the candidates are much more presentable. A shopgirl accepts the hospitality of a teashop, the chorus-girl goes to the Ritz. Both have one consuming passion, a desire for good food, for which they do not have to pay.
Reggie Boltover, who, to do him justice, knew everybody, entertained the girl for half-an-hour by pointing out the various celebrities in the room and Vera Flemming was interested without being enthusiastically so.
“I would rather you talked about yourself,” she said, “you are ever so much more interesting than these people.”
“Oh, no,” said Reggie, with a little giggle; “oh, no!”

===Chinese===
歌舞女郎不过是口味偏重鱼子酱和蜜桃的女店员。她们并不比同一阶层的姐妹们更有罪；唯一的分别是：虽然两下里面对的诱惑不相上下，歌舞女郎挑选的余地更大，候补的人选也体面得多。女店员领受的是茶馆的殷勤，歌舞女郎去的是丽兹饭店。两者同有一腔炽热的嗜好——巴望着吃一顿不必自己破钞的好饭。
雷吉·博尔托弗——说句公道话，他确实是无人不识——指点着厅中各位名流，足足招待了姑娘半个钟头；维拉·弗莱明听得有味，却谈不上热烈。
“我倒情愿您谈谈您自己。”她说，“您可比这些人有意思多啦。”
“哦，不，”雷吉说着轻轻傻笑了一声，“哦，不！”

===Original===
“You are, indeed, you are,” she said earnestly.
“Oh, come,” said Reggie; “oh, come! no! I am not interesting; oh, dear no!”
His life he admitted frankly was very ordinary. All that he did was to sign a few cheques, liquidate a few debts, see a few “fellows” about “things” and “there you are,” said Reggie.
“It must be wonderful to be in a position of power,” said the girl musingly. “Of course, I come from a very poor family. We only think in shillings where you think in thousands of pounds. And it is awfully hard to realize what it feels like to order people to do things instead of being ordered.”

===Chinese===
“您有意思，真的，您有意思。”她恳切地说。
“哎呀，哪里，”雷吉说，“哎呀，哪里！不！我这人最没意思了；哎呀，天哪，可不是！”
他坦白承认，自己的日子平淡无奇。他干的不过是在几张支票上签字、清几笔账、为几桩“事情”见几个“家伙”，“就这么回事”，雷吉说。
“大权在握想必妙得很。”姑娘若有所思地说，“当然啦，我家里穷得很。你们动辄按千镑盘算，我们只按先令思量。那种对人发号施令、而不是听人差遣的滋味，可真难想象。”

===Original===
Reggie Boltover, who had never ordered anybody to do anything in his life and would not have dared to dispute the judgment of the innumerable managers and directors whom his sainted father had appointed in his lifetime, wondered himself what it felt like. He had often meditated, with a shudder, upon the necessity which might one day arise, for his taking the initiative in the conduct of his business. He dimly realized that, in time, all his managers and directors would die and he had dimly speculated upon the question as to who would replace them. He had a feeling that perhaps one might go to Whiteleys and order some new ones, but it had never occurred to him that at his autocratic word managers and people of that description could be made out of mud, or that an order affecting the business which he was supposed to control would be acted upon if he were to give that order.
“Well, you know,” he said, “I never really tell people to do anything. You see, I never see them except very occasionally. Of course, they make reports and all that sort of thing and I have a man who reads them so everything is all right and I just sign cheques and see a few fellows and there you are.”
Under the genial influence of her sympathetic interest he expanded a little and proved that he was not as wholly incompetent as he pretended to be. For instance, he knew that the iron works and shipbuilding yard which still bore his father’s name, and incidentally his own, made “a deuced lot of money” every year and that certain other properties made no money.
There was one property of which he spoke with great bitterness but only because his father, in his lifetime, had also spoken of that matter with similar violence and asperity. Apparently, the one redeeming feature about Boltover’s Cement Works lay in the fact that it had no manager and therefore produced no reports. It was in fact a deserted shell of a building so infamously unprofitable that Boltover senior (now in Heaven) had directed almost with his last breath, if you believed Reggie, that his name should be erased from the official designation of the company.

===Chinese===
雷吉·博尔托弗这辈子没对任何人发过号施令，也断然不敢驳回他那位圣明的先父生前委任的无数经理和董事的决断——此刻他自己也发起琢磨来：那究竟是什么滋味。他常打着寒噤思量：有朝一日，生意上的事兴许要他来拿主意。他隐隐约约地明白，那些经理董事迟早都要死的，也曾隐隐约约地推想过谁来顶替。他总觉得，或者可以去怀特利商场订做一批新的；可他从没想过，只消他一句专断的话，经理之流的人物就能从泥里捏出来；更没想过，他名义上管辖的买卖，只要他一声吩咐，人家当真会照办。
“呃，您知道，”他说，“我从来不当真差人做事。您瞧，我难得同他们见面，偶尔才见一见。当然，报告之类他们照做，我雇了个人替我念，所以万事妥帖；我只管签支票、见几个家伙，就这么回事。”
在她体恤关怀的和煦熏染下，他稍稍舒展开来，证明自己并非如他装出的那样一无可取。譬如说，他知道那仍挂着父亲名号（顺带一提，也挂着他自己的名号）的炼铁厂和造船厂每年“赚一大笔钱”，而另外几处产业则分文不赚。
有一处产业，他提起时怨气冲天——其实只因为他父亲生前说起这桩事也是同样的疾言厉色、刻薄万分。显然，博尔托弗水泥厂唯一可取之处，在于它没有经理，因此不出报告。那实际上是一栋废弃的空壳厂房，亏折得声名狼藉，以致老博尔托弗（如今在天国）几乎咽气时还留下遗命——你若信雷吉的话——要把自己的名字从公司的正式名称里抹去。

===Original===
“You see it was bad cement; you know how cement is made, don’t you?”
“I should love to,” said the girl, her eyes shining, “I have often wondered.”
“Well,” said Reggie looking round the table for something to illustrate the object lesson, “you dig in the river and you take out a lot of stuff and you chuck it in a cart and then you chuck it into a fire and you pull it out and do something to it and there you are! That’s cement. Only our cement wasn’t cement, if you understand. That is what made the beastly thing so awkward.”
“How wonderful!” said the girl. “I shall always remember that.”

===Chinese===
“您瞧，那是劣质水泥。您知道水泥怎么造的吧？”
“我巴不得知道呢。”姑娘眼睛发亮地说，“我一直纳闷来着。”
“是这样，”雷吉说着，四下打量餐桌，想找点什么做直观教具，“您在河里挖，挖出一大堆东西，倒进车里，再把它们倒进火里，拉出来捣鼓几下，得，水泥就成了！只可惜我们的水泥压根儿不是水泥，您明白吧。这正是那破玩意儿难办的地方。”
“神了！”姑娘说，“我一辈子都记着。”

===Original===
“Of course, we’ve got our eyes open,” said Reggie now fairly launched upon the story of his life, “and one of these days we shall catch a mug.”
“Catch a﻿—?” asked the girl, puzzled.
Reggie went very pink, but he was excited and grateful at this demonstration of the girl’s refinement.
“Forgive the vulgarity, Miss﻿—Vera; I mean we shall find a purchaser. I once nearly sold the beastly thing for £10,000 and the day the deed was to be signed, they took the poor chap away to a lunatic asylum, poor old bird, not right in his head, you know. That is why he wanted to buy our cement works. Comic, isn’t it?

===Chinese===
“当然，我们眼睛可睁着呢，”雷吉说，这下算是正式开讲他的身世了，“早晚有一天，我们会逮着一个冤大头。”
“逮着一——？”姑娘困惑地问。
雷吉的脸涨得通红；但姑娘这一问显出她的斯文来，他又兴奋又感激。
“恕我口粗，弗莱明小姐——维拉；我是说我们会寻着一个买主。有一回我差点儿把那破玩意儿卖到 10,000 英镑，偏偏到了要签约的那天，人家把那可怜的家伙送进了疯人院——可怜的老兄，脑子不正常嘛。所以他才要买我们的水泥厂。逗不逗？

===Original===
“D’you know,” said Mr. Boltover, suddenly, “when I came round to the stage door that night I never expected to meet you?”
She looked at him in innocent surprise.
“Didn’t you really?” she said incredulously as though the idea had occurred to her for the first time, and then, thoughtfully, “I suppose you didn’t.”
“I didn’t expect to meet you,” repeated Mr. Boltover, who, when he had got hold of one complete sentence, held tight to it until his groping mentality had reached out and securely grasped another. “No, I didn’t expect to meet you, but I’m awfully glad. I feel I owe that young lady more than I can ever repay.”
He said this with an unusual display of sentimentality.

===Chinese===
“您可知道，”博尔托弗先生突然说，“那天晚上我绕到舞台后门去，压根儿没指望遇见您？”
她一脸无辜的惊讶望着他。
“当真没指望？”她说，满腹狐疑，仿佛这念头头一遭才进她脑子，随即又若有所思地说，“我想您是没指望。”
“我没指望遇见您。”博尔托弗先生重复道——他但凡抓着一句囫囵话，总要死死攥住，直到摸索的脑筋够着下一句才撒手。“是的，我没指望遇见您，可我高兴极了。我总觉得，那位小姐的情分，我这辈子也报答不完。”
他说这话时，少见地露出一腔柔情。

===Original===
“That young lady” was his companion’s chorus girl friend, who at that moment was retailing to her youthful companion at the far side of the room such details of Vera’s life as she had been able to secure in a seven-day acquaintance.
“Vera’s not in our show now, of course,” she said; “I don’t think she had ever been on the stage before. She’s an awfully fresh kid. Came late to rehearsals and all that sort of thing, but I like her immensely.”
She smiled and bowed to Vera who, at that moment, had caught her eye.
“She’s very pretty,” said her companion.
“Yes; isn’t she?” agreed the girl, her interest in her friend suddenly evaporating.

===Chinese===
“那位小姐”便是他同伴的歌舞女友；此刻她正在大厅那一头，向自己年轻的同伴转述维拉的种种底细——全凭她七天交情打听得来的。
“维拉如今当然不在我们戏里了，”她说，“我看她从前压根儿没登过台。她是个嫩得可以的小丫头，排练还迟到什么的，可我顶喜欢她了。”
她朝维拉含笑点头——维拉恰在此时也瞥见了她。
“她真漂亮。”她的同伴说。
“可不是嘛。”姑娘应道，对这位朋友的兴致顿时烟消云散。

===Original===
But there was one in that crowded dining-room whose every disengaged moment was employed in watching the girl and her companion. It involved his getting into the way of other waiters and called down upon his head execrations in Neapolitan, Sicilian and the choicest slang of the Montmartre. He was a man who had prayed for two years for such a moment as this, and his soul rejoiced in savage exaltation that so Heaven-sent an opportunity had come.
As the night wore on his plan took a definite shape. For the consequence he cared nothing. Here was his opportunity, here was his enemy. He seized a moment, slipped through the service door and passed down a flight of stone steps to the crowded kitchen filled at that moment with a babble of sound as the orders were repeated across the steaming brass pots and the blistering hot plates. He passed through the kitchen to the larder department, and found what he sought in the big cool vault where the butchers worked. It was a long thin knife. He waited until the butcher’s back was turned and slipped it up his sleeve, passed rapidly through the kitchen, ignoring the chef’s demand as to his business, and reached the warm, bright restaurant again.
He had no time to waste.
The butcher might at any moment detect the theft and the thief hauled into the service room to explain his conduct. He made his way across the room to where Mr. Reginald Boltover and his fair companion sat.

===Chinese===
然而在那间拥挤的餐厅里，有一个人但凡腾出一只手，眼睛便须臾不离那位姑娘和她的同伴。为此他挡了别的侍者的道，脑袋上落下不少咒骂——那不勒斯话、西西里话，外加蒙马特区最地道的行话黑语。这个人两年来日日祷告，求的便是这样一个时机；如今天赐良机真的来了，他的灵魂在野蛮的狂喜中欢呼。
夜渐深，他的计划有了眉目。后果如何，他毫不在意。机会就在眼前，仇人就在眼前。他瞅准一个空当，闪身进了上菜的门，沿一段石阶而下，来到拥挤的厨房——叫菜声隔着蒸腾的铜锅和滚烫的盘碟此呼彼应，闹成一片。他穿过厨房来到物料间，在屠夫们干活的阴凉大库房里找到了要找的东西。那是一把细长的刀。他等到屠夫转过身去，把刀顺进袖管，疾步穿过厨房——主厨喝问他的来路，他只作不闻——重新回到暖意融融、灯火通明的餐厅。
他一分钟也不能耽搁。
屠夫随时可能察觉失窃，窃贼随时可能被拖进配菜间去交代行止。他穿过大厅，直奔雷金纳德·博尔托弗先生和他那位白皙的同伴所坐的桌子。

===Original===
Reggie thought the man had a message, but Vera, looking up, saw the man’s evil face﻿—and knew. She half twisted, half flung herself against Reginald Boltover as the waiter’s hand came up to strike. She saw the knife glitter for a space of a second and closed her eyes, then there was the sound of a struggle and she opened them in time to see the vengeful man flung backward to the floor and an immaculate Michael Pretherston standing over him examining the knife with some interest.
She met the inspector’s eye and smiled, though the smile was forced, for even as he bowed, she heard the mockery of his surprise.
“Why, Kate!” he murmured. “I’m always meeting you.”

===Chinese===
雷吉还当这人是来传话的，维拉一抬眼，却看见那张凶恶的脸——她顿时明白了。侍者抬手欲击的一霎，她半扭半扑，一头撞进雷金纳德·博尔托弗怀里。她瞥见刀光一闪，闭上了眼；接着传来一阵扭打声，她再睁眼时，恰见那衔恨的家伙被人仰面掼倒在地，而衣冠楚楚的迈克尔·普雷瑟斯顿立在他身旁，正饶有兴味地察看那把刀。
她迎上探长的目光，嫣然一笑——那笑未免勉强，因为就在他躬身行礼的当口，她已听出他话里的惊讶带着讥诮。
“哎呀，凯特！”他低声道，“我老是遇见您。”

## XII A Motor Car Was Met by a Special Train / 十二 一辆汽车迎头遇上了专列

===Original===
Whatever distress animated the bosoms of humanity on that fateful Sunday afternoon and evening there were two people riotously and supremely happy, though the car which Alphonso Blaxton drove was an old one and badly sprung and though every hill it met reduced the two young adventurers to breathless apprehension for the car had a trick of stopping with its goal in sight and refusing to budge any farther.
They were happy though no word of love had been spoken between them from the moment she had drawn from his arms. And their happiness was such that even a faulty cylinder and a choked carburettor were matters of little moment.
They had eaten a very bad luncheon in Maidstone without noticing the fact. They had encountered perils innumerable (the steering gear had gone wrong and temporary repairs had to be effected without the aid of a tool chest) and were yet cheerful. They had been bumped and shaken and jarred but they had had compensation. They had seen the uprising ridges of the Kentish Rag green and white and starred with flowers. They had looked through a golden haze across mysterious valleys. They had heard the songs of birds and had tasted the joys which come only to those who love youth and young things.
If the clouds were banking up in the west and an occasional puff of cold wind came to remind them of May’s treachery they, for their part, saw no cloud in their sky, felt no chill winds in their rosy world.

===Chinese===
在那个决定命运的星期日下午和傍晚，无论世人心头萦绕着怎样的愁苦，总有两个家伙快活得放肆、快活到了极点——虽然阿方索·布拉克斯顿开的那辆车又旧又颠，虽然每遇一道坡，这一对年轻的冒险家就紧张得大气不敢出，因为那车有个怪癖：眼看坡顶在望，它偏要停住，再不肯往前挪半步。
自从她从他怀里挣脱的那一刻起，两人之间再没有吐露过一个爱字，可他们照样快活。他们的快活是那样圆满，就连汽缸出了毛病、汽化器堵了，都成了无关紧要的小事。
他们在梅德斯通吃了一顿很糟的午饭，自己却毫无察觉。他们经历了数不清的险情（转向机构失灵，只好在没有工具箱的情况下临时修理），却依旧兴致勃勃。他们被颠过、摇过、震过，可他们得到了补偿。他们看见了肯特拉格岩渐渐隆起的山脊，青白相间，缀满星点野花；他们透过金色的雾霭眺望神秘的峡谷；他们听见了鸟儿的歌唱，尝到了只有热爱青春、热爱青春万物的人才能尝到的欢乐。
即使乌云正在西边堆积，不时有一阵冷风来提醒他们五月的天说变就变，他们自己却望不见天空有一丝云影，在他们的玫瑰色世界里感不到半点寒风。

===Original===
They reached the top of a particularly trying hill and Alphonso stopped the car and got down. Before them the road dipped straightly down to a level crossing. A mile beyond the railway there was a little hill which promised no distress of mind.
“Wouldn’t this be a lovely place to paint!” said the girl.
“Don’t let’s talk about art,” he begged with a wry face, “let us talk of beautiful things﻿—such as tea and shrimps.”
She shrieked with merriment at his feeble jest.

===Chinese===
他们爬上一道格外折磨人的坡顶，阿方索停了车，走下来。前面道路笔直下探，直通一处铁路平交道口。铁路那一头一英里外有座小山，看着不叫人发愁。
“这地方画起画来多美呀！”姑娘说。
“咱们别谈艺术了，”他苦着脸央求道，“谈谈美好的东西吧——比如茶和虾仁。”
他这句干瘪的俏皮话逗得她咯咯直叫。

===Original===
“I wonder what is going to happen,” said the girl becoming grave.
“Happen, how, where?” he asked in surprise.
“About us,” she said.
He took her two hands in his.

===Chinese===
“不知往后会怎么样。”姑娘说，神色郑重起来。
“怎么样，怎么说，在哪儿？”他吃惊地问。
“说咱们俩呀。”她说。
他把她的双手握在自己手里。

===Original===
“I am going to be tremendously rich.”
“Did I tell you I was engaged?” she asked timidly after a long silence.
It was nothing less than an act of heroism for her to ask this question.
“I have a dim idea you said something about it a long time ago,” he said.

===Chinese===
“我将来会阔得不得了。”
“我跟你说过我已经订婚了吗？”沉默了好半天，她怯生生地问。
她问出这一句，简直称得上英雄壮举。
“我恍惚记得，很久以前你说起过一句半句的。”他说。

===Original===
“Did I really?” she asked relieved. “I had a feeling﻿—”
“If you didn’t tell me I saw your ring,” he said and she went red because she had removed that ring after their second meeting and had never worn it again.
“I think I have told you that I had £300 a year,” he went on; “now that we are confessing our handicaps I might as well own up to mine.”
“You told me you were absolutely penniless,” she said severely. “£300 a year is a fortune.”

===Chinese===
“我真的说过？”她松了口气问，“我总觉得——”
“你没告诉过我，可我看见你的戒指了。”他说。她脸红了——因为第二次见面之后，她就摘下了那枚戒指，从此再没戴过。
“我想我跟你说过，我一年有 300 镑进账。”他接着说，“既然咱们都在坦白各自的家底，我不妨也交个底。”
“你跟我说过你身无分文。”她板起脸说，“一年 300 镑就是一笔家财了。”

===Original===
“£300 a year is only a fortune to the immensely rich, to the poor it is worse than poverty.”
“You can do a lot with £300 a year,” she said thoughtfully, “and what shall I do with my money? I can’t throw it away.”
“You will do nothing with it,” he said firmly; “when my £300 a year has become £10,000 a year we can do things.”
She laughed happily, twisting his watch guard round her finger.

===Chinese===
“一年 300 镑，只在阔人眼里算家财；在穷人，它比赤贫还糟。”
“一年 300 镑能办不少事呢。”她若有所思地说，“那我的钱怎么办？总不能扔了呀。”
“你的钱一个子儿也不动。”他斩钉截铁地说，“等我的一年 300 镑变成一年 10,000 镑，咱们想干什么都成。”
她快活地笑着，把他的表链在手指上绕来绕去。

===Original===
“I cannot understand myself,” she said. “I have been such a selfish mercenary pig. I didn’t know there was any happiness in the world.”
For the second time that day he slipped his arm around her, raised her face to his and kissed her.
“Tea,” he said practically, started the engine and climbed into the driver’s seat, stretching out his hand to assist her to his side.
The car started with a jerk but ran smoothly down the hill.

===Chinese===
“我简直不认识自己了。”她说，“我从前是头又自私又贪财的猪。我压根儿不知道世上还有快乐这回事。”
那天他第二次搂住她，捧起她的脸，吻了她。
“吃茶去。”他讲究实际地说，发动引擎，爬进驾驶座，伸手把她拉到自己身边。
汽车一颠启动了，顺顺当当地滑下山坡。

===Original===
“It is rather lucky that gate is open,” he said as the machine gathered speed. “It would be rather comic if we couldn’t stop the car.”
A piercing shriek of an engine brought his head round.
“That must be another line,” he said uneasily and put his hand on the brake; “anyway, the gate is open,” he said relieved.
Again came the frenzied scream of the engine and he heard the thunder of its wheels. He was fifty yards from the crossing when he saw the gates begin to move. He pressed on the foot brake without producing any diminution of speed, gripped the hand brake, pulled it back until he felt the snap of the rotten handle as it broke. There was nothing for it but to take a risk. He pushed over the accelerator and the car leaped forward.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
“幸亏道口的栏木开着。”车子加速时他说，“要是车停不下来，那可就有喜剧瞧了。”
一声尖利的机车汽笛让他回过头来。
“那准是另一条线上的。”他不安地说，手按上刹车，“反正栏木开着呢。”他又放心了。
汽笛又疯狂地嘶叫起来，他还听见了车轮的轰雷。离道口还有五十码，他看见栏木开始挪动。他踩下脚刹，车速分毫未减；他抓住手闸猛力后拉，只听咔嚓一声，朽烂的闸柄断了。如今只剩冒险一搏。他把油门一踩到底，汽车向前蹿去……

===Original===
Car and gate and train seemed to reach the spot simultaneously.
The girl found herself flung headlong into a ditch, fortunately landing in the soft mud at the bottom. Alphonso’s fall was broken by the quick-set hedge which ripped his clothes to ribbons and scarred his face and hands. He picked himself up and went in search of the girl and found her as she was climbing unsteadily on to the permanent way.
The train had pulled up with a jerk amidst a chaos of smashed gate and mangled motorcar. Fortunately, it was slowing at the closed gate at the time the collision occurred, otherwise these two young people presenting a fantastic appearance might have ended their promising careers.
“Are you hurt?” were the first words she asked.

===Chinese===
汽车、栏木和火车，仿佛同时抢到了那一点上。
姑娘发现自己被甩进了沟里，所幸落在沟底软泥上。阿方索被一排绿篱挡住了下坠，篱笆撕碎了他的衣裳，划破了他的脸和手。他爬起来去找姑娘，正遇上她摇摇晃晃地往路基上爬。
火车猛地刹住，停在栏木粉碎、汽车稀烂的一片狼藉之中。幸而相撞时它正在闭合的栏木前减速，否则这两位模样狼狈的年轻人，前程似锦的性命怕是要当场了结。
“你伤着没有？”这是她的第一句话。

===Original===
His face was scratched and his clothes were torn but though he had by far the worse experience his was not the woebegone appearance which the girl presented. She was caked with mud, a dab of mud was on her cheek, her hat was gone and her long brown hair was flying in all directions.
The passengers of the “special” were perhaps more perturbed than its victims.
“It is an accident. We have run into a motorcar,” reported the conductor.
“Is anybody killed?” asked Sir Ralph in alarm.

===Chinese===
他脸上有抓痕，衣服也撕破了，可虽说他的遭遇糟糕得多，他那副狼狈还算比不上姑娘。她浑身糊满泥浆，脸颊上贴着一块泥巴，帽子飞了，一头长长的棕发四下乱飞。
“专列”上的乘客，只怕比闯祸的两位还要惊慌。
“出事了。我们撞上了一辆汽车。”列车员报告道。
“压死人没有？”拉尔夫爵士惊慌地问。

===Original===
“No, sir, a young man and a young woman who are more frightened than hurt.”
“Let us go and look at them,” said Lord Flanborough and stepped down to the permanent way.
It is a truism that there is no such thing as a paternal instinct and he would have indeed been a wise father who recognized his child in such disarray.
He was speechless for a moment.

===Chinese===
“没有，爵士。一男一女两个年轻人，吓得不轻，伤倒没伤着。”
“咱们去瞧瞧。”弗兰伯勒勋爵说着，跨下路基。
谁都说父爱并无本能可言，这已是老生常谈——能把这副狼狈相里的孩子认出来的，才真称得上明智的父亲呢。
他一时说不出话来。

===Original===
“Moya,” he gasped hollowly. “Moya! Great Heavens! What were you doing here?”
He stared round at the scarecrow by her side and at sight of the young man, Sir Ralph, who had been struck dumb by the apparition, found his voice.
“I see, I see,” he said bitterly.
“You have the advantage of me,” said the young man, “for I have got a little piece of Hampshire in my eye.”

===Chinese===
“莫亚，”他嗓音发空地喘道，“莫亚！我的老天！你在这儿干什么？”
他瞪眼看着她身边那个稻草人似的家伙；等看清那青年，被这幽灵吓哑了的拉尔夫爵士才找回自己的声音。
“我明白了，我明白了。”他咬牙切齿地说。
“您比我强，”青年说，“我眼睛里还进了颗汉普郡的沙子呢。”

===Original===
The girl swung round to him fumbling for her handkerchief.
“It is nothing, dear,” said the young man, blissfully unconscious of the identity of the well-fed gentleman who was regarding him so sternly.
“But, darling, you might be blinded,” pleaded the girl; “please let me.”
“Moya,” said Lord Flanborough in a pained tone, “may I ask what is the meaning of this?”

===Chinese===
姑娘霍地转向他，摸出手绢来。
“没事，亲爱的。”青年说，全然不知这位养尊处优、正狠狠打量自己的老爷是何许人也。
“可是，亲爱的，会瞎的呀。”姑娘央求道，“让我看看嘛。”
“莫亚，”弗兰伯勒勋爵痛心地说，“请问这是怎么一回事？”

===Original===
“Oh, I want you to meet Mr. Blaxton,” said the girl going red and white. “Fonso, this is papa.”
“I should be glad to see you,” said Fonso, groping wildly on the blind side of him.
“ ‘Fonso’?” repeated the enraged Flanborough, “and who, may I ask, is Fonso?”
She fastened back her unruly hair and rubbed her mud-stained cheek with her handkerchief before she replied.

===Chinese===
“哦，我正要给你介绍布拉克斯顿先生呢。”姑娘红一阵白一阵地说，“方索，这是我爸爸。”
“幸会，幸会。”方索说着，朝看不见的那一侧胡乱摸索。
“‘方索’？”弗兰伯勒勃然大怒，“请问这个‘方索’又是哪一位？”
她先把散乱的头发别好，用手绢擦了擦沾泥的脸颊，才开口回答。

===Original===
“I suppose it will come as a shock to you and a greater shock to Sir Ralph, but Fonso and I are going to be married,” she said.
Alphonso Blaxton blinked at her.
“I haven’t asked you yet,” he said.
“That doesn’t matter,” she replied calmly, “you do want me, don’t you?” And before her horrified father and her promised husband, Alphonso took her in his arms and hugged her.

===Chinese===
“我想这话对你是当头一棒，对拉尔夫爵士更是晴天霹雳——我和方索要结婚了。”她说。
阿方索·布拉克斯顿朝她直眨眼。
“我还没向你求婚呢。”他说。
“那不要紧。”她平静地答道，“你反正要我，对不对？”于是，在她吓坏了的父亲和她的未婚夫面前，阿方索把她搂进怀里，紧紧抱住。

===Original===
It was an awkward journey back to town. Sir Ralph sat by himself and rejected all Lord Flanborough’s attempts to discuss the matter. He was hurt in his pride and, if the truth be told, hurt in his pocket because an alliance with the family meant a considerable addition to his fortune.
It is a mistake to believe that rich people do not care for money or that a man with two millions is wholly indifferent as to whether he has two or three. Indeed, the reverse is the case. The man who thinks in thousands is indifferent to a figure or two, the man who counts his fortune in shillings seldom knows the number of shillings he has. Only your two-millionaire realizes the full value of money. The thrift of the millionaire might well serve as an example to the improvident poor.
“I shall speak to Moya when we get home,” said Lord Flanborough. “I have never been so distressed at anything so much in my life. It is disgraceful, Ralph.”
But Ralph did not encourage sympathy.

===Chinese===
回城的一路尴尬透了。拉尔夫爵士独自坐着，弗兰伯勒勋爵几次想谈这件事，都被他顶了回去。他的自尊受了伤；说老实话，他的钱袋也受了伤——同那家人结亲，原意味着他的财产要大大添上一笔。
以为有钱人不在乎钱，以为一个身家两百万的人全不在意自己有两百万还是三百万，那是想错了。事实恰恰相反。以千为单位思想的人，对零头数字毫不在意；以先令数家财的人，很少数得清自己有几个先令。唯有坐拥两百万的阔佬，才真正懂得钱的全部价值。百万富翁的节俭，大可给挥霍的穷人做个榜样。
“等回了家，我要跟莫亚谈谈。”弗兰伯勒勋爵说，“我一辈子没为什么事这么痛心过。真是丢人，拉尔夫。”
可拉尔夫不受这份同情。

===Original===
As a matter of fact, his lordship spoke to the girl before the special ran into London Street Station. It required some courage on his part, for it meant intruding upon the couple in the little stateroom which ordinarily served as a sleeping apartment when Sir Ralph’s private coach carried him on night journeys.
He found them a picture of decorum sitting rigidly bolt upright, one on either side of the carriage, looking out of the window with fine unconcern; but this attitude was probably due to the fact that the door of the compartment made a very loud rattling noise when the handle was turned.
“I want to speak to you alone, Moya.”
“Run away, Fonso,” said the girl with a gaiety out of harmony with her rigidity of attitude.

===Chinese===
其实，专列开进伦敦街车站之前，勋爵大人就跟姑娘谈过了。这颇需要些勇气，因为那意味着要闯进那间小包房——拉尔夫爵士的私人车厢夜里行驶时，那包房向来权作卧铺。
他看见两人端坐如仪，一人一边，腰板笔直，神情坦然地望着窗外，俨然一幅端庄图——不过这副姿态多半另有缘故：包房的门一拧把手就咯咯作响，响得厉害。
“我要单独跟你说句话，莫亚。”
“你先出去，方索。”姑娘说，语声轻快，跟僵直的坐姿全不相称。

===Original===
Alphonso stepped out of the saloon and closed the sliding door behind him.
“Now, Moya,” said his lordship with a badly simulated air of friendliness, “perhaps you will explain?”
“Why I am going to marry Fonso?” she asked, “because I love him. Why do you think that I should be marrying him?”
“This sounds very much like Michael. It is the way he would talk,” said Lord Flanborough bitterly. “This shows the danger of letting your children associate with irregular people. You know very well that you are engaged to Sir Ralph.”

===Chinese===
阿方索走出客厅车厢，随手拉上了滑动门。
“好了，莫亚，”勋爵大人装出一副蹩脚的和颜悦色，“你总可以解释解释了吧？”
“问我为什么嫁方索？”她反问，“因为我爱他。你倒以为我为什么嫁他？”
“这话真像迈克尔的口气，他就会这么说话。”弗兰伯勒勋爵恨恨地说，“可见让孩子同不三不四的人来往有多危险。你心里清楚得很，你已经同拉尔夫爵士订了婚。”

===Original===
“I know he gave me a ring and we agreed to get married,” she said, “but I have changed my mind.”
“But you *can’t* change your mind,” stormed her father; “it is impossible that my daughter should marry a wretched artist.”
“He’s not wretched and he is not an artist,” said the girl; “we have both agreed that he is not an artist and he is going to find something useful to do.”
“If you marry this man,” he pointed a trembling finger at her, “I will not receive you as my daughter.”

===Chinese===
“我知道他给过我戒指，我们也讲好了要结婚。”她说，“可我改了主意。”
“可你不能改主意。”父亲大发雷霆，“我的女儿要嫁一个穷酸画家，断无此理。”
“他不穷酸，也不是画家。”姑娘说，“我们俩都一致认为他不算画家，他打算找点正经有用的事做。”
“你要是嫁给这个人，”他颤着指头指着她，“我就不认你这个女儿。”

===Original===
“I don’t want to be received at all. You married whom you wanted to marry, didn’t you?”
“I married,” said Lord Flanborough virtuously, “in accordance with the wishes of my parents.”
“Do you mean to say,” said the girl incredulously, “that you had no voice in it? I cannot imagine it. My dear daddy, it is preposterous to suggest that a person of your strong character accepted the wife that somebody else found for him!”
“Well, I admit,” said her father somewhat mollified, “that I had a say in the matter but I had the sense to choose the right person.”

===Chinese===
“我压根儿不想让您认。您当年不也娶了自个儿想娶的人吗？”
“我成婚，”弗兰伯勒勋爵一脸正派地说，“是遵从父母之命。”
“你是说，”姑娘将信将疑地说，“这事你半个字都没得说？我简直没法想象。亲爱的爸爸，要说您这样性格刚强的人，娶的是人家替您物色的太太，这话也太荒唐啦！”
“呃，我承认，”父亲的火气消了些，“这件事我确实说得上话，可我有那个见识，挑中了合适的人。”

===Original===
“That is just what I am doing,” she cried in triumph, “choosing the right person! And, Daddy, if you are rude to Fonso, I shall be very rude to Ralph.”
“The man of course is a fortune hunter,” said Lord Flanborough savagely. “He knows that you have money in your own right and that I cannot save you from the consequences of your folly.”
“What is Ralph?” she asked tartly.
“Sir Ralph is a very rich man,” said her father with emphasis.

===Chinese===
“我现在干的正是这个，”她得意洋洋地喊道，“挑个合适的人！还有，爸爸，您要是对方索无礼，我就对拉尔夫非常无礼。”
“这家伙分明是图财来的。”弗兰伯勒勋爵恶狠狠地说，“他知道你自己名下有钱，知道我拦不住你自食蠢行的苦果。”
“拉尔夫又算什么？”她尖刻地问。
“拉尔夫爵士是个非常有钱的人。”父亲加重语气说。

===Original===
“What does he get with me?” she asked again.
This was the question which Lord Flanborough did not find it convenient to answer. He knew that marriage with his daughter would bring to Sir Ralph a much greater fortune than she possessed in her own right.
“Go and ask your disinterested friend if he will take me without a *dot*, and if I were to give my own income to found a hospital for women.”
“I am sure Sir Ralph would answer in the affirmative,” replied Lord Flanborough.

===Chinese===
“他娶了我，图到什么？”她又问。
这一问，弗兰伯勒勋爵觉得不便回答。他知道，娶了他的女儿，拉尔夫爵士到手的财产，远比她名下所有的多得多。
“去问问你那位无私的朋友，我要是不带一分嫁妆，还要把我自己的收入捐出去办一所妇女医院，他还肯不肯要我。”
“我敢说拉尔夫爵士会答应的。”弗兰伯勒勋爵答道。

===Original===
“Ask him,” she challenged.
He passed out of the compartment scowling at the offending Fonso and made his way to Sir Ralph. He had not intended putting the question, but some chance remark of the baronet’s just before the train reached London gave him an opportunity of introducing the subject.
“Would you care to marry Moya without the settlement we agreed, Ralph?”
“What on earth do you mean?” asked Sir Ralph, astonished out of his sulks. Money was a subject which invariably aroused him from the deepest lethargy.

===Chinese===
“那你去问。”她激他。
他走出包房，冲着惹祸的方索瞪了一眼，便朝拉尔夫爵士那边去。他本没打算问这个话，可火车到伦敦之前，从男爵随口说的一句话，正好给了他引出话头的机会。
“拉尔夫，要是没有咱们讲定的那笔财产陪嫁，你还愿意娶莫亚吗？”
“你这话究竟什么意思？”拉尔夫爵士问道，惊讶得从赌气中醒了过来。钱这个题目，向来能把他从最深的懒怠里唤醒。

===Original===
“I mean,” said his future father-in-law, “suppose I say ‘You love Moya and all that sort of thing. You are a very rich man, you can afford to keep her, take her without a settlement,’ what would you answer?”
“Certainly not!” said Sir Ralph furiously, “certainly not! I don’t understand this business at all, Flanborough, I really don’t understand it. We made an arrangement and now, it seems, you want to back out of it. What is the objection to the settlement?”
“I have no objection at all,” admitted Lord Flanborough uncomfortably, “but Moya thinks that money is a big factor in your choice of her.”
“Of course it is,” said Sir Ralph with brutal directness. “I was very fond of Moya, but the settlement was a big consideration.”

===Chinese===
“我是说，”他这位未来的岳丈说，“假如我说：‘你爱莫亚，等等等等。你是个大富翁，养得起她，娶她不必陪嫁’——你怎么回答？”
“当然不行！”拉尔夫爵士勃然大怒，“当然不行！这件事我全然不懂，弗兰伯勒，我实在不懂。咱们讲好的安排，如今你倒想反悔。那笔陪嫁有什么不妥？”
“我毫无异议。”弗兰伯勒勋爵承认道，颇为狼狈，“只是莫亚认为，你挑中她，钱是个大头。”
“本来就是。”拉尔夫爵士说得直白得不留情面，“我很喜欢莫亚，可陪嫁也是件大事。”

===Original===
“I see,” said Lord Flanborough incoherently, “Moya’s idea of course.﻿ ﻿…”
Michael met them at the station and noticed the constraint of the party. He understood the reason when a bedraggled Moya and a young man, whose face was crisscrossed with scratches and whose clothes were in threads, made their appearance. There was no explanation possible and Michael wisely asked for none. He handed over Lord Flanborough and his friend to the care of the city detective officer in charge of the case and when they had gone he turned to Moya.
“Have you two people been fighting?” he asked.
“Father’s horribly angry with me,” she said, “because I am going to marry Fonso.”

===Chinese===
“我明白了。”弗兰伯勒勋爵语无伦次地说，“自然是莫亚的主意……”
迈克尔到车站接他们，一眼看出这行人个个局促。等到衣衫不整的莫亚和一位满脸交叉抓痕、衣裳成条的青年露了面，他也就明白了缘由。这事无从解释，迈克尔识趣地一字不问。他把弗兰伯勒勋爵和他的朋友交给负责此案的城市侦探，等他们走后，才转向莫亚。
“你们俩打架了？”他问。
“爸爸气得不得了。”她说，“因为我要嫁方索。”

===Original===
He stared at her in amazement.
“Do you mean to tell me that you are not going to marry Ralph?”
“I am not,” she said resolutely.
“And this is Fonso?”

===Chinese===
他愕然瞪着她。
“你是说，你不打算嫁给拉尔夫了？”
“不打算。”她说得斩钉截铁。
“这位就是方索？”

===Original===
The girl nodded.
Michael threw back his head and filled the station with laughter.
“You don’t know Fonso, do you?” she said. “He’s horribly poor. Aren’t you, dear?”
“Horribly,” admitted the young man but did not seem unhappy.

===Chinese===
姑娘点点头。
迈克尔仰起头，笑声灌满了整个车站。
“你不了解方索，对吧？”她说，“他穷得吓人。是不是，亲爱的？”
“穷得吓人。”青年承认，却似乎并不苦恼。

===Original===
“And you are going to marry him?” said Michael.
“Of course I am going to marry him,” said the girl wrathfully. “I didn’t expect that you would disapprove.”
“Disapprove?” he chuckled and catching her up in his strong arms he kissed her.
“We will all go along and have some grub,” he said; “dash home and make yourself respectable, Moya. I see your father has left his car for you. Meet me at Sebo’s in an hour’s time.”

===Chinese===
“你要嫁的就是他？”迈克尔说。
“我当然要嫁他。”姑娘气呼呼地说，“没想到你会反对。”
“反对？”他轻声一笑，用有力的双臂把她一把抱起，吻了她。
“咱们这就都去吃点儿东西。”他说，“飞奔回家收拾齐整，莫亚。我看你父亲把车留给你了。一个钟头后塞博见。”

## VIII An Artist Makes an Exhibition of Himself / 八 画家当众出了洋相

===Original===
No man has ever understood a woman, for the simple reason that woman is unintelligible even to her own kind. If she were not, and if she were susceptible to explanation by her own sisters, be sure that her own sisters would lose no time in telling the first man she met all about her.
Lady Moya Felton possessed that rare combination of talents, beauty and acumen. She dressed well, she spoke well, and she looked well. She was a product of Newnham, an institution which, more often than not, gives the world a being which is something less than a woman and something more than a babu. This being is crammed with erudition and for many years fights life with a textbook. Sometimes she continues to the end, very self-assured, very confident of the facts she has culled from the printed page and very determined that she will never surrender her mechanical facts or her machine-made values. Sometimes, she succumbs to the humanising influences which daily contact with the verities of life bring to her and develops into a useful and charming member of society.
Moya had absorbed just as much of life as she thought was necessary to her comfort. She stopped short of the supreme lesson which finds expression in cheerful sacrifice but she was an eminently pleasing person and never discussed biological justice or gave forth as her own the shoddy philosophies she had acquired in hall. Therefore, she was bearable. Moreover, by realising﻿—here her instinct served her﻿—that Newnham had turned her out fit for nothing better than a churchgoing school ma’am, she conveyed an impression of her education rather than declaimed the fact.
Practical as she was, she had a guilty secret, not only a very dear one, to be hugged tight to her heart, but one which evoked the unusual emotion of profound disapproval in the more ordered compartments of her mind. Moya was a dreamer, a cold-blooded romanticist who had wonderful adventures with wonderful people whenever she walked or rode abroad. In the privacy of her big limousine, she would be absorbed in events of her own creation, wholly monopolised by men and women who bore no likeness to and had no relation with any person in her somewhat extensive list of acquaintances. She would often find herself in situations so absurdly impossible that even the penny novelette reader would have rejected them with the scorn which their crudity deserved. She did not dream of living people, the mere mental suggestion﻿—for the roving mind has a trick of taking charge at times﻿—that any of her visionary heroes had his prototype in flesh and blood ensured the ejection of the offending dream-man and the substitution of another, more wildly improbable but at the same time more unlikely to challenge relationship with anybody in the material world.

===Chinese===
从来没有一个男人懂得女人，道理很简单：女人这东西，连她的同性都看不懂。倘若不然，倘若她能被自己的姐妹解释明白，那你放心，她的姐妹们准会抢着把她的一切，一五一十地讲给她碰见的头一个男人听。
莫亚·费尔顿小姐得天独厚，才貌与精明兼而有之。她衣着入时，谈吐得体，仪容出众。她是纽纳姆学院的出品——这所学府十有八九向社会输送这样一种生物：比女人少了点女人味，比巴布多了点书卷气。这种生物满腹经纶，多年来靠一本教科书同生活搏斗。有的能一路撑到底，非常自信，笃信自己从印刷品上采撷来的种种事实，打定主意绝不放弃她那些机械的事实和机器造出来的价值观。有的则被日常接触生活真相带来的人性化影响所感化，长成社会里一个有用而可爱的一员。
莫亚吸收的生活知识，恰够她过舒坦日子。她没学到那门以愉快的牺牲为表现的最高课程，但她是个极讨人喜欢的人，从不空谈生物学意义上的公道，也从不把讲堂里贩来的蹩脚哲学冒充己见四处散播。因此，她还叫人受得了。再者——这一点上她的本能帮了她——她意识到纽纳姆把自己造就得只配做个按时做礼拜的女学究，于是她让自己的教养从言谈举止里自然透出，而不是挂在嘴上宣讲。
她固然务实，却藏着一桩心虚的秘密——这秘密不仅珍贵，要紧紧搂在心口，而且在她头脑里那些井井有条的格子间里，激起一种罕见的心绪：深切的自我不以为然。莫亚是个梦想家，一个冷血的浪漫派；每逢出门散步或乘车兜风，她总要同些了不起的人物经历些了不起的奇遇。躲在她那辆大轿车的私密天地里，她会沉浸在自己编造的种种情事之中，被一群男女完全霸占——这些人与她那份颇不算小的熟人名单上的任何人都毫无相似、毫无瓜葛。她常常陷入荒唐得无以复加的境地，连读一便士廉价小说的读者都会嗤之以鼻——那份粗劣本该落得这个下场。她从不梦见活人——因为那颗东游西荡的脑子有时会擅自做主——只要心里稍稍起个念头，觉得哪位虚构的英雄在血肉之躯里有原型，那位犯事的梦中人立刻就被逐出去，换上另一个：更加异想天开，同时也更不可能同物质世界里的什么人攀上关系。

===Original===
She could dream and yet accept the cold practicality of a Ralph Sapson and calmly consider a marriage so hopelessly prosaic.
That was inexplicable.
For an engaged lover Ralph had been singularly remiss. He had called once since his unemotional declaration of love. To do him justice he had skipped the tender demonstrations which usually accompany even the most formal engagements and had got down to the question of settlement in the shortest space of time. This was as Moya could wish, for she also was embarrassed at the thought that a human being might possibly approach﻿—suffering in comparison﻿—the extravagance, wordless and intangible as it was, of her shadowy friends.
It is a remarkable circumstance that romance in concrete form did not come to Moya, until the very week she engaged herself to marry Sir Ralph Sapson. It came in a curious way. She had driven to Leicester Square to see an exhibition of pictures. It was one of those collections which dawn upon London, bringing in its wake a name which has never been heard before, save in a very select circle and is never heard again outside of that circle; an orbit which swings beyond the ken of ordinary mortals.

===Chinese===
她能做梦，却也能接受拉尔夫·萨普森那种冷冰冰的实际，还能心平气和地考虑一桩无聊透顶的婚事。
这就没法解释了。
作为一个订了婚的情人，拉尔夫疏慢得出奇。自他那回不动声色的求婚宣言之后，他只来过一次。说句公道话，他省去了即便最讲形式的订婚也少不了的温存表示，以最短的时间直奔陪嫁问题。这倒正合莫亚的心意，因为她一想到一个血肉之躯居然可能接近——相形见绌地接近——她那些影子朋友虽无言语、无从捉摸却奢华无比的境界，也要发窘。
说来也怪，具体有形的罗曼史偏偏不来找莫亚，直到她订婚要嫁拉尔夫·萨普森爵士的那个星期，它才姗姗来迟。来路也古怪。她开车去莱斯特广场看一个画展。那类画展时不时降临伦敦，捎来一个前所未闻的名字——除了极小的圈子以外从未听过，此后在那个圈子之外也永远不会再听到；一条运行在凡人视野之外的轨道。

===Original===
She went into the gallery and found it a veritable desert. Save for a young man and a small, pinched and preoccupied girl, wearing a large pendant in which was inserted the photograph of her uninteresting fiancé, the place was empty. The girl with the pendant carried her excuse in her hand, in the shape of a bunch of catalogues. There was less excuse for the young man for he was healthy in appearance and it was not raining.
Moya began a conscientious inspection of the pictures, chiefly remarkable for their colouring and for the atmosphere which the artist had managed to secure. Indeed, the pictures were all atmosphere. The girl made a slow progress along the wall, comparing each framed atrocity with her catalogue and striving to sense, dimly, something of the artist’s honourable intentions.
She looked around once to discover what effect the pictures had upon her fellow sightseer. He was standing before a long panel representing, if the catalogue had been rightly compiled, *A Blue Wind on a Green Hill*. His face bore an expression of the deepest gloom, his hat was tilted to the back of his head and his hands were thrust deeply into his trousers pockets. The longer he looked at the *Blue Wind on the Green Hill* the more morose and unhappy did he appear.
This then was the attitude which the new colourist school demanded, one of fierce but approving antagonism if the paradox be permitted.

===Chinese===
她走进画廊，发现那儿真是一片荒漠。除了一个青年男子和一个瘦小、面容干瘪、心事重重的姑娘——姑娘胸前挂着个大吊坠，里面嵌着她那乏味的未婚夫的照片——整个展厅空空荡荡。挂吊坠的姑娘手里拿着自己的由头：一叠展览目录。青年男子的由头就少了，因为他看起来身体健康，天又没下雨。
莫亚开始一丝不苟地检阅那些画。画的可取之处主要在色彩，以及画家营造出来的那种气氛。说实在的，那些画全是气氛。姑娘沿着墙慢慢挪，把每一幅装框的骇人之作同目录对照，努力去隐约体会画家立意之正大。
她回头望了一眼，想看看这些画对那位同来观画的人有什么效果。他正站在一幅横长的画板前——倘若目录编得不错，那画题为《绿山上的蓝风》。他一脸深重的愁苦，帽子推到后脑勺，双手深深插在裤袋里。他看《绿山上的蓝风》看得越久，脸色就越阴沉、越不开心。
原来这便是新色彩派要求的态度：一种激烈却赞许的对立——如果允许这种矛盾修辞的话。

===Original===
She moved up till she was almost by his side, never thinking that in the presence of the girl with the programmes and the photographic miniature, he would dare address her. Yet he did.
“What do you think of that one?” he asked without turning his head.
She was taken aback and was prepared to be chilly and noncommittal. She looked at his face and the nearer view was a pleasing one. He was very fair, very good-looking and had the bluest eyes she had ever seen in a man. He was also unshaven and his collar was not clean, but he was well dressed enough and his tone was wholly Oxford﻿—and Balliol at that.
“I think it is rather weird,” she said.

===Chinese===
她往前挪，挪到他几乎身旁，绝没想到当着拿目录的姑娘和照片缩微吊坠的面，他竟敢同她搭话。可他偏偏搭了。
“您觉得那幅怎么样？”他问，头也不回。
她吃了一惊，摆好了冷淡含糊的架势。她看了看他的脸——凑近看还挺讨人喜欢。他一头浅色头发，相貌很好看，一双蓝眼睛是她见过的男人里最蓝的。他还没刮脸，领子也不干净，可衣着够体面，口音则是十足的牛津腔——还是巴利奥尔的牛津腔。
“我觉得相当离奇。”她说。

===Original===
“So do I,” he nodded vigorously. “I think it is﻿—‘weird’ is the word. As a work of art how does it strike you?”
She hesitated. She had a full range of studio jargon which she had acquired in the course of her after-education and could speak glibly on atmosphere, tone and light. She knew that it was possible to refer to a still-life study of a bunch of bananas as being “full of movement” without being guilty of an absurdity. In fact, she knew enough about art to have occupied a position on any average newspaper as a critic.
“As a work of art,” she said, “it is original and a little eccentric.”
“Frankly?” he demanded fiercely.

===Chinese===
“我也觉得。”他用力点头，“我看它——‘离奇’这词儿正合适。作为艺术品，您对它印象如何？”
她犹豫了一下。她拥有一整套画室行话——那是她后教育阶段学来的——谈起气氛、调子、光线都能出口成章。她知道，把一幅香蕉静物说成“充满动感”，也不算荒唐。事实上，论艺术她懂得够多，去任何一家平庸报纸当评论员都绰绰有余。
“作为艺术品，”她说，“它有独创性，也有点儿怪。”
“说老实话呢？”他气势汹汹地问。

===Original===
All the time he spoke he was glaring at the picture and had not turned his head toward her.
“Frankly,” she replied, “I think these are monstrosities.”
He nodded again.
“I agree with you,” he said, “and I know better than anybody else how monstrous they are﻿—I painted ’em!”

===Chinese===
他说话时一直瞪着那幅画，头也没朝她转过来。
“老实说，”她答道，“我认为这些是怪胎。”
他又点点头。
“我同意您的看法。”他说，“这些画有多怪，天下没人比我更清楚——是我画的！”

===Original===
Moya gasped.
“I am awfully sorry,” she began.
“I am sorry, too﻿—that I painted them,” he replied. “I am not sorry that I exhibited them, because all my friends told me that they were wonderful and naturally I get some satisfaction from proving that my friends are mentally deficient.”
He turned round and looked at her and was in turn surprised.

===Chinese===
莫亚倒吸一口气。
“真是抱歉得很。”她开口道。
“我也抱歉——抱歉画了它们。”他答道，“展出它们我倒不悔，因为我所有的朋友都说它们妙极了，能证明我的朋友们脑子缺根弦，我自然得到几分快慰。”
他转过身来看她，这一看，他倒吃了一惊。

===Original===
“Hello,” he said, staring at her with his blue eyes wide open, “I thought you were much older.”
She laughed.
“The fact is I didn’t look at you,” he confessed; “how can anybody look at anything with these beastly things staring one in the face﻿—Hi! Emma!”
Fortunately the programme girl was looking his way and realised that he was speaking to her.

===Chinese===
“嘿，”他说，蓝眼睛睁得老大盯着她，“我还当您年纪大得多呢。”
她笑了。
“实话是，我压根儿没看您。”他坦白道，“这些鬼东西瞪着人的脸，谁还看得见别的——喂，埃玛！”
幸而那位发目录的姑娘正朝他这边望，明白他是在同自己说话。

===Original===
“Your name is Emma, I suppose.”
“No, sir,” said the girl impressively, “my name is Evangeline.”
He turned to the girl.
“Here is an Evangeline whom I thought was an Emma; and here are my Emmas that I thought were Evangelines,” he said despairingly. “What made you come to this exhibition?”

===Chinese===
“你叫埃玛，我猜。”
“不是，先生。”姑娘郑重其事地说，“我叫伊万杰琳。”
他转向那姑娘。
“这儿是一位我当作埃玛的伊万杰琳；那儿是一批我当作伊万杰琳的埃玛。”他绝望地说，“您怎么想起来看这个展览？”

===Original===
“I saw a criticism of the pictures in yesterday’s papers.”
“In the *Megaphone*,” he said accusingly.
“Yes﻿—it was a very flattering criticism, I thought,” said the girl.
He nodded.

===Chinese===
“我在昨天的报纸上看见评这些画的文章。”
“登在《传声筒》报上。”他控诉似的说。
“是——我看那篇评论捧得很高。”姑娘说。
他点点头。

===Original===
“I wrote it myself,” he said without shame.
He turned to the programme girl.
“Tell your master to shut up the gallery, have the pictures packed away and sent home.”
“But,” said Moya in alarm, “I hope my stupid views won’t influence you.”

===Chinese===
“那是我自己写的。”他说，毫无愧色。
他转向发目录的姑娘。
“告诉你们老板，关了画廊，把这些画打包送回家去。”
“可是，”莫亚慌了，“我希望我那些蠢话不至于影响您。”

===Original===
“It isn’t your stupid view,” he said, “it is my original stupid view. You see, I can’t paint really. I know not the slightest thing about art, I have never had an artistic education or served under any master. I am a genius. These works are works of a genius. The frames cost a lot of money and the amount of paint I have used is prodigious. There is everything there,” he waved his hand to the covered walls, “except the know-how.”
She murmured a conventional expression of sympathy, but he did not invite sympathy, he invited condemnation and seemed to find a comfort in his own misfortune and was obviously all the happier, that he had reached a decision on his own merits.
They walked out of the gallery together and Moya wondered at herself. That she had in so brief a space of time entered into the aspirations and disappointments of a perfect stranger so that she felt something of his chagrin was truly amazing.
“I know you,” he said, breaking off in the midst of a sardonic dissertation on art, “you are Lady Moya Melton or Pelton.”

===Chinese===
“不是您的蠢话，”他说，“是我自己原装的正宗蠢见。您瞧，我其实不会画画。艺术我一窍不通，从没受过美术教育，也没拜过什么师父。我是个天才。这些作品是天才之作。画框花了一大笔钱，用的颜料更是海量。那儿什么都不缺，”他朝挂满画的四壁挥挥手，“就缺门道。”
她含含糊糊说了句例行的惋惜话，可他不要惋惜，他要的是谴责；他似乎在自己的不幸里找到了安慰，而且显然因为这是他自己拿定主意而愈发快活。
两人一同走出画廊，莫亚对自己暗暗称奇：在这样短的工夫里，她竟走进了一个素不相识的人的抱负与失意之中，连他的懊恼都能感同身受——这真是奇事一桩。
“我认得您。”他正发表着一篇嘲讽艺术的长篇大论，忽然打住，“您是莫亚·梅尔顿小姐，还是佩尔顿小姐？”

===Original===
“Felton,” she suggested, amused.
“Oh, yes, Felton,” he nodded. “I saw your portrait in the academy, a very bad portrait too.”
“People thought it was rather good,” she demurred.
“Idealised, but Lord, what do I know about art? This charabanc de luxe is yours, I presume,” he pointed to the big limousine.

===Chinese===
“费尔顿。”她觉得有趣，提醒道。
“哦，对，费尔顿。”他点头，“我在皇家美术院见过您的画像，画得糟透了。”
“人家觉得那画还不错呢。”她表示异议。
“理想化过了。可是天哪，我懂什么艺术？这辆豪华游览车想必是您的吧。”他指着那辆大轿车。

===Original===
“It does happen to be mine,” she said; “my father gave it to me on my twenty-first birthday.”
He inspected it critically.
“I wonder if I know as much about motorcars as I know about painting,” he said. “I used to think I knew something about both, but here, at any rate, is something real, it is a very nice car.”
He opened the door for her and she offered her hand.

===Chinese===
“它碰巧真是我的。”她说，“我父亲送我的二十一岁生日礼物。”
他挑剔地打量起车子来。
“不知道我对汽车的学问，是否也同对绘画的学问一样深。”他说，“我从前自以为两样都懂点儿。可这辆至少是件真东西——好车，非常不错。”
他为她开车门，她伸出手来。

===Original===
“I am so sorry about the pictures,” she said.
“Don’t worry,” he replied cheerfully.
She thought for a moment.
“Can I drop you anywhere?”

===Chinese===
“那些画的事，我真难过。”她说。
“别放在心上。”他快活地答道。
她想了一会儿。
“顺路送您去哪儿？”

===Original===
He fingered his unshaven chin.
“If you know of a nice deep pond where a man may drown himself without interference I should be obliged,” he said gravely, then, seeing the look of alarm in her eyes he laughed. “You probably don’t know my name,” he said.
As a matter of fact she did not and had been trying throughout the interview to take a surreptitious look at the catalogue. She knew it was something like Brixel.
“Fonso Blaxton﻿—” he said shortly. “Fonso stands for Alphonso, a perfectly rotten name, isn’t it? It would be quite all right for an artist. If there’s any need to send flowers, my address is Oxford Chambers.”

===Chinese===
他摸着没刮的下巴。
“您要是知道哪口又深又好的池塘，能让人不受打扰地淹死自己，我就感激不尽了。”他一本正经地说，随即看见她眼里的惊慌，笑了。“您多半还不知道我的名字。”他说。
她确实不知道，整场谈话里一直在偷偷想瞟一眼目录。她只记得那名字有点像布里克塞尔。
“方索·布拉克斯顿——”他简短地说，“方索是阿方索的简称，一个烂透了的名字，对不对？搁在画家身上倒正合适。要是有什么要送花圈的事，我的地址是牛津公寓。”

===Original===
He shook hands abruptly, handed her into the car and closed the door. He waited only the briefest spell and had lifted his hat and vanished before the car had started.
Moya drove back with so much to occupy her thoughts that she forgot to dream. So preoccupied was she, that she passed Sir Ralph Sapson and his chic companion turning into the park before she was aware that he was bowing to her or had time to note anything more about the lady than that she was very beautifully gowned and that her sunshade was tilted at such an angle that it was impossible to see her face.
“Who is your friend?”
Sir Ralph turned with a smirk.

===Chinese===
他唐突地握了握手，扶她上车，关上车门。他只站了极短的一会儿，举了举帽子，车还没开动人就不见了。
莫亚开车回去，满脑子心事，连梦都忘了做。她心不在焉到这般地步：拉尔夫·萨普森爵士携着他那衣着时髦的女伴正转弯进公园，她都开过去了，才发觉他在向自己鞠躬；至于那位女士，她只来得及看出她一身衣裳华贵之至，阳伞斜支的角度偏偏叫人看不见她的脸。
“您那位朋友是谁？”
拉尔夫爵士带着得意的笑转过头来。

===Original===
“That, Princess,” he said, “is Lady Moya Felton.”
“Oh, your fiancée,” said the girl, “isn’t it a bore being in London incognito; I should so much like to have met her.”
“Perhaps some day,” said Ralph.
“I should dearly love to,” murmured the girl; “but please go on, you interest me so much. I am beginning to realise why you English are so successful. You seem to know every detail of your business.”

===Chinese===
“公主殿下，”他说，“那是莫亚·费尔顿小姐。”
“哦，您的未婚妻。”姑娘说，“来伦敦还隐姓埋名，多闷呀；我真想见见她呢。”
“也许改天吧。”拉尔夫说。
“我可是求之不得。”姑娘喃喃道，“不过请接着讲吧，您真让我着迷。我渐渐明白你们英国人为什么这么成功了。您对自己的生意，仿佛了如指掌，连细节都一清二楚。”

===Original===
“Oh, dear no,” protested Sir Ralph good-humoredly. “I am rather a dunce if the truth be told, but one must know something of the details.”
“Something!” said the girl, raising her eyebrows. “I think you are very modest. Why, you seem to know the workings of your railway system from beginning to end.”
Sir Ralph stroked his moustache thoughtfully.
“One has to go into things,” he said vaguely, “and of course one takes a lot of credit for things which one is not entitled to take credit for. But the gold train was my idea altogether.”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，哪里。”拉尔夫爵士脾气很好地否认，“说实话我笨得很，不过生意上的事，总得知道个大概细节。”
“大概细节！”姑娘扬起眉毛，“我看您太谦虚了。瞧，贵国铁路系统从头到尾的运转，您简直门儿清。”
拉尔夫爵士若有所思地捋着小胡子。
“事情总得有人过问嘛。”他含含糊糊地说，“当然喽，人难免把不属于自己的功劳也记在自己账上。不过黄金列车这个点子，彻头彻尾是我一个人的。”

===Original===
“I never thought there was so much romance in business,” said the Princess, then suddenly, “do you mind telling the driver to turn about, I am tired of the park now.”
He leaned forward and instructed the chauffeur and the big car circled round.
“I am glad you suggested that,” he said.
“Why?” she asked.

===Chinese===
“我从没想到生意里还有这么多罗曼史。”公主说，随即忽然道，“麻烦您叫司机掉头好吗？这公园我看腻了。”
他俯身吩咐了司机，大轿车兜了个圈。
“亏得您提议掉头。”他说。
“为什么？”她问。

===Original===
“Did you notice a man in a grey felt hat talking to a lady in a victoria?”
She shook her head.
“He’s a weird bird,” said Sir Ralph; “he is a policeman, Michael Pretherston, Lord Pretherston’s brother. I don’t want to meet him, apart from the fact that he might recognize you, even through that veil of yours which would deny him so much happiness,” he added gallantly.
“Tell me some more about the gold train,” she said.

===Chinese===
“您留意到那个戴灰色毡帽、正同一位坐四轮敞篷马车的女士说话的男人吗？”
她摇摇头。
“那是个怪鸟。”拉尔夫爵士说，“他是个警察，迈克尔·普雷瑟斯顿，普雷瑟斯顿勋爵的弟弟。我不想碰见他——且不说他兴许认得出您，哪怕隔着您那层面纱——那可要剥夺他多少眼福啊。”他殷勤地补了一句。
“再给我讲讲黄金列车吧。”她说。

===Original===
Nothing loath Sir Ralph explained. He told the story of the Seahampton Docks and the big liners which would be coming in and the new services he had inaugurated to meet the increased traffic.
“We shall carry practically the whole of the gold which comes from the Rand mines,” he said impressively. “Naturally we have to be very careful although there is not much danger in England. The gold train is really two big safes on wheels. To outward appearance, they are just like ordinary closed railway trucks. In reality they are steel boxes, burglar proof and fire proof. Of course, nothing can go wrong and even if we had a smash the cars would be uninjured. But I have the best men on the system to run the train.”
“How very fascinating,” she said intensely interested. “I suppose you have a most elaborate timetable?”
“I have worked out every detail myself,” he said.

===Chinese===
拉尔夫爵士正巴不得讲。他说起西汉普顿码头的故事，说起将要进港的大邮轮，说起他为应付新增运量而开办的新线路。
“兰德金矿来的黄金，几乎全部将由我们承运。”他郑重其事地说，“自然，我们得万分小心，虽说在英国没什么危险。这列黄金列车，其实就是两只装了轮子的大保险柜。从外表看，它们同普通的封闭铁路货车毫无二致。实际上，它们是钢壳箱子，防盗防火。当然，绝出不了岔子；即便撞了车，那两节车厢也安然无恙。何况跑这趟车的人，都是全路系统里最好的手。”
“太引人入胜了。”她说，兴致浓得很，“想必你们有一张万分周密的行车时刻表？”
“每个细节都是我亲自敲定的。”他说。

===Original===
He took a notebook from his pocket.
“I will show you, Princess,” he said impressively.
He turned the gilt-edged leaves until he came to two pages covered with his fine writing.
“You will get some idea of the work involved in the running of a special train,” he said; “here are the times. There is the driver’s name, the fireman’s name, the assistant fireman’s name, the names of the two guards.”

===Chinese===
他从口袋里掏出一个笔记本。
“我给您看看，公主殿下。”他郑重地说。
他翻着烫金边的纸页，翻到写满娟秀小字的两页。
“您由此可以看出，开一列专列车要费多少事。”他说，“这儿是时刻。这儿有司机姓名、司炉姓名、副司炉姓名，还有两个乘警的名字。”

===Original===
She looked at the book.
“I cannot read your writing very well,” she laughed; “you must not forget that my family was very old fashioned and my dear father never allowed us to learn the Roman alphabet until we were quite grown up. But I can see what a very difficult business it is.”
She handed the book back to him with a little sigh.
“I am afraid I am very stupid,” she said; “figures always bother me and I can see that you revel in them. I hate writing, but by the way your book is filled, it seems that you revel in it! I cannot understand people who like to write. It is always an agony for me to compose an ordinary letter. My thoughts come so much faster than my poor hand can move.”

===Chinese===
她看着那个本子。
“您的字我可认不大了。”她笑着说，“您别忘了我家规矩极旧，亲爱的父亲从来不准我们学罗马字母，直到我们长大成人。可我看得出，这是桩顶顶麻烦的营生。”
她轻轻叹了口气，把本子还给他。
“恐怕我笨得很。”她说，“数字老跟我作对，我看您却乐在其中。我恨写字，可瞧您这本子填得满满当当，您写字想必也是乐在其中！我实在不懂喜欢写字的人。给我写封平平常常的信，也如同受刑。我的念头来得飞快，可怜这只手哪里跟得上。”

===Original===
She took a pad and pencil from the silver mounted stationery case in front of her.
“I will show you something,” she said.
She wrote rapidly, resting the pad on her knee and he watched her in astonishment as she proceeded to fill the sheet.
“There,” she said triumphantly, “that is what I can do best.”

===Chinese===
她从前头镶银的文具盒里取出一个便笺本和一支铅笔。
“我给您看样东西。”她说。
她把便笺本搁在膝上，奋笔疾书；看着她一页纸顷刻写满，他惊讶不已。
“瞧。”她得意洋洋地说，“这才是我最拿手的。”

===Original===
“It looks like shorthand,” he said.
“It is something like Russian shorthand,” said the girl, “and I am such a lazy person that I always use it whenever I want to write a note. My secretary, who is the only person in the world who understands it, transcribes it. I do it because I hate writing.”
“So you are clever, after all, Princess.”
She reached out her little hand and patted his arm.
“You don’t know how clever I am,” she said and they both laughed together.

===Chinese===
“看着像速记。”他说。
“它有点像俄文速记。”姑娘说，“我这人懒得很，凡要写个便条，一概用它。全世界只有我的秘书一人认得，由她誊清。我用它，就因为我恨写字。”
“这么说，您到底还是个聪明人，公主殿下。”
她伸出小小的手，拍了拍他的手臂。
“您还不知道我有多聪明呢。”她说。两人一齐大笑起来。

## XV As Sir Ralph Said, “Business Is Business” / 十五 正如拉尔夫爵士所说：生意是生意

===Original===
T. B. Smith walked into his outer office.
“Any news of Mr. Pretherston?” he asked.
“No, sir,” was the reply.
“Any news of Barr?”

===Chinese===
T·B·史密斯走进外间办公室。
“普雷瑟斯顿先生有消息吗？”他问。
“没有，先生。”回答如此。
“巴尔有消息吗？”

===Original===
“No, sir.”
T. B. clicked his lips impatiently.
“Who’s looking after them?”
“Detective-sergeant Grey, sir,” was the reply. “You know we traced him as far as Pinham Heights. After that he seems to have been lost sight of.”

===Chinese===
“没有，先生。”
T·B·不耐烦地咂了咂嘴。
“谁在盯这件事？”
“侦探警士格雷，先生。”对方答道，“您知道，我们追到平厄姆高地就断了线。打那以后，他似乎失踪了。”

===Original===
“Have you notified the chief constables of Hampshire, Sussex and Surrey?” asked T. B.
“That has been done, sir,” said the officer. “The local constabulary are making a search.”
T. B. bit his lips.
“I can understand Mr. Pretherston going,” he said, “but what has happened to Barr?”

===Chinese===
“汉普郡、苏塞克斯和萨里郡的警察局长都通知了吗？”T·B·问。
“都通知了，先生。”警官说，“当地警队正在搜查。”
T·B·咬住了嘴唇。
“普雷瑟斯顿先生出走，我能理解。”他说，“可巴尔出了什么事？”

===Original===
His subordinate very wisely offered no solution.
There were other anxious enquirers. Moya Felton had called that morning. Sir Ralph had made two visits to headquarters though it was doubtful whether his anxiety was in any way associated with the well being of Michael Pretherston.
“I think Michael will find the gang,” said T. B., “though he may be too late to get the gold.”
“What do I want the gang for?” demanded Sir Ralph wrathfully. “Will the government give me £2,800,000 for them? The gang can go to the devil so far as I am concerned. I want the gold.”

===Chinese===
他的下属非常识趣，一个解释也不给。
着急打听的还大有人在。莫亚·费尔顿那天上午来过。拉尔夫爵士两度造访总部——不过他的焦虑是否真与迈克尔·普雷瑟斯顿的安危有关，大可存疑。
“我想迈克尔会找到那伙人的。”T·B·说，“只是要追回黄金，恐怕就晚了。”
“我要那伙人干什么？”拉尔夫爵士怒气冲冲地质问，“政府会为那伙人赔我 2,800,000 镑吗？依我看，那伙人爱下地狱下地狱去。我要的是金子。”

===Original===
“You may get neither,” said T. B.; “at any rate, it ought to be very pleasing to you, Sir Ralph, that Michael Pretherston is risking his life to recover your property.”
“Isn’t he paid to do it?” demanded Sir Ralph. “Isn’t that the job of a policeman? By Gad! Commissioner, one would imagine that Pretherston was doing something out of the common! I take risks every day of my life.”
“If you could see my mind,” said T. B. Smith suavely, “you would realize that you are taking the biggest risk you have taken today. I advise you to go home and get into a calmer frame of mind.”
“When shall I hear anything?” asked the truculent baronet.

===Chinese===
“两样你也许都落空。”T·B·说，“不管怎样，拉尔夫爵士，迈克尔·普雷瑟斯顿正冒着性命危险替你追回财产，你总该觉得欣慰吧。”
“他干这个不是拿了薪水吗？”拉尔夫爵士质问，“这不就是警察的差事吗？老天！总监，听上去倒像普雷瑟斯顿干了什么了不得的大事！我天天都在冒险。”
“你要是看得见我的心思，”T·B·史密斯温和地说，“就会明白你正在冒今天最大的一次险。我劝你回家去，先把心气放平了。”
“我什么时候能听到信儿？”气势汹汹的从男爵问。

===Original===
“Whenever you are within earshot,” snapped the Commissioner. “Show Sir Ralph out, constable.”
Lord Flanborough did not obtrude his enquiries. He was so far reconciled to Moya that he could discuss the matter dispassionately, without reference to the mésalliance which threatened his family.
“I think on the whole, Moya,” he said, “I had better not see Ralph. After all, business is business and friends are friends; but I disclaim all responsibility for that gold after it left the ship. It is Ralph’s business entirely and I simply won’t accept his suggestion that I share his responsibility to the slightest degree.”
“Will he have to bear the loss?”

===Chinese===
“只要你在听得见的地方，随时。”总监厉声道，“警员，送拉尔夫爵士出去。”
弗兰伯勒勋爵没有紧着打听。他同莫亚总算言归于好，已经能不带感情地讨论这件事，只字不提那桩威胁他家的门户不当。
“总的看来，莫亚，”他说，“我还是不见拉尔夫为好。毕竟生意归生意，朋友归朋友；那批黄金一离船，其责任我就概不认账了。那是拉尔夫一个人的事，他倒暗示要我分担一丝一毫的责任，恕我不领受。”
“损失要他一个人扛吗？”

===Original===
“Well, partially bear the loss. A portion will be borne by the underwriters. Ralph, I am afraid, is a very mean man. I hate saying anything about my friends but Ralph is really economical to a point of meanness. I advised him to insure the gold and, to save a beggarly premium, he only insured half of it. I am very sorry for him,” he shook his head mournfully as a symbol of his sympathy. “I am very, very sorry for him, but I think it is better that we do not meet until this business matter is completely settled. On the whole,” he added thoughtfully, “perhaps it is better that your engagement with Ralph is broken off. He has said some very unkind things about you, Moya, which aroused my anger. I do not think you have been wise but I cannot allow any person to discuss you uncharitably.”
If the truth be told, Sir Ralph had said very little about the girl and very much about his lordship, whom he had accused of deliberately evading his responsibilities. This was at the one interview which they had had. It pleased Lord Flanborough to pose as a devoted father, but he did not deceive anybody but himself, for Moya had had a first hand account of the interview from Ralph who had asked her to use her influence to bring about a change in Lord Flanborough’s attitude.
It was the day after the disappearance of Michael Pretherston and Sir Ralph’s nerves were a little shaky. It was unfortunate in the circumstances that he had decided that afternoon to make a call upon the man who, a week before, he had fondly believed was to be his father-in-law. Lord Flanborough had not taken the precaution of warning his servants that he was not at home to Sir Ralph, so he had nobody to blame but himself when the door of his study was flung violently open that afternoon and Ralph Sapson stalked in.
“My dear Sapson,” stammered his lordship, flabbergasted by the unexpectedness of the visit. “Pray, do sit down.”

===Chinese===
“呃，扛一部分。有一部分由承保人承担。拉尔夫这个人，恕我直言，抠门得很。我很不愿意说朋友的闲话，可拉尔夫省俭得近乎吝啬。我劝他给黄金投保，他为了省那点可怜的保费，只保了一半。我很替他难过。”他悲哀地摇摇头，作为同情的表示，“我非常、非常替他难过。不过我想，这桩生意没个了结之前，我们最好别见面。总的说来，”他若有所思地补了一句，“你同拉尔夫解除婚约，或许倒是件好事。他说了你一些很不客气的话，莫亚，惹得我动了气。我不认为你当初明智，可我也不能容人刻薄你。”
说老实话，关于姑娘的话拉尔夫说得极少，关于勋爵大人的话倒说了一大堆，指责他存心推卸责任。那是他们唯一的一次会谈。弗兰伯勒勋爵乐意摆出一副慈父的架势，可他谁也没骗着，只骗了自己：那回会谈的详情，莫亚从拉尔夫那里得了第一手材料——拉尔夫还请她运用影响力，促成弗兰伯勒勋爵改变态度。
这是迈克尔·普雷瑟斯顿失踪的第二天，拉尔夫爵士的神经有点撑不住了。偏偏这天下午，他决定登门拜访那位一星期前他还痴心指望做岳父的人——时运不济。弗兰伯勒勋爵没来得及吩咐仆人“拉尔夫爵士来了一概回绝”，所以那天下午书房门被人一脚踹开、拉尔夫·萨普森大步闯进来时，他怪不得别人，只能怪自己。
“亲爱的萨普森，”勋爵大人口吃起来，被这不速之客吓了一跳，“快请坐，快请坐。”

===Original===
“I am not going to sit down. I tell you I am not going to sit down,” roared, rather than said, Ralph.
“Let me close the door,” said his lordship in alarm. “My dear man, please remember﻿—”
“I remember nothing except that I am on the brink of ruin. That is what it means. I am on the brink of ruin,” said Ralph, violently thumping the desk. “It is going to cost me a million and a half, and you must bear your share, Flanborough! You are responsible. If it had not been for your infernal daughter this would not have occurred.”
“My daughter,” said Lord Flanborough and feeling himself on perfectly safe ground he could speak with hauteur, “is not a matter for discussion and if you cannot speak respectfully of her, I beg you to leave this room.”

===Chinese===
“我不坐。告诉你，我不坐。”拉尔夫与其说是说，不如说是吼。
“让我把门关上。”勋爵大人慌忙说，“亲爱的伙计，请你记住——”
“我只记得我快破产了。就是这么回事。我快破产了。”拉尔夫说着，狠狠捶着书桌，“这要让我损失一百五十万，你的那份你必须出，弗兰伯勒！你要负责。若不是你那可恶的女儿，根本出不了这种事。”
“我的女儿，”弗兰伯勒勋爵说——自觉稳操胜券，说话也神气起来，“不在讨论之列。你若不能对她放尊重些，就请你离开这个房间。”

===Original===
“If it had not been for your daughter we should have remembered to send Griggs back.”
“I am not in charge of the railway,” said his lordship with mock humility. “I cannot order engine-drivers to return to Seahampton. Be reasonable, Sapson!”
“You have got to bear your share,” said the other doggedly, “you are morally responsible. I wish I had never thought of bringing your infernal ships to Seahampton.”
He was haggard and drawn of face. In two days he seemed to have shrunk so that his usually well-fitting clothes hung on him loosely.

===Chinese===
“要不是你女儿，我们就会记得把格里格斯派回去。”
“铁路又不归我管。”勋爵大人假装谦逊地说，“我总不能命令火车司机返回西汉普顿。讲点道理，萨普森！”
“你那份必须出。”对方死咬着不放，“道义上你难辞其咎。我真后悔，当初何必想起把你那些破船弄到西汉普顿来。”
他形容枯槁，面皮紧绷。才两天的工夫，他仿佛缩了水，平日合身的衣裳松松垮垮地挂在身上。

===Original===
“Everything can be discussed in a quiet businesslike way,” said Lord Flanborough. “I am very sorry that you have this loss. It is by no means certain that it is a loss, but business is business﻿—you cannot expect me to shoulder your responsibilities, my dear friend.”
“It is your responsibility as well as mine,” stormed Ralph, jumping up from his chair and advancing upon the little man who stepped cautiously backward, “and I insist upon your accepting your share.”
“Which would amount to?” suggested his lordship.
“About seven hundred thousand pounds,” growled the other.

===Chinese===
“什么事都可以心平气和、按生意规矩来谈。”弗兰伯勒勋爵说，“你遭了这场损失，我很遗憾。这算不算损失还远未可知呢。可是生意归生意——亲爱的朋友，你总不能指望我替你扛责任吧。”
“这是你的责任，也是我的责任。”拉尔夫咆哮着，从椅子上跳起来，朝小个子步步进逼；小个子小心翼翼地步步后退，“我坚持要你认下你那一份。”
“合多少钱呢？”勋爵大人提点道。
“大约七十万镑。”对方咆哮道。

===Original===
“Seven hundred thousand pounds! Impossible!” said Lord Flanborough emphatically.
Ralph turned livid.
“If you don’t,” he hissed, thumping his palm with his fist, “if you don’t﻿—”
At that moment help came in the shape of Moya. She nodded coolly to Sir Ralph and crossed the room to her father.

===Chinese===
“七十万镑！休想！”弗兰伯勒勋爵斩钉截铁地说。
拉尔夫的脸色转为铁青。
“你要是不认，”他咬牙低嘶，一拳拳砸着手心，“你要是不认——”
正在此时，救兵驾临，来的是莫亚。她朝拉尔夫爵士冷淡地点点头，穿过房间走到父亲身边。

===Original===
“There is no news of Michael,” she said.
“Dear me,” sighed his lordship.
“Michael!” sneered Ralph. “There is no news of the money! That’s the important thing, Moya!”
“We are not on the ‘Moya’ terms any more, Sir Ralph,” she said quietly.

===Chinese===
“迈克尔还没有消息。”她说。
“哎呀。”勋爵大人叹道。
“迈克尔！”拉尔夫冷笑，“钱没有消息，这才是要紧的，莫亚！”
“咱们之间已经不兴叫‘莫亚’了，拉尔夫爵士。”她平静地说。

===Original===
“Rub it in,” groaned the man.
“I don’t want to rub it in. We all have our troubles, but some of us bear them less courageously than others. It won’t ruin you if you do lose all this money. You know you are enormously rich.”
“I am not going to lose,” said Sir Ralph doggedly; “your father has to bear his share.”
“If father is responsible he will bear his share,” said the girl, “but it is not by any means certain that he is responsible, is it, papa?”

===Chinese===
“哪壶不开提哪壶。”那人呻吟道。
“我不是故意刺你。谁都有难处，只是有人扛得勇敢些，有人差些罢了。就算这些钱全丢了，也毁不了你。你自己知道，你富得流油。”
“我认损就没完。”拉尔夫爵士死咬着，“你父亲必须出他那一份。”
“父亲要是有责任，他会出他那一份。”姑娘说，“可他有没有责任，还远说不上呢，对吧，爸爸？”

===Original===
“Certainly not,” said Lord Flanborough, placing a table between himself and his infuriated partner.
There was a tap at the door and Sibble came in, somewhat furtively.
He looked mysteriously at Moya and she went to him.
“What is it, Sibble?” she asked.

===Chinese===
“当然说不上。”弗兰伯勒勋爵说，顺手把一张桌子横在他和怒火中烧的合伙人之间。
有人轻叩房门，西布尔溜了进来，神色鬼祟。
他冲莫亚神秘地使了个眼色，她便走了过去。
“什么事，西布尔？”她问。

===Original===
“There’s a man to see you, miss,” he said. “I think it is something very special.”
“To see me? Who is he?”
“I don’t know who he is, miss, but he has a very special message for you.”
She went out into the hall. A respectable looking man stood hat in hand. By his thick coat she thought at first he was an omnibus driver. In a sense, she was right.

===Chinese===
“有位先生要见您，小姐。”他说，“我看这事非同小可。”
“见我？他是谁？”
“不知道他是谁，小姐，可他捎来了顶要紧的话，是给您的。”
她走到门厅里。一个体面模样的男人手里捏着帽子站在那儿。看他那件厚外套，她起初只当是个公共马车司机。某种意义上说，她猜对了。

===Original===
“Are you Lady Moya Felton, madame?”
“Yes,” said the girl.
He handed her a card. She took it. It was a business card announcing that Messrs. Acton and Arkwright, contractors, were prepared to remove anything from machinery to furniture and that they had a “larger number of motor lorries than any other firm doing business in the south of England.”
“I am afraid there is a mistake,” she said. “I didn’t send for you.”

===Chinese===
“您是莫亚·费尔顿小姐吗，夫人？”
“正是。”姑娘说。
他递上一张名片。她接过来。那是一张营业名片，声明阿克顿与阿克赖特承运行承接各种搬运业务，上至机器下到家具，且拥有“英格兰南部同行中数量最多的运货汽车”。
“恐怕是弄错了。”她说，“我没派人请你们来。”

===Original===
“No, miss, we’ve brought the goods.”
“The goods?” she said puzzled.
He led the way to the door.
Lining one side of the street and stretching from the house to the corner of Gaspard Place were ten motor lorries.

===Chinese===
“没错，小姐，我们是送货来的。”
“货？”她一头雾水。
他在头里引路到门口。
沿街一侧停着十辆运货汽车，从宅门一直排到加斯帕德广场的拐角。

===Original===
“Here’s the name.”
He turned the card over.
“Lord Flanborough, Felton House, Grosvenor Avenue,” said the man reading it over her shoulder.
“Have you any letter?”

===Chinese===
“喏，名字在这儿。”
他把名片翻了过来。
“弗兰伯勒勋爵，格罗夫纳大道费尔顿府。”那人凑在她肩后念道。
“你们带了什么信件吗？”

===Original===
“No, miss, these are all the instructions I had. I was told to bring the chemicals to his lordship and ask for you.”
“Chemicals?” she said.
Her father had followed her to the door.
“What is it?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“没有，小姐，我得到的吩咐就这些。人家吩咐我把这批药品交给勋爵大人，并且指名要找您。”
“药品？”她说。
父亲随她走到了门口。
“什么事？”他问。

===Original===
“This man has brought some chemicals for you.”
“Oh, nonsense, there is some mistake,” said Lord Flanborough. “I am not a chemist.”
He went down the steps with the girl to the first lorry. She looked inside and apparently it was empty.
“What is it you have brought?” she asked in surprise.

===Chinese===
“这人给您送来了一批药品。”
“哦，胡闹，准是弄错了。”弗兰伯勒勋爵说，“我又不是药剂师。”
他同姑娘走下台阶，来到头一辆货车前。她往里一看，车厢里分明是空的。
“你们送的是什么呀？”她惊讶地问。

===Original===
“There they are, miss, on the floor.”
And then she saw a number of packages wrapped in sacking.
“They’re pretty heavy,” said the man, “considering their size.”
She reached out her hand and tried to draw one toward her. It defied her efforts. Lord Flanborough tried and succeeded in moving it. Something in its shape startled him.

===Chinese===
“在这儿呢，小姐，在车板上。”
这时她才看见一堆用麻布包裹的货包。
“论个头，”那人说，“这些家伙沉得够呛。”
她伸手去拖一个，纹丝不动。弗兰伯勒勋爵上手，才挪动了它。那形状里的什么东西让他心里一惊。

===Original===
“Have you a knife?” he asked the man.
The contractor produced a big clasp knife and opened it.
“Be careful, my lord,” he warned, “they’re dangerous﻿—”
But Lord Flanborough had ripped the canvas package and exposed a dull yellow ingot. He dropped the knife and stepped back.

===Chinese===
“你有刀吗？”他问那人。
搬运商掏出一把大折刀，打开递来。
“当心，大人，”他提醒道，“这东西危险——”
可是弗兰伯勒勋爵已经撕开帆布包，露出一块暗黄色的金锭。他撒手扔了刀，倒退一步。

===Original===
“How many wagons are there?” he asked huskily.
“Ten, sir. They’ve all got the same number of packages﻿—and are we to take them to the Docks?”
Lord Flanborough made a rapid calculation.
“Take them into the basement and put them into the coal cellar,” he said and went up the steps two at a time and back into his study.

===Chinese===
“一共几辆车？”他嗓音发哑地问。
“十辆，先生。每辆装的货包一样多——我们这就把它们送到码头去吗？”
弗兰伯勒勋爵飞快地心算了一下。
“搬进地下室，码到煤窖里去。”他说着，三步并作两步跨上台阶，回书房去了。

===Original===
Sir Ralph was still waiting. The rudeness of his host neither increased nor decreased his irritation.
Lord Flanborough stepped up to him briskly.
“Look here, Sapson,” he said. “What responsibility do you want me to bear in the matter of this gold?”
“I want you to bear half.”

===Chinese===
拉尔夫爵士还在等。主人的失礼既没添他的火，也没消他的气。
弗兰伯勒勋爵快步走到他跟前。
“听我说，萨普森。”他说，“这批黄金的事，你要我担几分责任？”
“我要你担一半。”

===Original===
“I will do more than that,” said his lordship. “I will assume the whole responsibility for two hundred thousand pounds.”
Ralph swung round.
“You will?” he said incredulously.
“I will.”

===Chinese===
“我不止担一半。”勋爵大人说，“我出二十万镑，把责任全认下来。”
拉尔夫霍地转过身。
“你肯？”他不敢相信。
“我肯。”

===Original===
“Done,” said Sir Ralph and pulled out his cheque book.
He wrote quickly and nervously but quite legibly enough and handed the slip to Lord Flanborough, what time his lordship was writing with more leisure but no less excitement on the other side of the table.
“There’s your cheque,” said Sir Ralph.
“And there’s my note freeing you from responsibility,” said his lordship.

===Chinese===
“成交。”拉尔夫爵士说着，掏出了支票簿。
他写得又快又紧张，好在字迹够清楚，写完把纸条递给弗兰伯勒勋爵；与此同时，勋爵大人正在桌子那头从容些、激动却不减分毫地写着字。
“这是你的支票。”拉尔夫爵士说。
“这是我给你出的字据，从此责任与你无干。”勋爵大人说。

===Original===
“I am sorry I have been so unpleasant,” said the baronet wiping his steaming brow, “but you will understand.”
“I quite understand,” said Lord Flanborough.
“Business is business,” said Ralph.
“Business is business,” repeated his lordship and folding the cheque slipped it into his pocket.

===Chinese===
“抱歉我方才失态了。”从男爵抹着冒汗的额头说，“你会体谅的。”
“我完全体谅。”弗兰伯勒勋爵说。
“生意是生意。”拉尔夫说。
“生意是生意。”勋爵大人重复道，把支票折好，塞进了口袋。

## I Eighty-Three Pearls on a String / 一 一串八十三颗珍珠

===Original===
The Earl of Flanborough pressed a bell push by the side of his study table and, after an interval of exactly three seconds, pressed it again, though the footman’s lobby could not have been far short of fifty yards from the library and the serving man was never born who could sprint that distance in three seconds.
Yet, in such awe was his lordship held that morning by his man-servants, his maidservants and everything within his gates, that Sibble, the first footman, made the distance in five.
“Why the dickens don’t you answer my bell when I ring?” snapped the Earl and glared at his red-faced servant.
Sibble did not reply, knowing by experience that, even as silence was insolence, speech could be nothing less than impertinence.
Lord Flanborough was slightly over middle age, thin, bald and dyspeptic. His face was mean and insignificant and if you looked for any resemblance to the somewhat pleasant faces of the Feltons and Flanboroughs of past generations which stared mildly or fiercely, or (as in the case of the first Baron Felton and Flanborough, a poet and contemporary of Lovelace) with gentle melancholy from their massive frames in the long hall, you looked in vain. For George Percy Allington Felton, Earl of Flanborough, Baron Felton and Baron Sedgely of Waybrook, was only remotely related to the illustrious line of Feltons and had inherited the title and the heavily mortgaged estates of his great-uncle by sheer bad luck. This was the uncharitable view of truer Feltons who stood, however, more remotely in the line of succession.

===Chinese===
弗兰伯勒伯爵按了按书房桌边的电铃，隔了恰好三秒钟，又按了一遍——其实男仆候班的门厅离书房少说也有五十码，而天生能三秒钟冲刺完这段路的侍者，还没有生出来过呢。
然而那天早晨，勋爵大人威风所慑，男女仆役乃至府里的一切，无不畏服：一等男仆西布尔居然五秒钟就赶完了这段路。
“喂，我按了铃，你怎么敢不来应？”伯爵厉声呵斥，瞪着那张红脸的仆人。
西布尔不作声——经验告诉他：沉默固然无礼，开口更是放肆。
弗兰伯勒勋爵年过半百还多一些，干瘦，秃顶，有胃病。他那张脸刻薄而无关紧要；长廊里那一辈辈费尔顿家与弗兰伯勒家先人的面容——或温和或威严地、或（如第一位费尔顿-弗兰伯勒男爵、诗人洛夫莱斯的同时代人那样）带着温柔的忧郁——从巨大的画框里望着你，你若想在他脸上寻出半点相似，那是白费工夫。因为这位乔治·珀西·阿灵顿·费尔顿——弗兰伯勒伯爵、费尔顿男爵兼韦布鲁克的塞奇利男爵——同显赫的费尔顿世系只有一点远亲关系，全凭十足的坏运气，继承了叔祖的爵位和负债累累的田产。这是血脉更正的费尔顿们刻薄的说法——不过论继承顺位，他们确实排得更远。

===Original===
Lord Flanborough had been Mr. George Felton of Felton, Heinrich and Somes, a firm which controlled extensive mining properties in various parts of the world, and the one bright spot in his succession to the peerage lay in the fact that he brought some two millions sterling to the task of freeing the estates of their encumbrances.
He was a shrewd man and an unpleasant man, but he had never been so objectionably unpleasant until he assumed the style and title of Flanborough and never so completely and impossibly unpleasant in the period of his lordship as he had been that morning.
“Now, what did I want you for?” asked Lord Flanborough in vexation. “I rang for something﻿—if you had only answered at once instead of dawdling about, I should﻿—ah, yes﻿—tell Lady Moya that I wish to see her.”
Sibble made his escape thankfully.
Lord Flanborough pulled at his weedy moustache and looked at the virgin sheet of paper before him. Then he took up his pen and wrote:

===Chinese===
弗兰伯勒勋爵原先不过是费尔顿-海因里希-索姆斯公司的乔治·费尔顿先生——那家公司掌管着世界各地的庞大矿业产业。他承袭爵位这件事里唯一的亮色是：他带来了大约两百万英镑，正好用来给这些田产解押。
他精明，也不讨人喜欢；可他从来没有像冠上弗兰伯勒的头衔之后那样讨人嫌，也从来没有像今天早晨这样，在勋爵任内讨人嫌到这步无可救药的田地。
“咦，我叫你来干什么来着？”弗兰伯勒勋爵懊恼地问，“我按铃总有件事——你要是马上应声，不在那儿磨蹭，我早就……啊，对了——去告诉莫亚小姐，我要见她。”
西布尔如蒙大赦，赶紧溜了。
弗兰伯勒勋爵捻着稀疏的小胡子，望着面前那张白纸。随后他提起笔，写道：

===Original===
> Lost or Stolen: Valuable pearl chain consisting of eighty-three graduated pearls. Any person giving information which will lead to their recovery will receive a reward of two hundred pounds.
He paused; scratched out “two hundred pounds” and substituted “one hundred pounds.” This did not satisfy him and he altered the sum to “fifty pounds.” He sat considering even this modest figure and eventually struck out that amount and wrote, “will be suitably rewarded.”
He heard the door click and looked up.
“Ah﻿—Moya. I am just tinkering away at an advertisement,” he said with a smile.
The Lady Moya Felton was twenty-two and pretty. She recollected in her admirable person many of the traditional family graces which had so malignantly avoided her parent. Well-shaped and of a gracious carriage, though no more than medium in height, the face with its delicacy of moulding was wholly Felton. If the stubborn chin, the firm mouth and the china-blue eyes had come from the dead and gone Sedgelys, the hair of bronze gold was peculiarly Feltonesque.

===Chinese===
> 寻物或寻赃：珍贵珍珠项链一条，共八十三颗，颗颗由大渐小。凡提供线索助其寻回者，酬谢两百镑。
他停了笔；把“两百镑”划掉，改成“一百镑”。还不满意，又把数目改成“五十镑”。他坐着盘算这个已经相当克己的数字，最后索性把这笔也划去，写上“必有重酬”。
他听见门上一响，抬起头来。
“啊——莫亚。我正捣鼓一则启事呢。”他含笑说。
莫亚·费尔顿小姐二十二岁，人长得漂亮。历代先人的种种门风家范——那些偏偏恶意躲开了她父亲的——都在她身上出色地重现了。她身段匀称，仪态娴雅，个子只算中等；那张脸轮廓秀致，是十足的费尔顿家风。倔强的下巴、坚决的嘴和瓷蓝色的眼睛或许得自作古的塞奇利家，那一头青铜色的金发却是费尔顿家独有的。

===Original===
When she spoke, however, the carping critic might complain that her voice lacked the rich quality upon which the family prided itself, for the Feltons were orators in those days when a parliamentary speech read like something out of a book. Moya’s voice was a trifle hard and without body; it was also just a little unsympathetic. Lord Flanborough boasted with good cause that his daughter was a “practical little woman” and at least one man beside her father could testify to this quality.
“Dear, don’t you think it is a little absurd﻿—advertising?” asked the girl.
She seated herself at the other side of the desk and, reaching out her hand, opened a silver box and helped herself to one of her father’s cigarettes.
“Why absurd, darling?” asked Lord Flanborough testily; “lost property has been found before now, by means of advertising. I remember years ago when I was in the city, there was a fellow named Goldberg﻿—”
“Please forget all about the city for a moment,” she smiled, lighting her cigarette, “and review all the circumstances. Firstly, I had the pearls when I was at Lady Machinstones’ house.
