I danced with quiet, respectable people﻿—Sir Ralph Sapson, Sir George Felixburn, Lord Fethington, Major Aitkens, and that awfully nice boy of Machinstones. *They* didn’t steal them. I had the pearls when I left, because I saw them as I was fastening my fur cloak. I had them in the car because I touched them just before we reached the house. I don’t remember taking them off﻿—but then I was dead tired and hardly remember going to bed. Obviously, Martin is the thief. She is the only person who has access to my room; she helped me undress; it is as plain as a pikestaff.”

===Chinese===
不过她一开口，挑剔的批评家兴许要抱怨：她的嗓音缺了自家引以为傲的那种醇厚——费尔顿家发迹的年代，一篇议会演说读起来就跟书上的文章似的有板有眼。莫亚的嗓音略硬，缺底气，还有点不近人情。弗兰伯勒勋爵夸自己的女儿是“务实的小女人”，倒也事出有据；除了她父亲，至少还有一个男人可以为此作证。
“亲爱的，你不觉得登启事有点可笑吗？”姑娘问。
她在桌子另一边坐下，伸手打开一只银烟盒，自取了父亲一支烟。
“怎么可笑，宝贝儿？”弗兰伯勒勋爵不耐烦地问，“失物靠登广告找回来的，从前也有过。我记得多年前进城做事那会儿，有个姓戈德堡的家伙——”
“请先把城里的事忘一分钟。”她笑着点上烟，“咱们把情形从头捋一遍。第一，我在马钦斯通太太府上的时候，珠链还在。陪我跳舞的都是安静体面的人——拉尔夫·萨普森爵士、乔治·费利克斯伯恩爵士、费辛顿勋爵、艾特肯斯少校，还有马钦斯通家那个好得不得了的小子。总不至于是*他们*偷的。离开的时候珠链还在，因为我系皮斗篷时还看见它来着。到宅门口之前我还摸着它，所以在车上也在。我不记得摘过它——可我实在累得要死，连怎么上的床都记不清。明摆着，贼就是马丁。能进我房间的只有她一个；是她伺候我宽的衣；这跟白纸黑字一样清楚。”

===Original===
Lord Flanborough tapped his large teeth with his penholder, a practice of his which annoyed his daughter beyond words, though at the moment she deemed it expedient to overlook the fault. The loss had frightened her, for the pearls were worth three thousand pounds and she was one of those people whose standard of values had a currency basis.
“I have asked Scotland Yard to send their very best man,” said Lord Flanborough importantly. “Where is Martin?”
“Locked in her room﻿—I have told Fellows to sit outside her door,” said the girl, and then, interestedly, “When will the detective arrive?”
Lord Flanborough picked up an open telegraph form from the table.
“ ‘Sending Inspector Pretherston’﻿—by Jove!”

===Chinese===
弗兰伯勒勋爵拿笔杆敲着他那口大牙——这个习惯他女儿厌恶得无以复加，只是此刻觉得不便计较。这场失窃吓得她不轻：那串珠子值三千镑，而她的价值观向来是以通货为本位的。
“我已经请苏格兰场派他们最得力的人来。”弗兰伯勒勋爵煞有介事地说，“马丁在哪儿？”
“锁在她自己房里——我吩咐费罗斯守在她门口了。”姑娘说，随即又来了兴致，“侦探什么时候到？”
弗兰伯勒勋爵从桌上拿起一张已拆开的电报纸。
“‘现派普雷瑟斯顿探长前来’——好家伙！”

===Original===
He blinked across the desk at his daughter.
“Pretherston,” she repeated thoughtfully; “isn’t it strange?”
“Pretherston﻿—hum,” said her father and looked at her again.
If he expected to see any confusion, any heightening of color, even so much as a faltering of glance, he was relieved, for she met his gaze steadfastly, save that there was a faraway look in her eyes and a certain speculative narrowing of lids.
The romance was five years old, and if she cherished the memory of it, it was the charity which she might show to a favored piece in her china cupboard; it was something to be taken out and dusted at intervals. Michael Pretherston was a bad match from every point of view, though his invalid cousin was a peer of the realm and Michael would one day be Pretherston of Pretherston. He was hideously poor, he was casual, he had no respect for wealth, he held the most outrageous views on the church, society and the state; he was, in fact, something as nearly approaching an anarchist as Lord Flanborough ever expected or feared to meet.

===Chinese===
他从桌子那边冲女儿直眨眼。
“普雷瑟斯顿。”她若有所思地念了一遍，“这不奇怪吗？”
“普雷瑟斯顿——嗯。”她父亲应了一声，又看了她一眼。
他若是指望看见她慌乱、脸红，哪怕只是目光一颤，那他可以放心了：她坦然迎着他的注视，只是眼神有点飘远，眼皮微微一眯，像在打什么盘算。
那段罗曼史已是五年前的旧事。她若还珍藏着那段记忆，也不过是她对瓷器柜里某件爱物的怜惜：时不时取出来掸掸灰罢了。迈克尔·普雷瑟斯顿从哪头看都不是门好亲事，虽说他那多病的堂兄是位上院贵族，他将来总有一天会成为“普雷瑟斯顿家的普雷瑟斯顿”。他穷得吓人，吊儿郎当，对财富毫无敬意，对教会、社会和国家抱持最无法无天的见解；实际上，弗兰伯勒勋爵平生料想或惧怕会碰见的无政府主义者，数他最像。

===Original===
His wooing had been brief but tempestuous. The girl had been overwhelmed and had given her promise. Recovering her reason in the morning and realizing (as she said) that love was not “everything,” she had written him a letter of fourteen pages in which she had categorically set forth the essential conditions to their union. These called for the abandonment of all his principles, the reestablishment of all his shattered beliefs and an estimate of the cost of placing Pretherston Court in a state of repair suitable for the reception of the Lady Moya Pretherston (née Felton).
To her fourteen pages, he had returned a thirty-two page letter which was at once an affront and a justification for anarchy. It was not a love-letter; rather was it something between a pamphlet by Henry George and a treatise by Jean Jacques Rousseau, interspersed with passionate appeals to her womanhood and offensive references to her “huckster-souled” father.
“He was always a wild sort of chap,” said Lord Flanborough, shaking his head darkly. “I understood that he had gone abroad.”
“I suppose there are other Pretherstons,” said the girl; “still it *is* strange, isn’t it?”
“Do you ever feel﻿ ﻿… ?” began her father awkwardly.

===Chinese===
他的求婚短促而狂风暴雨。姑娘被冲昏了头，许下了诺言。第二天早晨理智回位，她（用她自己的话说）认识到爱情并非“一切”，便给他写了一封十四页的长信，条分缕析地开列了两人结合的必要条件：他须放弃全部原则，重建全部业已粉碎的信仰，并预估一笔修缮普雷瑟斯顿庄园的费用，修到配迎接莫亚·普雷瑟斯顿小姐（娘家姓费尔顿）入住为止。
对她的十四页，他回了一封三十二页的信，那既是对她的冒犯，也是对无政府主义的辩护。那不是情书；倒像是亨利·乔治的小册子和让-雅克·卢梭的论文的混血，中间还夹着对她女性情怀的火热呼吁，外加对她那位“市侩灵魂”父亲的冒犯性议论。
“他向来是个野小子。”弗兰伯勒勋爵阴着脸摇头说，“我还当他出国去了呢。”
“普雷瑟斯顿这姓，天下总还有第二家吧。”姑娘说，“可这事儿到底是巧，不是吗？”
“你有没有有时候觉得……？”她父亲尴尬地起了个头。

===Original===
She smiled and laid down her cigarette on the crystal ashtray.
“He was wholly impossible,” she agreed.
There came a gentle tap at the door and a girl entered.
She was dressed neatly in black, and her prettiness was of a different type to that of her employer (for Lady Moya indulged in the luxury of a secretary). It was a beautiful face with a hint of tragedy in the down-turned lips and, it seemed, a history of wild sorrow in her big grey eyes. Yet of sorrow she knew nothing, and such tragedy as she had met had left her unmoved. Her abundant hair was of a rich brown; the hand that clasped a notebook to her bosom was small and artistic. She was an inch taller than Lady Moya, but because she did not show the same erectness of carriage she seemed shorter.
“Father, you asked me to let you have Miss Tenby this morning,” said Lady Moya with a nod for the girl. “I don’t know whether you will still want her?”

===Chinese===
她笑了笑，把香烟搁在水晶烟灰缸上。
“他根本要不得。”她表示同意。
门上轻轻一叩，一个姑娘走了进来。
她穿一身黑，干净利落，长得也标致，却与她的雇主不是同一路（莫亚小姐可是养得起秘书这份阔气的）。那是一张美丽的脸：微垂的唇角带着一丝悲剧的影子，灰色的大眼睛里仿佛藏着一段狂乱的伤心史。其实伤心她一无所知，碰见过的悲剧也从未打动过她。她一头浓密的深棕色头发；把笔记本抱在胸前的那只手小巧而有艺术气质。她比莫亚小姐高出一英寸，只因不像对方那样挺拔，反倒显得矮些。
“爸爸，今早你吩咐我把滕比小姐给你送来。”莫亚小姐朝那姑娘点点头说，“我不知道你眼下还用不用她？”

===Original===
“I am *so* sorry this dreadful thing has happened, Lord Flanborough,” said the girl in a low voice; “it must be terrible to feel that there is a thief in the house.”
Lord Flanborough smiled good-humoredly.
“We shall recover the pearls, I am certain,” he said; “don’t let it worry you, Miss Tenby﻿—I hope you are comfortable?”
“Very, Lord Flanborough,” said the girl gratefully.
“And the work is not too hard, eh?”

===Chinese===
“发生这种可怕的事，我实在难过，弗兰伯勒勋爵。”姑娘低声说，“家里出了贼，那种滋味一定糟透了。”
弗兰伯勒勋爵和颜悦色地一笑。
“珠子一定找得回来，我有把握。”他说，“别为这个发愁，滕比小姐——你住得还舒适吧？”
“很舒适，弗兰伯勒勋爵。”姑娘感激地说。
“活计也不算太重，呃？”

===Original===
The girl smiled slightly.
“It is nothing﻿—I feel awfully ashamed of myself sometimes. I have been with you a month and have hardly earned my salt.”
“That’s all right,” replied his lordship with great condescension; “you have already been of the greatest assistance to me and we shall find you plenty of other work. I was glad to see you in church on Sunday. The vicar tells me that you are a regular attendant.”
The girl inclined her head, but said nothing. For a while she waited and then at a word of polite dismissal, she left the library.
“Deuced nice girl, that,” said his lordship approvingly.

===Chinese===
姑娘浅浅一笑。
“不算什么——有时我自己都惭愧得很。来了一个月，简直白拿工钱。”
“不打紧。”勋爵大人极其恩赐地答道，“你帮我的忙已经帮得极大了，往后有的是活计给你。上礼拜天在教堂看见你，我很高兴。牧师告诉我，你是常来做礼拜的。”
姑娘欠了欠头，没说什么。她等了一会儿，等来一句客气的打发，便退出书房去了。
“顶好的姑娘。”勋爵大人赞许地说。

===Original===
“She works well and quickly, and she can read French beautifully﻿—I was very fortunate,” said Moya carelessly. “What were we talking about when she came in? Oh, yes﻿—Michael Pretherston. I wonder now﻿—”
The door opened and a footman announced,
“Inspector Pretherston, m’lord.”
“Inspector Michael Pretherston, you silly ass,” corrected the annoyed young man in the doorway.
It was Michael, then!

===Chinese===
“她干活又快又好，法文也念得漂亮——我好运气。”莫亚漫不经心地说，“她进来之前咱们说到哪儿了？哦，对——迈克尔·普雷瑟斯顿。我倒纳闷——”
门开了，一名男仆高声通报：
“普雷瑟斯顿探长到，大人。”
“是迈克尔·普雷瑟斯顿探长，你这蠢驴。”门口那位恼火的青年纠正道。
果然是迈克尔！

===Original===
A little older, a little better-looking, a little more decisive﻿—but Michael, as impetuous and irresponsible as ever.
“He spoilt my entrance, Moya,” he laughed, as he came with rapid strides toward the girl; “how are you after all these years﻿—as pretty as ever, confound you. Ah, Lord Flanborough, you’re wearing well﻿—I read your speech in the House of Lords on the Shipping Bill﻿—a fine speech; did you make it up yourself?”
Moya laughed softly and saved what might have been a most embarrassing situation﻿—for his lordship was framing a dignified protest against the suggestion that he had shared the honours of authorship.
“You are not changed, Michael,” she said, looking at him with undisguised, but none the less, detached admiration; “but what on earth are you doing in the police force?”
“Extraordinary,” murmured Lord Flanborough, and added humorously, “and an anarchist, too.”

===Chinese===
老了一点，俊了一点，果断了一点——但终究是迈克尔，依旧那么风风火火，依旧那么没个正经。
“他把我出场的好戏搅了，莫亚。”他大笑着说，快步向姑娘走来，“这些年不见，你可好——还是那么漂亮，真可恨。啊，弗兰伯勒勋爵，你保养得不错——上院那次航运法案的演说我拜读了——好演说；是你自己写的吗？”
莫亚轻轻一笑，把一场极可能难堪的局面圆了过去——勋爵大人正组织一篇堂皇的抗议，抗议对方暗示那篇演出的著作权还有他一份。
“你倒是一点没变，迈克尔。”她说，毫不掩饰地欣赏着他，欣赏里却又隔着一层，“可你到底怎么进了警察队伍？”
“真是奇了。”弗兰伯勒勋爵喃喃道，又打趣地补一句，“还是个无政府主义者呢。”

===Original===
“It is a long story,” said Michael. “I really received my promotion in the Special Branch﻿—the Foreign Office Branch﻿—and was transferred to the C.I.D. after we caught the Callam crowd, the Continental confidence tricksters. It is disgraceful that I should be an inspector, isn’t it? But merit tells!” He chuckled again, then of a sudden grew serious. “I’m forgetting I’ve a job to do﻿—what’s the trouble?”
Lord Flanborough explained the object of his urgent call, and a look of disappointment appeared upon Michael Pretherston’s face.
“A miserable little larceny,” he said reproachfully. “I thought at least Moya had been kidnapped. Now, tell me all that happened on the night you lost the pearls.”
Step by step the girl related her movements and the periods at which she had evidence that the pearls were still with her.
“And then you reached your bedroom,” said Michael, “and what happened there? First of all, you took your fur wrap off.”

===Chinese===
“说来话长。”迈克尔说，“我这一级其实是在特别科——外事科——升上来的；抓了卡拉姆那伙大陆骗局老手之后，才调到刑事调查科。我居然当上探长，丢人吧？可功劳就是硬道理！”他又咯咯一笑，随即忽然正经起来，“差点忘了我是来办案的——出了什么事？”
弗兰伯勒勋爵说明了他急电相请的来由，迈克尔·普雷瑟斯顿脸上现出失望。
“一桩寒酸的小小失窃。”他责备似的说，“我还当至少是莫亚遭了绑票。好了，把丢珠子那天夜里的事，从头说给我听。”
姑娘一步一步交代了自己的行踪，以及在哪几个时点她能证明珠链还在身上。
“然后你就进了卧室。”迈克尔说，“在那儿出了什么事？头一件，你把皮斗篷脱了。”

===Original===
“Yes,” nodded the girl.
“Were you in a cheerful frame of mind or were you rather cross?”
“Does that matter?” she asked in surprise.
“Everything matters to the patient and systematic officer of the law. Temperamental clues are as interesting and material as any other.”
“Well, if the truth were told,” she confessed, “I was rather cross and very tired.”

===Chinese===
“是。”姑娘点头。
“你当时心情愉快呢，还是有点烦躁？”
“这也要紧吗？”她惊讶地问。
“在耐心而有章法的执法官眼里，样样都要紧。性情的线索同别的线索一样有趣、一样顶用。”
“呃，说老实话，”她招了，“我相当烦躁，也累得很。”

===Original===
“Did you take your cloak off, or did your woman?”
“I took it off myself,” she said after a pause, “and hung it up.”
He asked her a few more questions.
“Now, we will see the sorrowful Martin,” he said, “and let me tell you this, Moya, that if this girl is innocent she has grounds for action against you for false imprisonment.”
“What do you mean?” demanded Lord Flanborough with asperity. “I have a perfect right to detain anybody I think is guilty of theft.”

===Chinese===
“斗篷是你自己脱的，还是你的女仆脱的？”
“我自己脱的。”她顿了顿说，“把它挂了起来。”
他又问了她几个问题。
“好了，咱们去见见那位愁眉苦脸的马丁。”他说，“而且我把话放在这儿，莫亚：这姑娘若是清白的，她大可以告你一个私设拘禁。”
“这话什么意思？”弗兰伯勒勋爵厉声质问，“凡是我认定偷了东西的人，我全有权扣押。”

===Original===
“You have no more right to lock a woman in a room,” said the other calmly, “than I have to stand you on your head. But that is beside the point. Lead me to the prisoner.”
The prisoner was very pale and very tearful; a middle-aged woman who felt her position acutely and between sobs and wails made an incoherent protest of her innocence.
“I suppose you have searched everywhere?” asked Michael, turning to the girl.
“Everywhere,” she replied emphatically. “I have had every box and every corner of the room examined.”
“Suppose the string of the pearls broke, would they all fall off?”

===Chinese===
“你把一个女人锁在屋里，”对方心平气和地说，“和我把你倒立起来一样，都谈不上有权没权。不过这话扯远了。带我去见见这位囚徒吧。”
囚犯脸色惨白，泪眼汪汪；一个中年妇人，把自己的处境看得天大，抽抽噎噎、哭哭啼啼地申辩着自己如何清白，语无伦次。
“想来你们各处都搜过了？”迈克尔转向姑娘问。
“各处都搜了。”她斩钉截铁地回答，“每只箱子、每个角落我都叫人查过了。”
“假如珠链的线断了，珠子会全撒下来吗？”
===Original===
“No, they would still remain on, because each pearl was secured. Father gave them to me as a birthday present and he was very particular on that point.”
“I would like to bet,” said Michael suddenly, “that those pearls are not out of this room. Show me your wardrobe.”
The girl’s wardrobe occupied the whole of one wall of her dressing-room, and the tearful Martin opened the rosewood doors for his inspection.
“This is your fur cloak, I presume? Did you examine this after the loss?”
“Examine the cloak,” said Lady Moya in surprise, “of course not. What has the cloak to do with the loss? There are no pockets in it.”

===Chinese===
“不会，珠子还都串在线上，因为每颗珠子都是锁死的。这是父亲送我的生日礼物，这一点他顶讲究。”
“我敢打赌，”迈克尔忽然说，“珠子根本没出这个房间。领我看看你的衣橱。”
姑娘的衣橱占了梳妆室整整一面墙，泪眼婆娑的马丁打开花梨木橱门，供他查验。
“这是你的皮斗篷吧？失窃之后查过它没有？”
“查斗篷？”莫亚小姐好生奇怪，“当然没有。斗篷同失窃有什么相干？上面又没有口袋。”

===Original===
“But if I know anything about the fur cloaks that are fashionable this season,” said Michael, wisely, “I should say that there is a possibility that this luxurious garment had a great deal to do with the loss. In fact, my dear Moya,” he said, “your mysterious loss has been duplicated and triplicated this year. In two cases the police were called in, and in the other case the owner had the intelligence to find her lost trinket without assistance.”
He lifted the cloak down very carefully and opened it to show the silk lining and there, caught in one of the long flat hooks, dangled the pearls. The girl uttered an exclamation of delight and slipped them from its fastening.
“Wonderful, isn’t it?” said Michael dryly. “That is what has happened, not three times but half-a-dozen times since these flat hooks have been introduced. You take the cloak off in a bad temper, the hook catches the chain, breaks it, you bundle the cloak in your wardrobe and there you have the beginning of a great jewel mystery.”
“I can’t tell you how delighted I am,” said the girl. “Michael, you’re wonderful!”
Michael did not reply. He turned to the frightened waiting-woman with a kindly smile.

===Chinese===
“不过，今冬时兴的这种皮斗篷，我要是还懂一点的话，”迈克尔老成地说，“我敢说，这件奢侈玩意儿同这桩失窃案大有干系。说实在的，亲爱的莫亚，”他说，“你这笔糊涂账，今年已经是第三起、第四起了。两起报了警，另一起的主人有本事，不用人帮就把失物找着了。”
他小心翼翼地把斗篷取下来，翻开亮出绸里子——那串珠链正挂在一枚长长的扁平衣钩上，滴溜溜地晃。姑娘欢呼一声，把珠链从钩子上捋了下来。
“神了吧？”迈克尔干巴巴地说，“打这种扁钩子问世以来，出这种事的没有三起也有半打了。你一赌气脱斗篷，钩子挂住链子，扯断了，你把斗篷往衣橱里一团——一桩大珠宝疑案就此开了场。”
“你不知道我多高兴。”姑娘说，“迈克尔，你真神了！”
迈克尔没答话。他转向吓坏了的贴身女仆，和蔼地一笑。

===Original===
“I am so sorry you have been worried about this, Mrs. Martin,” he said, “but when people lose very valuable property they are also inclined to lose their very valuable heads. I am sure Lady Moya is sorry and will make you due compensation for any inconvenience you have been put to.”
The girl stared at him resentfully.
“Of course, I am awfully sorry, Martin,” she said, coldly.
“Oh, my lady,” said the woman eagerly, “I am only too pleased that you have recovered your chain. The worry of it has made me quite ill.”
“You can have a week’s holiday,” said Lord Flanborough, magnificently. “I will get you a free railway ticket to Seahampton,” he added.

===Chinese===
“让你为这个担惊受怕，实在过意不去，马丁太太。”他说，“人一丢贵重财物，往往连自己金贵的头脑也一起丢了。莫亚小姐一定过意不去，她一定会为你受的委屈补上应得的赔偿。”
姑娘怨恨地瞪着他。
“自然，我非常抱歉，马丁。”她冷冷地说。
“哎呀，小姐，”妇人忙不迭地说，“您的项链找回来了，我就千欢万喜了。这一急，急得我病了一场。”
“准你一个礼拜假。”弗兰伯勒勋爵豪迈地说，“我再给你弄一张去西汉普顿的免费火车票。”他补了一句。

===Original===
“So you see, Mrs. Martin,” said Michael with that bland air of his which scarcely veiled the sarcasm so irritating to his lordship, “your generous employers will leave no stone unturned to minister to your comfort, regardless of expense. And when you are at Seahampton, Mrs. Martin, (I trust you will not lose the return half of your free ticket) you will be allowed to walk up and down the promenade on equal terms with the aristocracy and breathe the ozone which, ordinarily, is created for your betters. You may sit on the free seats and watch the pageant of life step past you and, reflecting upon the generosity of your betters, you may appreciate the good fortune which brought you into hourly contact with the aristocracy of England. And on Sundays, Mrs. Martin, you may go to church where quite a number of the seats are also free and may even share a hymnbook with a Gracious Person who is so vastly above you in social standing that he will never recognize you again, and there, I trust, you will pray with a new fervence that the deliberations of the House of Lords may receive divine inspiration.”
“Oh, indeed I will, sir,” said Mrs. Martin almost stunned by his eloquence.
He left the woman, overwhelmed, and returned with a very ruffled Lord Flanborough and an indignant Moya to the library.
“What utter nonsense you talk, Michael,” said the girl angrily. “I don’t think it was kind of you to attempt to set my servants against me.”
“Beastly bad taste,” said Lord Flanborough, “and really, Pretherston, you came here as an officer of the law and not as an old acquaintance and I think that you exceed your duties, if you don’t mind my saying so.”

===Chinese===
“所以你瞧，马丁太太，”迈克尔带着他那副温吞吞的神气说——那底下藏着的讥诮，勋爵大人一看就火——“你慷慨的东家为了你的舒坦，是不惜血本、无微不至的。等你到了西汉普顿，马丁太太（但愿你别把免费票的回程联弄丢了），你就可以同贵族老爷太太们平起平坐，在海滨大道上溜达，呼吸那平日专为你上等人制造的臭氧。你可以坐免费的长椅，看人生的游行从你面前走过，一面感念你上等人的慷慨，一面体味你这份好福气——它让你时时刻刻同英国的贵族为伍。礼拜天呢，马丁太太，你还可以上教堂，那儿的座位也有不少是免费的，没准还能同一位尊贵人物共用一本圣歌集——他的社会地位高出你十万八千里，往后是再不会认得你的。到那时，我信托你会以崭新的热忱祈祷，愿上院的各项审议蒙受神启。”
“哦，我一定，先生。”马丁太太说，几乎被他的口才震住了。
他撇下那位晕头转向的妇人，同火冒三丈的弗兰伯勒勋爵和愤愤然的莫亚回到书房。
“你这些鬼话，迈克尔。”姑娘怒道，“想挑拨我的仆人反对我，我看这可不厚道。”
“恶俗不堪。”弗兰伯勒勋爵说，“说真的，普雷瑟斯顿，你今日是以执法官的身份来的，不是以故人的身份——恕我直言，你这是越权了。”

===Original===
“Old acquaintances,” said Michael, picking up his hat and his coat from a chair where he had put them before the interview, “are especially made to be forgotten, a peculiarity of which one is reminded in that Bacchanalian anthem which is sung at all public dinners where sobriety is bad form. I was merely endeavouring to inculcate into the mind of your slave a few moral principles, beneficial to you, and to society.”
“Don’t tell me that,” growled Lord Flanborough, “as though I didn’t recognize your sarcasm.”
“Children and the lower orders never recognize sarcasm,” said Michael with a broad smile.
He held out his hand and somewhat reluctantly his lordship extended his own flabby paw.
“Before I go,” he said, “I suppose I had better take a full account of this case. You haven’t a secretary or anybody to whom you can dictate the circumstances? You see I have to make a report to my cold-blooded superiors.”

===Chinese===
“故人么，”迈克尔说着，从会谈前放帽子和外套的椅子上把它们拾起来，“生来就是专门叫人忘掉的；一切公共宴会必唱的那支酒神颂，提醒的就是这层道理——在那样的场合，头脑清醒反倒失礼。我不过是想往你家奴仆的脑子里灌输几条道德原则，于你、于社会都有益。”
“少来这套。”弗兰伯勒勋爵咆哮道，“当我听不出你的挖苦？”
“孩子和下等人从来听不出挖苦。”迈克尔咧嘴大笑。
他伸出手，勋爵大人才不大情愿地伸出自己那只软塌塌的爪子。
“走之前，”他说，“这案子我最好还是录一份全案笔录。你们不是有位秘书，或者随便什么人，可以由你口述、把案情记下来？你知道，我得向我那些冷血的上司交报告。”

===Original===
Moya had reached the stage where whatever remains there was in her friendship with Michael Pretherston had not only died but had been cremated in the fires of her smothered anger and she was as anxious to see the end of this interview as was her father.
“Perhaps you will ring for Miss Tenby,” she said after a pause.
Her father pressed the bell and the waiting Sibble answered it.
“Send Miss Tenby,” said his lordship.
“And I do hope, Michael,” said the girl severely, “that when Miss Tenby is here you will not make such extravagant comments as you did before Martin.”

===Chinese===
莫亚此刻的心境是：她对迈克尔·普雷瑟斯顿的情谊纵有残存，不但死了，而且已在她强压的怒火里烧成了灰；这场会面赶紧收场，她同她父亲一样求之不得。
“或许可以按铃把滕比小姐叫来。”她顿了一顿说。
父亲按了铃，一直守着的西布尔应声而来。
“去请滕比小姐。”勋爵大人说。
“还有，迈克尔，我真希望，”姑娘严正地说，“滕比小姐来了之后，你别再像方才当着马丁那样大发怪论。”

===Original===
“Miss Tenby,” interposed Lord Flanborough, “will not welcome such talk. She is a young girl with﻿—er﻿—”
“I know, I know,” said Michael solemnly, “she is genteel. She does forty words a minute on the typewriter and goes to church, filling in her odd moments with needlework and accompanying you on the piano.”
“It must be a wonderful thing to be a detective,” said Moya, sarcastically; “as a matter of fact Miss Tenby is one of the fastest typists in the world.”
Michael swung round on her with an odd look on his face.
“Fastest typists in the world,” he repeated with all the humor gone out of his tone; “does she sing?”

===Chinese===
“滕比小姐可不会爱听这种话。”弗兰伯勒勋爵插嘴道，“她是位年轻姑娘，生性——呃——”
“我知道，我知道。”迈克尔一本正经地说，“她是体面人。打字一分钟四十词，礼拜天上教堂，闲来做做针线，再给您弹弹钢琴伴个奏。”
“当侦探真是件美差。”莫亚讥讽道，“告诉你吧，滕比小姐可是全世界最快的打字员之一。”
迈克尔霍地转过身来，脸上神色古怪。
“全世界最快的打字员。”他重复了一遍，话里的笑意一扫而空，“她唱歌吗？”

===Original===
It was the girl’s turn to be astonished.
“Yes, she does, and very beautifully.”
“Does she prefer Italian opera?” he asked.
At this, the girl laughed aloud.
“Somebody has been telling you all about her and you are trying to be mysterious,” she accused.

===Chinese===
这回轮到姑娘惊讶了。
“唱，而且唱得美极了。”
“她更爱意大利歌剧？”他问。
听到这句，姑娘放声大笑。
“有人把她的底细一五一十告诉了你，你就在这儿故弄玄虚。”她指控道。

===Original===
Further conversation was cut short by the arrival of the girl, who walked in, closed the door and came straight to the desk. She stopped dead at sight of Michael. Moya saw the meeting, saw the girl stiffen and her sorrowful eyes fixed upon the detective’s face.
“Why, Kate!” said Michael Pretherston softly. “Well, well, well! and to think that we meet again under such noble auspices.”
Miss Tenby said nothing.
“And what is the great game?” asked Michael, banteringly. “What beautiful impulse brought you to this sheltered home and how is the Colonel and friend Gregori and all those dear boys? By-the-way, the Colonel must be out by now, Kate. What did he get, three years?”
Still Miss Tenby made no reply.

===Chinese===
话没能再说下去：姑娘到了。她走进来，关上门，径直朝书桌走来。一看见迈克尔，她猛地站住。莫亚看在眼里：姑娘浑身一僵，那双哀愁的眼睛死死盯住侦探的脸。
“哎呀，凯特！”迈克尔·普雷瑟斯顿轻声说，“嘿，嘿，嘿！想不到你我竟在这般高贵的福地重逢。”
滕比小姐一言不发。
“这回玩的是哪一手大牌？”迈克尔打趣地问，“是哪阵美丽的善心把你吹进这座深宅大院的？上校可好？格雷戈里先生可好？还有那帮亲爱的孩子都好？顺便说一句，上校如今总该出来了，凯特——他判了几年，三年？”
滕比小姐仍旧不答。

===Original===
“What is the meaning of this?” demanded Lord Flanborough, feeling that the moment had arrived to assert himself. “Do you know this lady?”
“Do I *know* her,” said Michael, ecstatically; “why, I am one of her greatest admirers, aren’t I, Kate?”
The girl’s sad face softened to a smile which showed the regular lines of her white teeth. She spoke and her voice was gentle and appealing.
“It is perfectly true, Lord Flanborough,” she said quietly, “Mr. Pretherston knows me. He also knows that my uncle, Colonel Westhanger, has been mixed up in a very serious scandal which brought him within the reach of the law. It is perfectly true that when I was a little girl I was known as Kate. It is just as true that I am trying now to live down my association with lawbreakers and am trying to rehabilitate myself in the world.”
“H’m,” murmured Lord Flanborough, a little taken back, “very creditable.”

===Chinese===
“这是怎么回事？”弗兰伯勒勋爵喝问，自觉立威的时刻到了，“你认识这位小姐？”
“我何止认识她。”迈克尔心醉神迷地说，“我还是她最忠实的崇拜者之一呢，对不对，凯特？”
姑娘哀戚的脸化开一个微笑，露出一口齐整的白牙。她开口了，嗓音温柔动人。
“千真万确，弗兰伯勒勋爵。”她平静地说，“普雷瑟斯顿先生认识我。他也知道，我的叔叔韦斯特汉杰上校曾牵进一桩严重的丑闻，险些落到法网里去。我小时候确实叫凯特，这也不假。如今我正努力洗掉同违法之徒的那段瓜葛，努力在这世上重新做个正派人，这同样不假。”
“唔。”弗兰伯勒勋爵有点吃惊，喃喃道，“难能可贵。”

===Original===
Moya turned to Michael indignantly.
“I suppose that you think you are rendering a great service to the world in trying to drag this poor girl down to the gutter, in exposing her to her employers and in obtaining her dismissal from honest employment.”
“I do,” said Michael shamelessly.
“I think it is a barbarous thing to do!” said Moya angrily.
She had not yet decided in her own mind as to what steps she would take in face of this revelation. In view of her own character, it is possible that “Miss Tenby” would have a very short shift at her hands. But for the moment the opportunity for the display of benevolence and Christian charity was not to be passed over. She saw the girl’s appealing eyes and clasped hands and, for a moment, she felt a sincere thrill of pity for a brave sister struggling to escape the octopus tentacles of law and crime; for a moment she felt a genuinely unselfish desire to help another.

===Chinese===
莫亚愤愤地转向迈克尔。
“我想你一定觉得，把这么个可怜的姑娘往泥沟里拖，把她的老底抖给东家，让她丢掉正当的饭碗，是替全世界立了一大功吧。”
“正是。”迈克尔厚颜无耻地说。
“我看这事野蛮得很！”莫亚怒道。
她心里还没打定主意，面对这番揭底该如何处置。以她的性子论，“滕比小姐”落在她手里，饭碗多半朝不保夕。不过眼下，这个展示宽仁与基督慈爱的大好机会，她可不肯错过。她看见姑娘那双哀恳的眼睛和紧握的双手，一时间，当真为一位挣扎着要逃出法律与犯罪那张章鱼网的姐妹感到一阵怜悯；一时间，当真生出一念无私的诚意，想拉别人一把。

===Original===
If she expected Inspector the Hon. Michael Pretherston﻿—for such was his incongruous title﻿—to wilt under her reproaches, she was disappointed. Michael had not taken his eyes from the secretary, nor had the twinkle in those eyes abated. He nodded to “Miss Tenby.”
“Kate,” he said, “you are really a wonder, and to think that you have never yet come into the clutches of the law until now.”
“Until now,” said the girl quickly, raising her voice.
He nodded.
“The Prevention of Crimes Act,” murmured Michael. “I *can* take you,”﻿—he emphasized the “can”﻿—“on a charge of obtaining employment with forged letters of recommendation, also with being a Suspected Person.”

===Chinese===
她若是指望“尊敬的”迈克尔·普雷瑟斯顿探长——他这头衔本就滑稽地不伦不类——在她的责备下败下阵来，那她要失望了。迈克尔的眼睛一直没有离开那位秘书，眼里的火花也丝毫未减。他朝“滕比小姐”点了点头。
“凯特，”他说，“你真是个奇才。想想看，直到今天，你竟然一次也没落进过法律的爪子。”
“直到今天。”姑娘立刻提高了嗓音。
他点点头。
“《犯罪预防法》。”迈克尔低声道，“我*能*拘你。”——他把“能”字咬得很重——“罪名是用伪造的荐书骗取职位，外加身为涉嫌人员。”

===Original===
The girl dropped her attitude of humility, threw back her head and laughed, showing her even white teeth.
“Oh, you Mike!” she railed him. “Oh, you busy fellow!”
Her amusement did not last long for instantly her face was set again and the grey eyes blazed with rage.
“One of these days you will be too clever,” she said bitterly. “I have seen better men than you and cleverer men than you go out, Michael Pretherston. You and your Prevention of Crimes Act! You can’t put that bluff over me. The Act does not come into operation until you have a conviction against my name, and that you will never get, you brute!”
“Kate, Kate!” murmured Michael. “There’s a lady present.”

===Chinese===
姑娘卸下那副低眉顺眼的架子，仰头大笑，露出一口匀净的白牙。
“哦，你个迈克！”她笑骂他，“哦，你个大忙人！”
她的笑没有维持多久：脸霎时又绷紧了，灰眼睛里怒火直冒。
“早晚有一天，你要聪明过头。”她狠狠地说，“比你强、比你精的人是怎么栽的，我都见过，迈克尔·普雷瑟斯顿。还有你那部《犯罪预防法》！你吓唬不倒我。没有定在我名下的罪名，那部法就落不到我头上；这一条你永远办不到，你这畜生！”
“凯特，凯特！”迈克尔低声道，“这儿还有位小姐呢。”

===Original===
She nodded.
“I guess I’ll get my kit together,” she said; “it hasn’t been exactly a holiday trip.”
“My sympathies are entirely with you,” said Michael; “it must have been awfully dull after the gay orgies of Crime Street.”
“There is one thing I have always wanted to know,” said the girl, pinching her lip thoughtfully.
She walked to the desk, and Lord Flanborough was too much taken back to arrest her progress. Without a word she opened the silver box on the table and took out a cigarette.

===Chinese===
她点点头。
“我看我还是收拾行李吧。”她说，“这一趟本来也不算什么度假。”
“我十二万分同情你。”迈克尔说，“在犯罪街的狂欢盛宴之后，这儿一定闷得要命。”
“有件事我一直想知道。”姑娘说着，若有所思地捏住嘴唇。
她朝书桌走去，弗兰伯勒勋爵惊得忘了拦她。她一言不发，打开桌上的银烟盒，取出一支烟。

===Original===
“I have always wanted to know what kind of dope this dear old gentleman smoked.”
She looked at the cigarette critically and with an exclamation of disgust threw it back on the desk.
“Gold Flavours!” she said scornfully; “can you beat it, Mike? And he has a hundred thousand a year!”
“You must make allowances for the decadence of the governing classes,” said the soothing Michael.
He turned and nodded farewell to the girl and with Miss Tenby’s arm in his he passed out of the room, and Lord Flanborough and his daughter looked at one another in speechless amazement.

===Chinese===
“我一直想知道，这位可爱的老先生抽的是哪一路烟。”
她把那支烟端详了一遍，厌恶地叫了一声，把它丢回桌上。
“‘金味’牌！”她轻蔑地说，“你见过这种事没有，迈克？他一年可有十万镑进账！”
“统治阶级的没落嘛，得多多包涵。”迈克尔宽慰道。
他转身朝姑娘点头作别，挽起滕比小姐的手臂走出了房间；弗兰伯勒勋爵父女面面相觑，惊讶得说不出话来。

## Endnotes / 尾注

===Original===
1. See *Rex v. Tolmini* (Notts. Assizes). This was evidently the big mail robbery which failed, owing to the precipitancy of one of the criminals. —﻿Editor ↩︎

===Chinese===
1. 参见《雷克斯诉托尔米尼案》（Rex v. Tolmini，诺丁汉郡巡回法庭）。这显然是那桩因一名案犯操之过急而失败的大宗邮件劫案。——编者 ↩︎

## VI Kate Came to the Flat / 六 凯特来到公寓

===Original===
> At 9:40 on the night of the 15th instant I was present at Sebo’s Club. The room was full of diners and amongst them was Mr. Reginald Boltover and a girl giving the name of Miss Vera Flemming, who was in reality Kate Westhanger. At 9:52 an Italian named Emil Tolmini, employed as a waiter at Sebo’s Club, attempted to stab Kate Westhanger but was prevented and taken into custody. In the course of the struggle in which he was disarmed he sustained a slight scalp wound and permission was given for him to be taken to the kitchen to have the wound dressed. I regret to state that he succeeded in making his escape. He is a convict on license (record No. P.C.A./C.C.C. 85943). He is an old associate of the Crime Street gang and was obviously attempting to avenge himself upon the girl for some injury, real or imaginary, which he had suffered.
> I made no attempt to warn Mr. Boltover as to the character of his companion, but subsequently calling at his flat in Piccadilly on the pretence that I wished to get information about the attempted murder, I discovered that he had been introduced to the girl at a theatre where she was posing as a chorus girl. She had evidently laid a deep plan to meet him, for what reason it is not clear. He is a very wealthy man and it may be necessary at a later stage to warn him, but at present I have taken upon myself the responsibility of refraining from that act.
Michael Pretherston ended off the report with his neat signature, folded it and inserted it into an official envelope which he addressed to his chief. By good fortune he met that brilliant man coming into Scotland House as Michael was going out.

===Chinese===
> 本月 15 日晚 9 点 40 分，本人在塞博俱乐部。室内宾客满座，其中有雷金纳德·博尔托弗先生和一位自称维拉·弗莱明小姐的姑娘——她实为凯特·韦斯特汉杰。9 点 52 分，一名叫埃米尔·托尔米尼的意大利人，系塞博俱乐部侍者，企图行刺凯特·韦斯特汉杰，未遂被捕。在缴其凶器的搏斗中，他头部受了轻伤，经准许被带往厨房包扎。本人遗憾地陈述：他已逃脱。此人是假释中的罪犯（档案号 P.C.A./C.C.C. 85943），系犯罪街团伙的旧相识，显然是因某种或真或幻的宿怨，企图向该姑娘泄愤报复。
> 本人未尝试就同伴的底细向博尔托弗先生提出警告。随后本人以了解这宗谋杀未遂案为由，到他在皮卡迪利的公寓拜访，得知他是在一家剧院结识该姑娘的，当时她正扮作歌舞队女孩。她显然为结识他布下了深远的计划，目的何在尚不清楚。他极为富有，日后也许有必要向他提出警告，但目前本人自行担起了暂不此举的责任。
迈克尔·普雷瑟斯顿以他端正的签名结束报告，折好，装进一只公函信封，写上呈交长官的地址。也是凑巧，他出门时正碰见那位杰出人物走进苏格兰场。

===Original===
“I think you did right,” said T. B., after he had heard the story; “I wonder what her game is? I have a good mind to detail a man to take the whole case up.”
“Let me do it,” said Michael, eagerly.
T. B. Smith pursed his lips.
“You are rather a big man for a job like that, Michael,” he said, “it may turn out to be nothing more than a common or garden chorus girl’s romance.”
“Kate isn’t the chorus girl type,” said Michael, “if it is big enough for her to be in it, it is quite big enough for me.”

===Chinese===
“我认为你做得对。”T. B. 听完事情经过后说，“我纳闷她葫芦里卖的什么药？我很想派个人把这案子整个接过去。”
“让我来吧。”迈克尔急切地说。
T. B. 史密斯抿起了嘴唇。
“干这种差事，你这尊神未免太大了，迈克尔，”他说，“说不定到头来不过是歌舞队姑娘的一段寻常韵事。”
“凯特不是歌舞队姑娘那路人，”迈克尔说，“这案子够她下场，就足够我接手。”

===Original===
The chief thought for a moment.
“Very well then,” he said at length, “you can take on the job. Do it by yourself if you possibly can, I haven’t any men to spare. But keep in touch with me. Blowing a whistle won’t be of any service to you if these people mean business and get after you.”
He hesitated again.
“Confound Kate!” he said. “I suppose you have circulated a description of the ice-cream merchant?”
All Latin criminals came under this generic description with T. B.

===Chinese===
长官想了一会儿。
“那好吧，”他终于说，“这差事归你了。能单干就尽量单干，我抽不出人手。但要随时跟我联络。这帮人要是动真格的、盯上了你，吹哨子可救不了你。”
他又迟疑了一下。
“凯特这家伙真折腾人！”他说，“那个卖冰激凌的，通缉描述你已经发了通稿吧？”
“在 T. B. 那里，凡是拉丁裔罪犯一概归在这个统称之下。”

===Original===
Michael nodded.
“Well, good luck,” said the chief, “but be careful!”
When the young man had gone T. B. beckoned to an officer who was passing.
“You’re the very man, Barr,” he said; “pick up Mr. Pretherston and don’t lose him﻿—you may choose your own opposite number.”
The sergeant saluted and hurried out after his charge.

===Chinese===
迈克尔点点头。
“那么，祝你好运，”长官说，“不过要当心！”
年轻人走后，T. B. 向一名路过的警官招了招手。
“你正是合适的人选，巴尔，”他说，“盯住普雷瑟斯顿先生，别把他跟丢了——搭班的搭档你自己挑。”
警长敬了个礼，匆匆出门追他盯的对象去了。

===Original===
Michael went back to his rooms with a light heart. It was the kind of job that he liked better than any other. He had not told the chief all his suspicions. Kate’s game was a big one. High-flyer as she was, she was out for a height record﻿—that he realised. There was some association between her month with Lord Flanborough and the careful cultivation of Reggie Boltover’s acquaintance. When he came to think of it she must have met Boltover while she was still with Flanborough. He had taken it for granted that the girl was a resident secretary but possibly he had arrived at this conclusion in error. So it proved next morning when he called Lord Flanborough’s house on the telephone and had a private conversation with the butler. The young lady, during the time she had been at Felton House, had left every afternoon at four o’clock.
A little talk with the stage manager at the theatre showed that the girl had never attended any of the morning rehearsals and had missed one of the matinées. Michael saw this part of the scheme plainly enough. Kate, through her spies, had discovered that Boltover had an acquaintance who had a friend at the theatre. She had come to the stage with no other object than making a friend of the girl who all unwittingly was the instrument by which she was to meet Reggie.
The detective knew that this was no chance acquaintance. He followed the manoeuvres of Kate through all their devious paths. He took the opportunity in the afternoon to call upon Reggie at his office which was something between a board room and a boudoir.
Reggie’s theoretical interests were multifarious. He was the nominal head of a dozen different corporations which his industrious father had created for his profit. In practice he knew very little about any of them and nothing about some.

===Chinese===
迈克尔心情轻快地回了寓所。这是他最爱干的那种差事。他没把全部疑心都告诉长官。凯特要玩的是一大局。她本就是个高飞的人，这回更是要破高度纪录——这一点他明白。她在弗兰伯勒勋爵家的那一个月，与小心经营同雷吉·博尔托弗的交情之间，必有某种关联。细想起来，她一定还在弗兰伯勒家时就已结识了博尔托弗。他原想当然地以为姑娘是位住家秘书，如今看来这结论也许下错了。第二天早晨他打电话到弗兰伯勒勋爵府上，跟管家私下谈了谈，果然如此。那位小姐住在费尔顿宅邸期间，每天下午四点必定外出。
跟剧院的舞台监督聊了几句，便知那姑娘从没参加过一次晨间排练，还缺席过一场日场。这一环计谋迈克尔看得清清楚楚。凯特通过她的眼线查明：博尔托弗有个熟人，那熟人又在剧院里有位朋友。她登上舞台的目的只有一个——结交那位姑娘；而那姑娘全然不觉，自己正是引她与雷吉相识的工具。
侦探知道这绝非萍水相逢。他循着凯特的种种迂回布局一路追索。下午他寻了个机会，到雷吉的办公室去拜访——那地方介于董事会议室与闺房之间。
雷吉名下的利益五花八门。他是十几家不同公司的挂名首脑，那些公司都是他勤勉的父亲为让他坐享其利而创办的。实际上，这些公司他多半知之甚少，有几家干脆一无所知。

===Original===
“I hope your lady was not alarmed,” said Michael, with spurious anxiety.
“Oh, no, the lady was not alarmed; oh, no,” said Reggie, shaking his head violently. “Oh, dear no. She was not alarmed. Of course, it would have been different if she had been alone, but being with me, naturally she﻿—er she﻿—er was not alone.”
“Naturally,” agreed Michael.
“No, she was not alarmed,” said Mr. Boltover, “in fact, she was very cool, remarkably cool. I have never seen anybody so cool.”
“I hope when you see her again,” said Michael, “you will tell her I asked.”

===Chinese===
“但愿您那位女士没受惊。”迈克尔说着，一脸假兮兮的关切。
“哦，没有，那位女士没有受惊；哦，没有。”雷吉使劲摇头，“哦，当然没有。她没受惊。当然啦，她要是独自一人，那又当别论，可她跟我在一起，自然她——呃她——呃不是独自一人。”
“自然。”迈克尔附和。
“不，她没受惊，”博尔托弗先生说，“其实，她镇定得很，镇定得出奇。我从没见过这么镇定的人。”
“希望您再见到她时，”迈克尔说，“替我问一声。”

===Original===
“Certainly,” said Mr. Boltover heartily; “certainly I shall tell her you asked.” And he added after a moment, “When I meet her again.”
“She seemed, if you will forgive the impertinence, so interested in everything,” encouraged Michael.
“You are quite right,” said Reggie eagerly, “you are perfectly right. That just describes her. She is interested in everything.”
“It is nice to meet people who are interested in one’s business,” Michael went on artlessly. “I never mind people being interested in my business, do you?”
“Oh, dear no,” replied Mr. Boltover in alarm, as though the very thought that anybody should be discouraged from an interest in his affairs, caused him acute mental unhappiness; “oh, dear no. Certainly not. Not at all.”

===Chinese===
“一定，”博尔托弗先生热诚地说，“我一定告诉她您问起了。”过了一会儿他又补一句，“等我再见到她的时候。”
“恕我冒昧，她好像对什么都那么感兴趣。”迈克尔引逗着说。
“您说得太对了，”雷吉急切地说，“说得千真万确。这话正好形容她。她对什么都感兴趣。”
“遇到对自己的生意感兴趣的人，真是件乐事。”迈克尔一副天真相地继续说，“别人关心我的行当，我从不介意，您呢？”
“哦，那哪里话，”博尔托弗先生惶恐地答道，仿佛光是想到有人被劝阻、不去关心他的事务，就令他痛心不已，“哦，哪里话。当然不。一点儿也不。”

===Original===
“Of course,” smiled Michael, “she could not very well understand all the complexities of your business, Mr. Boltover﻿—it is such an enormous one.”
“Well,” hesitated the other, “I don’t know. I am not so sure. She is a very intelligent young lady. I was talking to her about my business when this dreadful affair happened and she was so calm that she just went on talking about it, don’t you know. My business, I mean. I thought it was a most remarkable instance of coolness. I was telling one of our directors today about it, and he thought it was a remarkable instance of coolness. Yes, even when I was taking her home she told me a lot about herself and﻿—things. Her grandfather is a very wealthy man, a financier. I didn’t know that.”
Michael might have said that he too was unaware of the fact, but he knew just the moment when a tactless interpolation might dry up the fount of Mr. Boltover’s eloquence.
“Very intelligent lady indeed,” wandered Mr. Boltover, “oh, yes, I was talking about her grandfather﻿—he is a very rich man. She thought that he might be able to take one of our properties off our hands. I was awfully surprised. Naturally, I did not think she had any money being in the chorus and all that﻿—I hope I haven’t been indiscreet?” he asked anxiously. “You possibly did not know that she was on the stage.”
“Oh, yes, I did,” said Michael with a smile; “you have betrayed nothing, Mr. Boltover.”

===Chinese===
“当然啦，”迈克尔微笑道，“您生意里那些盘根错节，她未必能全弄明白，博尔托弗先生——它可是庞大得很呐。”
“呃，”对方迟疑着，“这很难说。我不敢肯定。她是位非常聪明的年轻女士。那桩可怕的事情发生时，我正跟她谈我的生意，她镇定得很，照样谈下去，您知道吧。我是说我的生意。我当时想，这真是镇定的绝好例证。今天我还跟我们一位董事讲了这事，他也觉得是镇定的绝好例证。是啊，连我送她回家的路上，她还跟我讲了许多她自己的事，还有——种种事情。她外祖父是位非常有钱的人，一位金融家。这个我原先可不知道。”
迈克尔本可以说自己也不知道这一事实，但他深谙何时一句不得体的插话，会把博尔托弗先生雄辩的泉眼堵死。
“确实是非常聪明的女士。”博尔托弗先生信马由缰地说下去，“哦，对了，我刚说到她外祖父——他是位大富翁。她想他也许能把我们的一处产业从我们手里接过去。我吃惊极了。自然，我以为她既然在歌舞队里混，诸如此类，该是没有钱的——我没说漏嘴吧？”他担心地问，“您原先大概不知道她在舞台上吧。”
“哦，不，我知道。”迈克尔微笑着说，“您什么也没泄露，博尔托弗先生。”

===Original===
“I am awfully glad,” replied the other, relieved; “what was I saying, about her grandfather, yes. I think I might sell him that property. I hate parting with properties﻿—we have refused quite a number of good offers﻿—sheer sentiment, don’t you know?”
“But perhaps this is not a paying property.”
“Oh, no, not at all,” said Mr. Boltover; “by no manner of means whatever. Still we don’t like parting with them. Of course, I talk a lot of rot about people wanting to buy the works and I always tell that great joke about a lunatic﻿—ha, ha﻿—but really it isn’t true. No, not really true, oh, no.”
Michael had never heard the great joke about the lunatic. What he was anxious to hear were details of Kate’s projected purchase but in this he was foiled. There was precious little of the business man about Mr. Reggie Boltover but one lesson he had learnt, and learnt thoroughly, and that was the art of silence. His revered father was wont to say, “If you never open your mouth, Reggie, nobody will know what an ass you are,” and in business, at any rate, Reggie most religiously lived up to this injunction.

===Chinese===
“那就太好了。”对方如释重负地答道，“我刚才说到哪儿了？哦，对她外祖父，对了。我想我也许该把那处产业卖给他。我舍不得出手产业——好几份像样的报价我们都回绝了——纯粹是出于感情，您知道吧？”
“可这处产业也许并不赚钱。”
“哦，不，根本不赚，”博尔托弗先生说，“无论如何都不赚。可我们还是不愿出手。当然，关于有人想买下整个厂子的话，我说过一大堆浑话，我还老讲那个疯子的绝妙笑话——哈，哈——其实那不是真的。不，不是真的，哦，不是。”
那个关于疯子的绝妙笑话，迈克尔从未听过。他急于听到的是凯特那笔拟议中收购的详情，但这一手落了空。雷吉·博尔托弗先生身上商人的气派少得可怜，可有一课他学到了家，那就是沉默的艺术。他那可敬的父亲常说：“雷吉，只要你不开口，就没有人知道你是个蠢驴。”至少在生意场上，雷吉对这条训诫奉行得虔诚无比。

===Original===
What was the girl’s object?
Michael was puzzled. Strangely enough the obvious never occurred to him, or if it did he dismissed it without a second consideration. He did not look upon Kate as the type that would find any amusement, whatever the profit might be, in the inveigling of a young fool to the altar. Kate wanted the excitement, not the money. That was her history. He had first met her when he was in the Special Department and it had been over a little matter of a King’s messenger’s despatch bag which on a cross-channel journey had mysteriously disappeared, though it was practically handcuffed to the owner’s wrist, that he had first become acquainted with the girl. He was interested in her, but only mildly so, because, at the time, he arrived at a somewhat hasty judgment. It was later, when the strongroom of the *Muranic* was forced and twenty-five packets of diamonds vanished in mid-ocean and when he had been in charge of the investigations which had resulted in the imprisonment of Colonel Westhanger, that he had first formed a true estimate of the girl’s character﻿—an estimate which he had had cause to modify, but never to change.
Michael lived in a big block of flats near Baker Street, where he maintained a somewhat elaborate establishment for an inspector of police. He had, however, a private income of his own which he had inherited from his maternal grandmother and as he was a man of simple tastes and very few extravagant needs, he was able to live very comfortably indeed. He reached his home a little before eight o’clock and was astonished as he came through the lobby of the flat to meet Beston, his manservant, clad in fine raiment and going forth.

===Chinese===
姑娘图的是什么？
迈克尔困惑了。说也奇怪，那个最显眼的答案从没在他脑子里出现过；即便闪过，也被他不加思索地挥走了。他不认为凯特是那种会把诱骗一个年轻傻瓜上圣坛当作乐趣的女人——不管能捞多少好处。凯特要的是刺激，不是钱。她一贯如此。他初识她时还在特别科，起因是国王信使的公文包在一趟渡海旅程中神秘失踪的小小案件——那包几乎等于铐在主人手腕上。当时他对这姑娘只有一点淡淡的好奇，因为那时他下结论下得仓促了些。直到后来，“穆拉尼克”号的保险库被撬，二十五包钻石在汪洋大海中央不翼而飞，又由他主持调查、致使韦斯特汉杰上校锒铛入狱，他才对这姑娘的品性形成真正的判断——这判断后来有过修正的缘由，却从未动摇更改。
迈克尔住在贝克街附近一座大公寓楼里，以一名警探的身份而言，他的家什排场略显讲究。不过他有一笔外祖母留下的私人进项，加之口味简单、极少奢侈的需求，日子过得着实舒服。快到八点时他到家，穿过公寓门厅，却吃了一惊：他的男仆贝斯顿一身盛装，正要出门。

===Original===
“Hello, Beston, where are you off to?” he asked in surprise.
The man touched his hat cheerfully.
“I am going to the theatre, sir, and thank you very much for the tickets,” he said. “Cook went ten minutes ago and I stayed behind to tidy things up.”
“Oh, cook went ten minutes ago, did she?” said Michael. “That’s good. When did the tickets arrive?”
“About an hour ago, sir, by a district messenger. It was very kind of you to wire to us that you were sending them.”

===Chinese===
“喂，贝斯顿，你这是上哪儿去？”他诧异地问。
男仆兴冲冲地碰了碰帽檐。
“我去看戏，先生，多谢您给的票，”他说，“厨娘十分钟前走了，我留下收拾妥当再出门。”
“哦，厨娘十分钟前走的，是吗？”迈克尔说，“很好。票是什么时候送到的？”
“约莫一个钟头前，先生，区里派来的信差送的。您还特意来电报说给我们送票，真是太周到了。”

===Original===
Michael laughed softly.
“Your surprise at my consideration hurts me, Beston,” he said. “I always do things like that. By the way, did they spell your name correctly in the telegram?”
“I think so, sir,” said the man in surprise, fumbled in his pocket and produced the orange slip.
> “I am sending you two tickets for the theatre tonight. May not be home until tomorrow. Pretherston.”

===Chinese===
迈克尔轻轻笑了。
“你对我的周到这么吃惊，可伤了我的心，贝斯顿，”他说，“我一向是这样的。对了，电报上你的名字拼对了吗？”
“应该没错，先生。”男仆一脸意外，摸摸口袋，掏出那张橙色电报纸。
> “今夜送上戏票两张。明日前未必归家。普雷瑟斯顿。”

===Original===
Thus read the wire, which had been handed in at the Strand Office.
Beston sensed some difficulty.
“I hope it’s all right, sir,” he asked anxiously.
“Quite all right,” replied Michael with a cheerful nod. “Don’t wait for me now, I shall not be in very long.”

===Chinese===
电文如此，是在河岸街电报局拍发的。
贝斯顿觉出有些不对。
“但愿没出岔子吧，先生。”他不安地问。
“一点岔子也没有。”迈克尔回答，愉快地点点头，“别等门了，我在家待不久。”

===Original===
He mounted the carpeted stairs, opened the door of his flat and closed it carefully behind him. He went straight to his study, pulled down the blinds and drew the thick curtains across the windows, then he turned on the light, took up the telephone and gave a Treasury number.
“Is that Sergeant Pears?” he asked. “Is there a telegram waiting at the Yard for me?”
“Yes, sir,” said the sergeant’s voice.
Michael winked at the wall.
“Do you mind opening and reading it?”

===Chinese===
他登上铺着地毯的楼梯，打开公寓的门，小心地关上。他径直进了书房，放下百叶帘，拉上厚窗帘，然后开灯，拿起电话，拨了一个财政部的号码。
“是皮尔斯警长吗？”他问，“警察厅有我的电报在等吗？”
“有的，先生。”警长的声音说。
迈克尔冲墙壁眨了眨眼。
“麻烦你拆开念念？”

===Original===
There was a little pause and then the sergeant repeated into the receiver:
> “To Inspector Michael Pretherston, Scotland House. Come up by the earliest train. Am staying at Adelphi. T. B.”
“Handed in at Manchester, I suppose?”
“Yes, sir,” said the sergeant, “at three-fifteen.”
“Is the chief in Manchester?”

===Chinese===
稍停了片刻，警长对着话筒念道：
> “致苏格兰场迈克尔·普雷瑟斯顿探长：乘最早班车来。现下榻阿德尔菲。T. B.”
“是在曼彻斯特拍发的吧？”
“是，先生，”警长说，“三点一刻。”
“长官在曼彻斯特？”

===Original===
“Yes, sir; he went by the morning train.”
“Excellent,” said Michael, “thank you very much, sergeant.”
He hung up the receiver.
This was Kate’s work﻿—the beautiful detail of it, the knowledge she possessed of T. B. Smith’s movement. She had probably sent a man up on the same train with the chief and had given him the telegram in advance, with exact instructions as to the minute it was to be handed in. Yes, it was Kate. Yet (he became uncomfortable at the thought) it was not like her to leave things to chance. How came she to miss him at the Yard? He returned to the telephone and again called up his assistant.
“What time did the telegram arrive?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“是，先生；他坐早班车去的。”
“好极了，”迈克尔说，“多谢，警长。”
他挂上听筒。
这是凯特的手笔——细节漂亮，对 T. B. 史密斯的行踪了如指掌。她多半派了个人与长官同车北上，事先把电报交给他，连哪一分钟递发都交代得一丝不差。没错，是凯特。然而（想到这里他有些不安）把事情交给运气，可不像她的作风。她怎么会在警察厅扑了他的空？他回到电话跟前，又拨了他的助手。
“电报几点到的？”他问。

===Original===
The sergeant’s voice was apologetic.
“I am very sorry, sir, I am afraid it arrived while you were here, this afternoon. It was given to a messenger to take in to you and in some extraordinary way the constable forgot it. I have reprimanded him.”
“That’s all right,” said Michael, relieved.
His relief, curiously founded, he might have found it difficult to explain. It was the relief which the matador feels when he sees the bull, which steps so proudly into the ring, will put up a good fight. It was the relief of the huntsman when a strong fox breaks from covert. He wanted Kate and that extraordinary organization, which he had set himself to conquer, to be at its best that his victory might be the more satisfactory.

===Chinese===
警长的声音满是歉意。
“非常抱歉，先生，恐怕今天下午您还在厅里时它就到了。电报交了信差给您送去，不知怎么搞的，那位警员把这事忘了。我已经训过他了。”
“没关系。”迈克尔说，松了口气。
这口气松得古怪，他自己也未必说得清缘由。那是斗牛士眼见雄赳赳踏进场中的公牛肯奋力一搏时的欣慰；是猎人眼见一条壮狐破林而出时的快意。他要凯特和那个他立志征服的非同寻常的组织拿出全副本事，这样他的胜利才更够味。

===Original===
He looked at his watch. It was five minutes past eight. He knew that his visitor would give the servants an hour and he must employ that hour profitably. He began to write rapidly on a pad of scribbling paper, tearing off the sheets as fast as he had filled them. He had been working for an hour when he heard a bell tinkle. Someone was at the front door. He switched out the light, walked into the passage (he had already removed his shoes) and listened. Whoever was coming had sent an agent in advance to discover whether the flat was empty. Again the bell rang. Michael made no sign. It rang a third and last time. The detective made his way stealthily to the window and slipped behind the curtains. He had left his study door open, so that he could hear every sound. He had ten minutes to wait before the faint click of the lock told him that the door had been opened. He knew that the visitor would come to the study last, and he proved to be right. Three minutes passed﻿—as near as he could judge﻿—before he caught the flash of a lamp which was directed cautiously to the curtained window. The light passed slowly along the floor until it reached the skirting, travelled round until it found the lower edge of the drawn curtain. Through the slit he had cut in the heavy velvet hangings Michael witnessed the search. Presently the light went out after focusing itself upon the electric switch. There was a click and the room was illuminated.
The girl who stood by the desk was soberly dressed and was apparently in no hurry. She pulled her gloves off slowly, whilst she allowed her eyes to rove over the littered table. Half a dozen sheets of writing attracted her attention and when her gloves were removed she picked the papers up, pulled the big writing chair to the table and sat down to read. She read the notes through carefully and once she smiled. When she had finished she put them down, leaned back in the chair and looked around the room, then,
“Come out, Mike,” she said.

===Chinese===
他看看表。八点五分。他知道来客会给仆人们留出一个钟头，这一个钟头他必须用出成效。他抓过一本便笺纸疾书起来，写满一页撕一页。写了一个钟头，忽听得门铃轻轻一响。有人到了正门外。他熄了灯，走进过道（他早已换下皮鞋），侧耳细听。来人先派了探子来探明公寓是否空无一人。铃又响了一遍。迈克尔不动声色。第三遍，也是最后一遍。侦探蹑手蹑脚摸到窗前，闪进窗帘后面。他有意敞着书房门，好听见每一丝动静。等了十分钟，锁上轻轻一声咔哒，告诉他门开了。他知道来客会最后才到书房，果然不出所料。约莫过了三分钟——他只能约莫——他看见一道手电的闪光，小心翼翼地射向垂着窗帘的窗子。光柱缓缓扫过地板，扫到踢脚线，绕着找，直到寻见拉严的窗帘下沿。透过他事先在厚丝绒帷幔上割开的细缝，迈克尔把这场搜查看在眼里。不久，光柱定在电灯开关上，熄了。咔哒一声，满室通明。
立在书桌旁的姑娘衣着素淡，看样子毫不着急。她慢慢褪着手套，一面让目光在凌乱的桌面上逡巡。五六页手稿引住了她的视线，手套一脱完，她便拿起那几页纸，把大写字椅拖到桌前，坐下读了下去。她仔细读完全部笔记，中间笑了一次。读完，她把纸放下，靠进椅背，环视四周，然后——
“出来吧，迈克。”她说。

===Original===
Michael stepped forth without embarrassment.
“I was nearly deceived,” she said, “with your precious account of the happening at Sebo’s and then I realized that this could not have been written more than five minutes before. You forgot to blot the last sheet and the ink is still damp.”
She rubbed her fingers over to prove the fact.
“Why aren’t you in Manchester?” she asked.
The staggering question nearly took his breath away.

===Chinese===
迈克尔大大方方地走了出来。
“我差点上了当，”她说，“你那篇塞博俱乐部事件的宝贝报告——后来我想明白了，这东西至多是五分钟前写的。你忘了吸最后一页的墨，墨迹还潮着呢。”
她用手指蹭了蹭纸面，以证明此言不虚。
“你怎么没在曼彻斯特？”她问。
这问题凌厉之极，险些噎得他喘不上气。

===Original===
“Well, if you aren’t the real Kate!” he said admiringly.
“I’m in your chair I’m afraid,” she said.
“Not a bit.”
He dropped into a deep settee.
“Now tell me all the news. But before we go any farther,” he said with mock concern, “wouldn’t you like a chaperone?”

===Chinese===
“好家伙，你才是如假包换的凯特！”他叹服地说。
“恐怕我坐了你的椅子。”她说。
“不碍事。”
他一屁股陷进深深的长沙发里。
“现在把新闻都讲给我听吧。不过再往下谈之前，”他假装体贴地说，“你不想来位女伴陪着？”
===Original===
“Don’t worry,” she replied, “I have a chaperone.”
“Not in my flat I hope,” he said in a tone of alarm. “You, I can trust, Kate, but the idea of your low thieving friends being up against all my movable goods gives me a little pain.”
She fished in her bag and produced a little gold case. She opened it and took out a cigarette.
“You won’t have one, of course?”
“Not one of yours, Kate,” he said reproachfully. “No, I’ll have one of my own if you don’t mind.”

===Chinese===
“不劳费心，”她答道，“我带着女伴呢。”
“但愿不在我这公寓里，”他语带惊慌地说，“你嘛，我信得过，凯特，可一想到你那些下九流的小偷朋友对着我会动的家当虎视眈眈，我就有点儿心痛。”
她把手伸进手提包摸了摸，掏出一只小金盒，打开，取出一支烟。
“你自然不肯来一支？”
“不抽你的，凯特，”他怪怨地说，“我自己有烟，你不介意吧。”

===Original===
“I think you are very rude,” she said with a lift of her brows.
“It’s better to be rudely awake than politely asleep,” he said meaningly. “When one has to deal with clever criminals one has to take all sorts of precautions.”
She laughed and looked at him curiously.
“I wonder what made you a policeman?”
“Nature,” he said promptly.

===Chinese===
“我看你可真无礼。”她扬了扬眉毛说。
“无礼地醒着，总好过客气地睡着。”他意味深长地说，“跟聪明的罪犯打交道，就得有种种防备。”
她笑了，好奇地打量他。
“真奇怪，是什么让你当了警察？”
“造化。”他应声而答。

===Original===
She was puzzled.
“I don’t quite get your humor,” she said.
“Nature provides all things with some form of protection. It gives the oyster its shell and the tiger its stripes. It gives the squid his ink-sack and the shark his teeth. Nature always produces antidotes. When criminals are stupid they have stupid policemen to deal with them. When criminals are extraordinarily clever, Nature provides the police force with an officer of unusual intelligence. I came to the police in blind obedience to the laws of Nature.”
She laughed softly in his face.
“It’s so nice to be able to discuss things with a man of sensibility,” she said. “Of course, some of my friends are awfully clever and uncle is very philosophical, but then they all take a very one-sided view of things, and I think it’s so much better to hear the other side of every question. You can get two views on all subjects except crime,” she went on. “If you believe in Darwin’s theory you can meet hosts of clever people who bitterly oppose it. If you are a Christian Scientist you can meet hosts of Theosophists. Even if you are a firm believer in monogamy you can generally hire a Mormon to argue on the other side. It is only when we come down to crime that you meet the truly insular view, held by people who know nothing whatever about its finesse, or the genius necessary to break the laws without leaving a big hole to show where you went in and another to show where you came out. That is why I like you, Mike,” she said frankly.

===Chinese===
她被弄糊涂了。
“我没太明白你的幽默。”她说。
“造化给万物都备下某种保护。它给牡蛎壳，给老虎条纹；给乌贼墨囊，给鲨鱼利齿。造化总会造出解药来。罪犯蠢的时候，自有蠢警察去对付；罪犯聪明绝顶的时候，造化就给警界派来一位智力超常的警官。我进警察这一行，不过是盲目服从造化的法则。”
她当着他的面轻轻笑了。
“能跟一个有灵性的男人讨论事情，真好。”她说，“当然，我的朋友里有几个聪明极了，叔叔也很有哲学头脑，可他们看事情都偏在一面，而每个问题都能听听另一面的意见，岂不更好。天下万事都听得到两种论调，唯独犯罪例外，”她接着说，“你要信达尔文的学说，能遇见一大群极力反对它的聪明人；你要是基督科学教徒，能遇见一大群通神论者；哪怕你坚定信奉一夫一妻，也总雇得到一个摩门教徒来替另一方辩护。唯独谈到犯罪，你碰到的才是真正偏狭的见解，持论者对犯罪的精妙一无所知，也不懂那种天才——犯了法却不留下一个大洞标明你从哪儿进、另一个洞标明你从哪儿出。所以我喜欢你，迈克。”她坦白地说。

===Original===
“Any appreciation is very gratifying to me,” said Michael, “but that which is so enthusiastic that it leads my admirer to break into my flat to ravish my secret thoughts, is a little overwhelming.”
“I wanted to know what you were saying about me,” she said, “though I ought to have known that you would not leave things about for me to read﻿—still,” she justified herself, “to do myself justice, I did not expect to find your confidential reports on your desk.”
There was a big safe in one corner of the room.
“I was going to open that.”
She nodded toward the strongbox.

===Chinese===
“任何赏识都令我感激，”迈克尔说，“可赏识热烈到害我的倾慕者破门闯进公寓来掠夺我的秘密思想，就有点消受不起了。”
“我想知道你背后怎么议论我，”她说，“虽然我本该想到，你不会把东西摊在外面给我读——不过，”她给自己辩解，“凭良心说，我没料到会在你书桌上见到机密报告。”
房间一角立着一座大保险柜。
“我本打算把它打开。”
她朝那铁柜点点头。

===Original===
“You saw me the other night,” she turned the conversation suddenly.
“At Sebo’s﻿—yes,” he said, “I saw you.”
“What did you think?” she asked quietly.
“I thought you were with the loquacious Mr. Boltover for a special reason of your own,” he said slowly.
“He *is* an orator﻿—isn’t he?” she agreed﻿—“but he’s quite a nice boy, really. God didn’t give him brains and it’s not fair to make fun of a man’s deficiencies.”
“What did you want of Reggie?” asked Michael.

===Chinese===
“那晚你看见我了。”她猛地转过话头。
“在塞博俱乐部——是的，”他说，“我看见你了。”
“你怎么想？”她平静地问。
“我想你陪着那位健谈的博尔托弗先生，自有你的特别缘故。”他慢吞吞地说。
“他确实是个演说家——可不是嘛，”她表示同意，“不过他真是个挺不错的男孩。上帝没给他脑子，拿人家的短处取笑，未免不公平。”
“你找雷吉要什么？”迈克尔问。

===Original===
“I just wanted to know all about him,” she said, “that kind of people are always interesting to me.”
“What did you want of Reggie?” he asked again.
“How insistent you are!” she laughed.
She got up and began strolling about the room, taking down books from the big bookshelf and examining their titles.
“What catholic tastes you have, Mike﻿—and Tennyson, too. How depraved!”

===Chinese===
“我只是想摸清他的底细，”她说，“这类人总让我觉得有趣。”
“你找雷吉要什么？”他又问。
“你可真爱刨根问底！”她笑了。
她起身在屋里踱起步来，从大书架上抽下书本，翻看书名。
“你的趣味真广泛，迈克——居然还有丁尼生。堕落透顶！”

===Original===
“You will find a Browning somewhere,” he said carelessly.
“That’s more encouraging,” she smiled. “It’s an awfully comfortable room. Quite like the room I thought you would have.”
She looked at a book plate on the cover of one volume.
“You were at Winchester, I see. So was uncle.”
“The poison and the antidote!”

===Chinese===
“什么地方你还能翻出一本勃朗宁。”他随口说。
“这才叫人放心。”她笑了，“这屋子舒服极了。跟我料想中你的屋子一个样。”
她看了看其中一册封面上的藏书票。
“你上过温彻斯特公学，我看出来了。叔叔也是。”
“毒药与解药！”

===Original===
“You are not fair with uncle. He’s a mental degenerate, too. Crime is a disease with him.”
“And with you?” said Michael quickly.
“It’s a hobby. It’s a tremendous excitement.”
She put the book down and turned to him.
“You don’t know what it’s like. To work things out and make them happen, to cover a couple of sheets of paper with writing and then see all sorts of things move in obedience to those instructions, to see thousands and tens of thousands of pounds change hands, to know that men are going long journeys, that special trains are being run, that telegraph wires are humming all over the Continent, that a dozen brilliant thief-catchers are working and worrying in a vain attempt to undo all that twenty or thirty lines of writing have done.”

===Chinese===
“你对叔叔不公平。他也是个心智退化者。犯罪于他是一种病。”
“那么于你呢？”迈克尔紧着问。
“于我是嗜好。是了不得的刺激。”
她放下书，转向他。
“你不知道那是什么滋味。把事情推演出来，再让它一一成真；写满两页纸，然后看着万事万物循着那些指令运转；看着成千上万英镑易手；知道有人在长途跋涉，专列在开行，电报线在整个欧洲大陆嗡嗡作响，十几个精明的捕盗高手操碎了心，却徒劳地想撤销那二三十行字干下的一切。”

===Original===
“This will be used in evidence against you,” warned Michael flippantly.
The girl was not posing. Of that he was convinced. Her big grey eyes were brighter, her whole face was alight with the excitement of the thought, her voice had a new thrill. She was exalted, transfigured at the thought of the power which her shrewd brain gave to her.
“What did you want of Reggie?” he asked again.

===Chinese===
“这些话将来都要用作指控你的证据。”迈克尔轻飘飘地警告。
姑娘不是在装腔。这一点他信。她那双灰色大眼睛更亮了，整张脸因这念头而焕发光彩，嗓音里添了新的颤动。一想到那颗精明脑袋赋予自己的力量，她便如痴如醉，容光焕发。
“你找雷吉要什么？”他又问了一遍。

===Original===
The light faded out of her eyes and she was her normal self again.
“Oh, I wanted to pick his pocket,” she said mockingly; “or, no, I know something better﻿—I wanted to marry him. He’s worth two millions.”
“I don’t think you will ever marry for money,” said Michael.
“What makes you say that?” she asked quickly.

===Chinese===
她眼里的光淡了下去，又恢复了平常的样子。
“哦，我想掏他的腰包，”她嘲弄地说，“不对，我有更好的主意——我想嫁给他。他值两百万呢。”
“我不信你会为了钱结婚。”迈克尔说。
“你凭什么这么说？”她急忙问。

===Original===
He shrugged his shoulders.
“That is the estimate I have formed of you. I may be wrong.”
“I shall never marry,” she said with decision. “I’m not of the marrying kind. I hate men in some ways. I hate them so much, that it gives me a real joy to take away the one thing in the world that they really love. You know the Claude Duval tradition﻿—I mean the idealized Claude Duval of tradition, not the sneak-thief valet of actuality﻿—of robbing the rich and never robbing the poor﻿—well, I rob men, and I never rob women.”
“In fact you rob the people who have the money,” said Michael. “That isn’t clever.”

===Chinese===
他耸耸肩。
“那是我为你下的评语。我也许看错了。”
“我永远不结婚，”她斩钉截铁地说，“我不是结婚那类人。我在某些方面恨男人。恨到这般地步，以至于把世上他们唯一真正心爱的东西拿走，能给我实实在在的快乐。你知道克劳德·杜瓦尔的传统吧——我是说传说里理想化了的克劳德·杜瓦尔，不是现实中那个顺手牵马的贴身男仆——劫富而不劫贫——嘿，我劫男人，从不劫女人。”
“说穿了，你劫的是有钱的人，”迈克尔说，“这算不得聪明。”

===Original===
“No, but it sounds awfully good. I’m thinking of including it in the great speech I shall deliver one of these days at the Old Bailey.”
“What did you want from Reggie?” he asked.
“You are almost monotonous,” she laughed. “Well, I wanted information.”
She turned and again he saw that bright light in her eye and that eager look in her face.

===Chinese===
“是算不得，可听起来漂亮极了。我正琢磨着把它收进那篇宏论里——早晚有一天，我会在老贝利发表它的。”
“你到底想从雷吉那儿得到什么？”他问。
“你简直单调得可以。”她笑道，“好吧，我要的是情报。”
她转过身来，他又看见她眼里那簇亮光、脸上那副热切。

===Original===
“I will tell you, Michael Pretherston,” she said, pointing a white finger toward him. “We will play fair. I am going to do a big thing. I am going to make the most wonderful steal that the world has ever known. That is why I found Reggie. That is why I made a martyr of myself and endured the boredom of Lord Flanborough’s society.”
She clapped her hands like a child.
“It’s a big thing, Michael, but it’s full of complications, wonderfully full of strategy, and I am going to do it all with your assistance.”
He jumped up and flung out his hand.

===Chinese===
“我告诉你，迈克尔·普雷瑟斯顿，”她伸出一根白皙的手指点着他说，“咱们公平较量。我要干一件大事。我要来一桩举世未见的绝妙大窃案。所以我结识别雷吉。所以我甘当殉道者，忍受了弗兰伯勒勋爵社交的乏味。”
她孩子似的拍起手来。
“这是件大事，迈克尔，处处是曲折，妙不可言的全是谋略，而我，要借你的鼎力相助来把它办成。”
他跳起来，猛地伸出手。

===Original===
“Put it there, Kate,” he said.
“This is going to be the big thing for both of us and I am going to be the victor. If you win you have whatever you’re after. If I win, you have me,” she said with a little laugh.
He looked at her in silence.
“I can almost see you gripping my arm and pushing me into the steel pen,” she said. “I can see you sitting in court in a brown﻿—no, a blue﻿—overcoat, with your hat nicely balanced on your knees, looking up at me in the dock and wondering how I am going to take it.”

===Chinese===
“来握个手，凯特。”他说。
“这将是咱们俩共同的大事，而胜者会是我。你赢了，你要什么有什么。我赢了，你就得到我。”她轻笑着说。
他默默望着她。
“我几乎能看见你攥着我的胳膊，把我推进铁栅栏里了，”她说，“我看见你坐在法庭上，穿一件棕色——不，蓝色——的大衣，帽子端端正正搁在膝头，抬头望着被告席上的我，琢磨我会怎么受这份罪。”

===Original===
A cloud passed over his face.
“You’re a pessimistic little devil,” he growled. “No, I wasn’t thinking about that.”
“What were you thinking about?” she asked, her eyes wide open in surprise.
“I was thinking I’d marry you,” he said.

===Chinese===
一片阴云掠过他的脸。
“你这个小悲观鬼。”他咕哝道，“不，我不是在想那个。”
“那你在想什么？”她睁大眼睛，一脸惊讶地问。
“我在想，我要娶你。”他说。

===Original===
She looked at him in amusement.
“You’re mad, aren’t you?”
“Yes,” he said; “didn’t you know?”
“Marry you!” she said scornfully. “Great Heavens!”
“You might do worse,” he said with his cheerful smile.

===Chinese===
她饶有兴味地看着他。
“你疯了吧？”
“是啊，”他说，“你不知道吗？”
“嫁给你！”她不屑地说，“老天爷！”
“更糟的出路也不是没有。”他带着愉快的微笑说。

===Original===
“Can you name anything I could do that would be more hopelessly degrading than marry a policeman?”
“Yes,” he said, “you might be an old maid and keep cats. You take it for granted, of course,” he went on, “that I am letting you go now.”
“Naturally,” she replied, “I have given you something to live for.”
“You may be right,” he said quietly and opened the door for her.
They walked down the felt covered passage to the front door.
“I owe you something,” she said as they stood in the doorway. “The young man from the South nearly put an end to my promising career.”

===Chinese===
“那你说说，我能干出什么比嫁给警察更没救、更跌份的事来？”
“有啊，”他说，“你可以当个老姑娘，养一群猫。当然，”他接着说，“你把这当作理所当然：我现在就放你走。”
“当然，”她答道，“我给了你活下去的盼头嘛。”
“也许你说得对。”他安静地说，替她开了门。
两人走过铺着毛毡的过道，来到正门前。
“我欠你一份情，”两人站在门口，她说，“那位南方来的小伙子，差点给我的锦绣前程画上句号。”

===Original===
“A little thing like that is hardly worth mentioning. Good night, Kate, are you sure it is safe for you to be out alone so late?”
She made a little face at him and went tripping down the stairs. She turned into the street, but had not gone two paces when a hand caught her arm.
“Excuse me,” said a voice.
By the light of a street lamp she recognized her captor as a detective sergeant from Scotland Yard.
Before she could protest a voice spoke from the darkness of the balcony above and it was the voice of Michael.

===Chinese===
“这点小事不值一提。晚安，凯特，这么晚一个人出门，你确定安全吗？”
她朝他做了个鬼脸，踏着轻快的步子下了楼。她拐进街道，还没走出两步，一只手抓住了她的胳膊。
“对不起。”一个声音说。
借着路灯的光，她认出抓她的人是苏格兰场的一名侦探警长。
她还没来得及抗议，头顶阳台的黑暗里响起一个说话的声音——是迈克尔的声音。

===Original===
“All right, sergeant,” he said.
She shook herself free of the man and looked wrathfully up at the dim figure.
“I forgot you’d have your nurse handy, Michael,” she jeered.
“Good night, dear,” said the voice from the balcony and to her intense annoyance she felt an extraordinary sensation wholly new to her, but which with her quick woman’s wit she correctly diagnosed, as she hurried angrily along the street.
For Kate Westhanger had blushed for the first time in her life.

===Chinese===
“行了，警长。”他说。
她挣脱那人，怒冲冲地抬头望着楼上模糊的身影。
“我倒忘了，你的保姆随叫随到，迈克尔。”她讥笑道。
“晚安，亲爱的。”阳台上的声音说。令她懊恼万分的是，她竟感到一阵全然陌生的异样滋味；她带着女人的机敏，一边气冲冲地沿街疾走，一边就把那滋味诊断了个正着。
因为凯特·韦斯特汉杰平生头一回，脸红了。

## XI Lady Moya Was Curiously Unlike Herself / 十一 莫亚小姐出奇地一反常态

===Original===
There was a greater reason for Sir Ralph’s perturbation than either he knew or Michael guessed. Both might have been enlightened, had they stood on Cannon Street Station one Sunday morning and seen the distress of Mr. Alphonso Blaxton as the big minute hand of the station clock grew nearer to nine. The guard was closing the doors of the carriages and the collector was preparing to shut the gate, when Moya came flying breathlessly through the barrier.
“Oh, I *am* so sorry!” she gasped; “my watch stopped.”
Mr. Alphonso Blaxton bundled her into an empty first-class carriage and jumped in himself as the train moved.
“There’s not another train for three hours,” he said severely.
“We could have gone to church.”

===Chinese===
拉尔夫爵士心神不宁的原因，其实比他自己所知道、迈克尔所猜到的都要重大。倘若某个星期天早晨他们俩站在坎农街车站，看见阿方索·布拉克斯顿先生望着车站时钟那根大分针一点点逼近九点时的焦急模样，就全都明白了。列车员正在关车厢门，收票员正要关闸，莫亚气喘吁吁地飞奔过了检票口。
“哎呀，真对不起！”她喘着气说，“我的表停了。”
阿方索·布拉克斯顿先生把她塞进一节空着的头等车厢，趁火车开动之际自己也跳了进去。
“再等三个小时才有下一班车。”他严厉地说。
“咱们本可以去教堂的。”

===Original===
“What a mind!” said the young man in admiration. “I never thought of church!”
“Anyway, I didn’t lose the train,” she said tartly. “Have you brought everything?”
She looked round for the collapsible easel, the paint boxes and the paraphernalia which usually accompanied their sketching tours.
“I have brought nothing,” he said frankly.
“But how can you sketch?”

===Chinese===
“多么好的头脑！”年轻人赞叹道，“我就从来没想起过教堂！”
“反正我没误车，”她尖刻地说，“东西都带齐了吗？”
她环顾四周，找那架折叠画架、颜料盒，还有他们外出写生时惯常带着的那一套行头。
“我什么都没带。”他坦白地说。
“那你怎么写生？”

===Original===
“I am not going to sketch,” he said. “I decided that it was too nice a day to waste.”
She looked up at him and laughed.
“You will never be an artist,” she said, suddenly severe. “To what part of the country are we going?”
“I thought we would go to Maidstone. There are some lovely drives from there. I’ve hired a motor car to meet us at the station and I thought we would go through Sussex and lunch at Seahampton.”
“Not Seahampton,” she said quickly; “my father is at Seahampton today.”
She might have added that Sir Ralph was also at Seahampton, but, for reasons of her own, she kept that information to herself because Sir Ralph was not a subject which she had found it necessary to discuss. She looked at her companion approvingly.

===Chinese===
“我不打算写生，”他说，“我拿定主意，这么好的天气可不能浪费。”
她抬眼看他，笑了。
“你永远当不成艺术家。”她忽然严厉起来，“咱们这是上哪儿去？”
“我想咱们去梅德斯通。从那儿出发有几条很美的兜风路线。我雇了一辆汽车到车站接咱们，打算穿过苏塞克斯，到西汉普顿吃午饭。”
“别去西汉普顿，”她急急地说，“我爸爸今天就在西汉普顿。”
她本可以再补一句：拉尔夫爵士今天也在西汉普顿。但她出于自己的缘故把这话咽了回去，因为拉尔夫爵士还不是她认为有必要讨论的话题。她赞许地打量着同伴。

===Original===
“You are ever so much more presentable than I have ever seen you, before,” she said, “and you have actually shaved! You are getting less and less like an artist every day.”
He had a peculiarly sweet smile and a laugh which was all bubbling youth and happiness. He laughed like a girl, indeed it nearly approached a giggle. He laughed now as the train sped through the suburban stations, stretched out his feet on the cushions opposite and searched for a cigarette. She watched him with glee as he produced, not the ornate case in which the men of her acquaintance carried the expensive products of Egypt and Syria, but a gaudy yellow carton containing fifty of the cheapest cigarettes that ever brought discredit to the fair State of Virginia.
“Do you like those things?” she asked.
“These ‘yellow perils’? Rather!”

===Chinese===
“你这模样比我以往见过的哪一次都体面多了，”她说，“你居然还刮了脸！你一天比一天不像艺术家了。”
他的微笑甜得出奇，笑声里全是冒泡似的青春和快乐。他笑起来像个姑娘，简直接近咯咯的娇笑。此刻火车飞驰过郊区一个个小站，他又笑了，把脚伸到对面的坐垫上，摸出一支烟。她饶有兴味地看他掏出来的，不是她熟识的那些男人们装埃及叙利亚高级香烟的华丽烟盒，而是一只花哨的黄纸盒，装着五十支最贱的香烟——那烟贱得足以让美好的弗吉尼亚州蒙羞。
“你喜欢抽那种东西？”她问。
“这种‘黄祸’牌？可喜欢了！”

===Original===
“Your taste is awfully uncultivated, isn’t it?” she bantered; “why don’t you﻿—” she abruptly attempted to change the subject by an incoherent reference to a cow which was gazing in a field by the side of the line.
“Why don’t I smoke gold-laced Machinopolos through an amber and diamond cigarette holder?” he suggested. “Because, little Moya, I am a poor hardworking artist who has been saving up all the week for this bust.”
“I am so sorry,” she said; “I am awfully thoughtless. Won’t you forgive me?”
“I won’t forgive you,” he said, “unless you keep in your mind the big fact that I am as immensely poor, as you are immensely rich.”
“Why should I keep that in my mind?” she asked.
“Because,” he said slowly, “until you are immensely poor or I am immensely rich we shall meet very occasionally and indulge in very infrequent busts.”

===Chinese===
“你的品味可真够没教养的，是不是？”她打趣道，“你怎么不——”她忽然没头没脑地提起铁道旁田里正瞪着眼的一头母牛，生硬地把话岔开。
“我怎么不抽金线缠嘴的马基诺波洛斯，再插在镶钻石的琥珀烟嘴上？”他替她说完，“因为啊，小莫亚，我是个卖力气干活儿的穷艺术家，为今儿这趟快活游攒了整整一个星期的钱。”
“真对不起，”她说，“我太欠考虑了。你肯原谅我吗？”
“我不原谅你，”他说，“除非你把一个大事实牢记在心：我穷得彻头彻尾，就像你富得彻头彻尾。”
“我为什么要记着这个？”她问。
“因为，”他慢悠悠地说，“不等你穷个透，或者我富个透，咱们就只能偶尔见见面，快活游也只能稀罕地来一回。”

===Original===
“But what difference does money make?” she faltered.
She found it difficult to speak plainly or even clearly. There was a lump in her throat which made her voice sound unnaturally hoarse. She had a strange sinking feeling within her and to her amazement she found the hand that she put up to brush back a stray curl trembling. She had never experienced any such sensation before. Her heart was thumping quickly; she was breathless, hot and cold by turns.
He did not answer. She was seated by his side and she could only see his face out of the corner of her eyes, then she felt his arm slipping about her and before she knew what had happened, his lips were pressed to hers.
This happened in a first-class railway carriage on a nonstop train. It had happened before to quite common people (as Moya had heard), but she never thought it would possibly happen to her, or that so vulgar a proceeding could be so wonderfully sweet.
---

===Chinese===
“可钱又有什么要紧的呢？”她声音发颤。
她觉得话都难以说囫囵，连说清楚都不容易。喉咙里堵着一块东西，嗓音哑得不像是自己的。她心底生出一种奇怪的往下坠的感觉，更让她惊愕的是，她抬起手去拢一缕散落的鬈发，那手竟在发抖。这样的感觉她从未体会过。她的心怦怦跳得飞快；她气都喘不匀，一阵阵发热，又一阵阵发冷。
他没有回答。她坐在他身旁，只能用眼角看见他的脸。随后她觉出他的手臂悄悄揽住了自己，还没弄明白发生了什么，他的唇已经贴上了她的唇。
这一幕发生在一列直达车的头等车厢里。这种事从前也发生在相当普通的人身上（莫亚有所耳闻），可她从没想过可能轮到自己头上，更没想到如此俗气的一件事竟能如此美妙，如此甜。
---

===Original===
Sir Ralph and Lord Flanborough had met the local authorities. There had been a lunch and speeches in which Sir Ralph had distinguished himself by likening the forthcoming arrival of the Austral-African mail ship to the return of Ulysses and the landing of the Pilgrim Fathers. A wireless message from the ship stated that she did not expect to make harbour until nine o’clock in the evening, and this explained the earlier festivities. That they were of a sober and restricted nature, was explained by the fact that the day was Sunday. Later, it was intended that the sailings of the Austral-African line from Cape Town should be timed to bring the ships to port on the Saturday, but there had been no time to alter the arrangements for the *Charter Queen* had sailed before Lord Flanborough and Sir Ralph had definitely decided the date on which the new service should be inaugurated.
A few pressmen who had come down from London for the purpose, with certain directors and their wives, were shown over the docks; the new trains were admired and particularly two brand new trucks, the peculiar character of which was exhibited by Sir Ralph to a select few of his fellow directors. A safe on wheels was an excellent description for one of these. Specially strengthened undercarriages, each truck supported by two bogies, they were designed to carry a tremendous weight.
“I am sure Lord Flanborough doesn’t mind my telling you,” said Sir Ralph to the little party, “that this will carry twenty tons of bar gold tonight.”
“What will be the value of that?” asked one of the interested audience.

===Chinese===
拉尔夫爵士和弗兰伯勒勋爵会见了当地要员。午宴上有演说，拉尔夫爵士大出风头，把澳非邮船即将到港比作尤利西斯返航和清教徒前辈登陆。船上发来无线电报，说预计要到晚上九点才能进港，这就解释了庆典为何提前。庆典之所以节制而收敛，则因为这天是星期天。往后澳非航线的班轮从开普敦启航的时间本该调整到星期六进港，只是《宪章女王》号早在弗兰伯勒勋爵和拉尔夫爵士定下新航线首航日期之前就已经起航，来不及改了。
几位专程从伦敦赶来的记者，连同若干董事及其夫人，被领着参观了船坞；新列车备受称赞，尤其是那两辆崭新的货车——拉尔夫爵士向精心挑选的几位同僚董事展示了它们的特殊性能。说其中一辆是“装了轮子的保险柜”，是个绝妙的形容。底架特别加固，每辆车由两组转向架承托，专为载运惊人的重量而设计。
“我相信弗兰伯勒勋爵不会介意我告诉诸位，”拉尔夫爵士对这一小群人说，“今晚这一辆要装二十吨金条。”
“那得值多少钱？”听众里有人来了兴致。

===Original===
“£2,867,200,” said Sir Ralph impressively; “representing six months’ output of the whole of Lord Flanborough’s gold properties.”
The directors made appropriate noises to signify their astonishment.
There were visitors to Seahampton interested in this great transportation, who were not invited to participate in the function. One of these, a dark foreign looking man, went no nearer to the docks than a little public house in the ancient High Street. He was visited by a man who was pallid of face and laconic of speech.
“It’s all up!” he said under his breath.
“What is wrong?” said the other in the same tone.

===Chinese===
“2,867,200 英镑。”拉尔夫爵士字字铿锵地说，“相当于弗兰伯勒勋爵全部金矿六个月的产量。”
董事们发出得体的惊叹声，以示震惊。
到西汉普顿来关注这场大运输的访客中，也有没受邀参加典礼的。其中一个肤色黝黑、貌似外国人的汉子，走到古老大街上的一家小酒馆便再不肯朝船坞近一步。有个脸色苍白、言语简短的人来见他。
“全泡汤了！”他压着嗓子说。
“出了什么岔子？”对方用同样的声调问。

===Original===
“It is quite impossible to get the driver or the fireman. They are two old servants of the company, both have money saved and would no more think of accepting a bribe than Flanborough himself.”
“You didn’t press the matter, I hope?” asked the other quickly.
The pallid man shook his head.
“I went as far as I dared with the driver,” he said. “I found out he had a son in the army in India and I told him that I had met the boy and got quite friendly with the old chap﻿—but he is a sea-green incorruptible, Gregori.”
“I will get on the phone to Kate,” said the other. “I suppose we shall have to hold up the train somewhere﻿—I don’t want to do any shooting if it can be avoided. Are the drivers armed?”
“It is funny you should ask that,” said the pallid man, sipping his beer. “The old man is armed for the first time in his life. He was full of it and quite proud of his ability to loose off a gun.”

===Chinese===
“司机和司炉根本拿不下来。俩人都是公司的老仆役，都有积蓄，你想让他们收贿，就跟指望弗兰伯勒本人收贿一样。”
“你没把事情做过头吧，我希望？”对方急忙问。
苍白脸摇了摇头。
“跟司机那边，我话说到敢说的极限了，”他说，“我打听到他有个儿子在印度当兵，就告诉他我见过那孩子，跟老爷子处得挺热乎——可他是块海绿色的硬骨头，油盐不进，格雷戈里先生。”
“我这就打电话给凯特，”对方说，“看来我们只好在什么地方拦火车了——能避免的话，我不想开枪。司机带武器了吗？”
“亏你问到这个，”苍白脸呷着啤酒说，“老爷子这辈子头一回带上了枪。他满脑子都是这事，对自己会放枪还挺得意。”

===Original===
Gregori looked very serious.
“Kate must be prepared with the alternative scheme,” he said. “Anyway, you will join me here with Cunningham at eight o’clock. I am perfectly prepared for almost all contingencies. Millet has given me a dozen authorities to meet almost any developments. Did you see the train?”
“I couldn’t get near it,” said the other. “I left just before Sapson brought his party to make their inspection.”
Sir Ralph had carried his guests from the siding to the engine shed and shown them the brand new Atlantic locomotive which was to draw the train to London.

===Chinese===
格雷戈里先生脸色郑重起来。
“凯特必须备好第二套方案，”他说，“总之，你八点钟带坎宁安先生到这儿来会合。几乎所有意外我都有备无患。米利特先生给了我十几条锦囊，几乎什么变故都应付得了。列车你见到没有？”
“我根本靠不了边，”对方说，“萨普森带人来进行检查之前，我就撤了。”
拉尔夫爵士领着客人从侧线走到机车库，参观那辆即将把列车拉往伦敦的崭新“大西洋”式机车。

===Original===
“They don’t seem to have finished it yet,” said one of the guests, and pointed to a workman busily drilling a hole in the front plate.
Ralph laughed.
“They omitted to put a bracket for the lamp. You see, I wanted three green lights in a line for the Gold Train﻿—it is very necessary that it should be very accurately and easily distinguished and signalled. By some chance only two of the brackets were in place when the engine came from the works. It is all the more annoying, because I had already given definite instructions upon that point, but we shall not go wrong for a lamp,” he said humorously.
It is agreed that the three hours between two and five on a Sunday afternoon are the three dullest in the hundred and sixty-eight which constitute a week. After the guests had left for London Sir Ralph and Lord Flanborough remained at the little station hotel﻿—Ralph had already projected a more palatial establishment to meet the increased traffic﻿—for it had been arranged that they should greet the *Charter Queen* on her arrival.
At three o’clock that afternoon Ralph burst unceremoniously into Lord Flanborough’s private sitting room where his lordship sat dozing.

===Chinese===
“他们好像还没装完呢，”有位客人说，指着一个正在前脸板上忙着钻孔的工人。
拉尔夫笑了。
“他们漏装了一个灯架。是这样，黄金列车我要三盏绿灯排成一线——务必让人家一眼就能认出它、轻易给它信号，这一点非常要紧。不知怎么的，机车出厂时灯架只装好了两个。尤其气人的是，这一点我早就下过明确指示。不过我们总不至于少了盏灯就抓瞎。”他说得妙趣横生。
世人公认，星期天下午两点到五点这三个钟头，是一个星期一百六十八个小时里最无聊的三段。客人们回伦敦去了，拉尔夫爵士和弗兰伯勒勋爵留在小站旅馆里——拉尔夫已经计划着盖一座更气派的旅馆，以应付增多的客流量——因为已安排好，他们要迎接进港的《宪章女王》号。
当天下午三点，拉尔夫不客气地一头闯进弗兰伯勒勋爵的私人起居室，勋爵大人正在里头打盹。

===Original===
“Have you had a wire?” he said.
He held a pink form in his own hand.
“A wire! What about?” asked Lord Flanborough startled.
“Read this.”
The telegram was signed “Michael,” and read:
> “Simultaneous attempt made to burgle your strong room at Austral-African office and Flanborough’s safe at headquarters of mining corporation. Both unsuccessful. Both doors blown out by nitro-gelatine. Will confirm by phone.”

===Chinese===
“你收到电报没有？”他说。
他自己手里捏着一张粉红色的电报纸。
“电报！什么事？”弗兰伯勒勋爵吃了一惊。
“看看这个。”
电报署名“迈克尔”，内容如下：
> “有人同时企图盗窃澳非公司贵方保险库与矿业公司总部的弗兰伯勒保险柜。两处均未得手。两扇柜门均被硝化凝胶炸开。容后再电话确认。”

===Original===
Lord Flanborough looked at the other open-mouthed.
“This is very serious,” he said.
“I have ordered a special to take us to town. We will wait till we get the phone message through.”
Ten minutes after they were in communication with Michael.
“Both doors have been blown out,” he repeated, “and there are one or two very puzzling features about the burglaries. Nobody could have been present in either office when the explosions occurred. There was no fire and, so far as I can see, nothing has been taken away. You had better come up and examine things for yourself.”
“It is rather awkward,” said Sir Ralph thoughtfully as he hung up the receiver; “my ‘special’ driver is also the driver of the gold special.”

===Chinese===
弗兰伯勒勋爵目瞪口呆地望着他。
“这事非同小可。”他说。
“我已经订了趟专车送我们进城。等电话一到我们就动身。”
十分钟后，他们和迈克尔通上了话。
“两扇门都炸飞了，”他重复道，“不过这桩盗窃案有一两处非常费解的地方。爆炸发生时，两间办公室里都不可能有人。没有起火，就我看来也没丢任何东西。你们最好亲自上来瞧瞧。”
“这就有点棘手了，”拉尔夫爵士挂上听筒，若有所思地说，“我那趟‘专车’的司机，也是黄金专列的司机。”
===Original===
“It doesn’t require any great genius to drive a gold special,” snapped Flanborough; “put another man on to work tonight’s train and let us get up to town as soon as we can.”
The special was waiting in the station by the time they had reached the platform. Sir Ralph stayed long enough to give a few instructions to the superintendent and then boarded the train and was soon flying northward.
That Sunday morning had been an interesting one for Michael. He had been aroused by telephone at five o’clock only to learn from an apologetic operator that the wrong number had been called. Although it was two hours before he usually rose, he had his bath and dressed and not waking his servants made himself some coffee.

===Chinese===
“开趟黄金专列又用不着什么天才，”弗兰伯勒没好气地说，“换个人来开今晚的车，咱们好赶紧进城。”
他们走到站台时，专车已在站里候着。拉尔夫爵士耽搁了片刻，给段长交代了几句，便上了车，很快向北飞驰而去。
那个星期天早晨，对迈克尔来说颇有意思。五点钟电话把他吵醒，结果接线员赔着不是告诉他，号码打错了。虽然离他惯常起床还有两个钟头，他还是洗了澡、穿了衣，没叫醒仆人，自己煮了点咖啡。

===Original===
It was a bright morning, such as so often precedes a day of rain, when he turned into the deserted street. He had no particular aim or destination but he was in that mood which invites exercise. He walked down the Marylebone Road and through Portland Place without meeting anybody save an occasional policeman and so came to Piccadilly Circus where he bought a Sunday newspaper from an early vendor and passed down through Waterloo Place to the Park.
The gates had only just been opened and beyond the park-keepers and a slouching tramp he met nobody. He sat on one of the garden seats by the side of the lake, pulled his overcoat about his legs for the morning was chilly and began to scan the headlines in the newspaper. There was nothing startling here, but he read the columns conscientiously.
There was nothing in life which did not interest Michael Pretherston. He might have taken for his motto *homo sum; humani nihil a me alienum puto*. It was a saying of T. B. Smith’s that Michael could even write a readable volume on the psychology of dogfights. Every little larceny, however sordid, every tiny embezzlement however paltry, every swindle whether it was carried out by the great confidence men who “worked London” or by the smaller fry in the half-crown line of business gave him food for reflection and some little scrap of information which he stored away for future use.
He was in the midst of a long account of an East End arson charge when he heard his name called softly and looked up. He jumped to his feet.

===Chinese===
他走进空无一人的街道时，正是那种常有大雨在后头的晴朗清晨。他没有特定的目的或去处，只是心境正好想活动活动。他沿玛丽勒本路走过波特兰广场，一路只遇见偶尔一名警察，随后到了皮卡迪利广场，从早出的报贩手里买了份星期天报纸，穿过滑铁卢广场进了公园。
公园大门刚开，除了园丁和一个没精打采的流浪汉，他谁也没碰见。他在湖边一条长椅上坐下，早晨天凉，他把大衣裹住双腿，开始浏览报上的标题。没有什么骇人的新闻，他仍旧一字不落地读着各个版面。
