世上没有一件事不让迈克尔·普雷瑟斯顿发生兴趣。他尽可以拿“我是人，人间之事无不与我相关”当座右铭。T. B. 史密斯有句口头禅：迈克尔连斗狗的心理都能写出一本可读的书。每一桩小小的偷窃，无论多么龌龊；每一笔微末的侵吞，无论多么寒酸；每一场骗局，不管出自“吃遍伦敦”的大骗子之手，还是干半克朗小买卖的小角色，都能给他提供思考的材料，添一点零碎信息，收存起来备将来之用。
他正读到一篇关于东区纵火案的长篇报道，忽听得有人轻声唤他的名字，抬起头来。他霍地站起身。

===Original===
“Why, Kate,” he said, “haven’t you got any home?”
The girl was standing a few feet from him with an odd look on her face.
“I think it must be fate that brought me out this morning,” she said; “sit down, Mike, and tell me all the news.”
She showed no sign of resentment of his uncavalier treatment.
“Did you follow me here, or did I follow you?”
“I tell you it was fate,” she said. “I could not sleep and I drove my Mercedes down.”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，凯特，”他说，“你就没有个家吗？”
姑娘站在离他几步远的地方，脸上有种古怪的神情。
“我想今早把我引出来的一定是命运，”她说，“坐下吧，迈克，把新闻都讲给我听。”
对于他那毫无骑士风度的做派，她没有露出一丝恼意。
“是你跟到这儿来的，还是我跟你来的？”
“跟你说过了，是命运，”她说，“我睡不着，就把我的梅赛德斯开了出来。”

===Original===
“And how is the Princess Bacheffski?” he asked as she seated herself by his side.
“The Princess﻿—?”
“Bacheffski﻿—poor old Ralph! What a thing to put over him!”
She leant forward, her chin on her palm, her elbow on her crossed knee.
“You frighten me sometimes,” she said. “I have not been able to make up my mind whether you are clever or whether you are lucky.”
“I am both lucky and clever,” he said. “Tell me something about your property in the Ural Mountains,” he said.

===Chinese===
“巴切夫斯基公主可好？”她在他身边坐下时，他问。
“公主——？”
“巴切夫斯基——可怜的老拉尔夫！给他玩了这么一手！”
她俯身向前，下巴搁在掌心，手肘支着交叠的膝头。
“你有时候真吓人，”她说，“我一直拿不准，你到底是聪明，还是运气好。”
“我又聪明运气又好，”他说，“给我讲讲你在乌拉尔山里的产业吧。”

===Original===
“In Poland,” she corrected him.
“Mines, I suppose?”
“There are no mines on my property,” she said calmly; “would you be greatly surprised if I told you I had an estate in Poland?”
“Nothing you said would surprise me, unless you told me you were going to be a good girl and respect the law relating to property.”
He folded his paper and dropped it into a wire receptacle provided for that purpose and she followed the operations with amusement.
“What a tidy soul you are,” she said; “fancy doing things you are told and obeying even bylaws.”

===Chinese===
“在波兰。”她纠正他。
“是矿场吧？”
“我的产业上没有矿，”她平静地说，“要是我告诉你，我在波兰有一座庄园，你会大吃一惊吗？”
“你说什么都不会让我吃惊，除非你说你要学好，尊重那条关于财产的法律。”
他把报纸折好，丢进专为放报设置的铁丝筐里，她饶有兴味地看他做完这一套动作。
“你可真是个规规矩矩的人，”她说，“瞧瞧，让做什么就做什么，连附例都肯遵守。”

===Original===
“We all obey bylaws. You are not so original as you think. For instance, I observe that you are wearing a little toque﻿—is that the word?”
“That is the word,” she agreed.
“Toques are fashionable at this present moment. You are obeying the bylaws. You haven’t the courage to come out in a sky-blue tam-o’shanter with an ostrich feather because it is against the bylaws. Also I remark that your dress is very short and very full. You are not wearing a Roman toga or a Grecian gown, or even a hobble skirt. Why? Because it is against the bylaws. It is absurd to disobey one set and slavishly obey another.”
“You are quaint!” was the only answer she gave.
“Will you tell me, Princess?”
“Don’t call me ‘Princess’ if you please,” she said quietly.

===Chinese===
“我们都遵守附例。你可不像自己以为的那么别出心裁。比如说，我注意到你戴了顶小小的无边帽——是这个词吧？”
“正是这个词。”她认了。
“无边帽眼下正时兴。你就是在遵守附例。你不敢戴一顶缀鸵鸟毛的天蓝色贝雷软帽出门，因为那不合附例。我还注意到你的裙子又短又宽。你没穿罗马托加袍，也没穿希腊长衫，甚至连窄底裙都没穿。为什么？因为不合附例。一面违抗这一套规矩，一面又奴颜婢膝地服从另一套，未免可笑。”
“你真是个怪人！”她只回了这一句。
“您肯告诉我了吗，公主殿下？”
“请你别叫我‘公主’。”她安安静静地说。

===Original===
“Well, will you tell me, my landowner, what was the game with Ralph? He described you with the greatest enthusiasm by-the-way. The night you met him you were all dolled up to kill. Did you bring down your birds?”
“I got him,” she admitted.
She was not as bright as usual.
“You are overdoing it,” said Michael; “you are trying to do too much. Your doctor would probably tell you that you ought not to commit more than one burglary a month.”
She laughed softly.

===Chinese===
“好吧，那您肯告诉我吗，我的女地主——跟拉尔夫玩的是哪一出？顺便说一句，他可劲儿地夸您来着。你们碰面的那天晚上，你打扮得花枝招展，存心迷死人。鸟儿可都打下来了？”
“打下来一只。”她承认。
她不像平日那么明快。
“你干得太猛了，”迈克尔说，“你想干的事太多。你的大夫多半会嘱咐你，入室盗窃一个月顶多干一桩。”
她轻轻笑了。

===Original===
“You are very quaint,” she said again.
“You don’t feel like making a full and frank confession, I suppose,” he suggested; “you would not like to burst into tears and sob out your young heart on my shoulder?”
“That sob stuff never did agree with me.”
He raised a disapproving hand.
“Kate,” he said, “I have noticed a disposition in you to adopt the slang which is employed exclusively by American newspaper reporters, vaudeville artistes and other members of the criminal classes.”
“I will tell you this,” she said sitting upright and looking him fully in the face, “we are going to do a big thing. The most colossal, the most daring that has ever been done and we are going to do it today. You want to know why I went to Flanborough’s, why I made up to that unspeakable person, Ralph Sapson? Those are my two victims. I will tell you more than this,” she said after a moment’s thought, “in order to ensure the success of my scheme I have arranged for those two gentlemen to be out of London on this bright Sabbath day. I can’t tell you any more, Mike.”

===Chinese===
“你真是个怪人。”她又说了一遍。
“我看你是不想彻底坦白从宽了，”他提议道，“也不打算泪如泉涌，把你那颗年轻的心哭倒在我肩膀上？”
“哭哭啼啼那一套，从来不合我的脾胃。”
他抬起一只手，表示不赞成。
“凯特，”他说，“我注意到你有种倾向，爱用起美国报纸记者、杂耍艺人和其他犯罪阶层专用的俚语来。”
“我可以告诉你一件事，”她坐直身子，直直望着他的脸，“我们要干一件大事。有史以来最庞大、最大胆的一件，而且今天就要干。你想知道我为什么去弗兰伯勒家，为什么去讨好拉尔夫·萨普森那个说不出口的人？这两位就是我的牺牲品。我还可以多告诉你一点，”她略一思索又说，“为了保证计划成功，我已经安排妥当，让这两位先生在这个晴朗的安息日都出伦敦城。再多的我不能说了，迈克。”

===Original===
“You are like a serial story, you finish off at the most interesting place,” he grumbled.
His keen grey eyes searched hers and she met them fairly.
“I wish you weren’t,” he said.
“Weren’t what?” she asked.
“In this business,” he nodded. “I wish you weren’t.”
“Perhaps I will be good one of these days,” she said, “and then you can recommend me for a job at two-ten-per. I’d make an ideal secretary for you, Mike. I know all the underworld by name. You could cut out your finger print department and leave it to Kate. What would happen, do you think,” she went on, “if I went to a Salvation Army officer and said, ‘I have been very wicked but now I am going to be good. Will you please assist me. I have no money but I’ve a good heart﻿—’ Mike, he would put me to chopping wood for a week and then he would find me a place as undersecretary to a housemaid in a strictly religious family which gave me two evenings and one Sunday a month. You see, Mike, even at goodliness one has to start at the bottom of the ladder; you can’t break in on the roof. I hate good people.”

===Chinese===
“你就像部连载小说，总在最要紧的地方戛然而止。”他抱怨道。
他那双锐利的灰眼睛探进她的眼睛，她坦然相对。
“我巴不得你不是。”他说。
“不是什么？”她问。
“不干这一行。”他点点头，“我巴不得你不干。”
“也许哪天我会学好，”她说，“到那时候你就可以推荐我去干那份周薪两镑十先令的差事。我会是你的理想秘书，迈克。黑道上有名有姓的我全认得。你们可以把指纹科裁掉，全交给凯特。你猜猜会怎么样，”她接着说，“要是我走到救世军军官面前说：‘我作过孽，如今要学好。请帮帮我。我没有钱，可我有一颗好心——’迈克，他会先派我去劈一星期柴，然后给我找个差事，去某户极虔诚的人家当女佣的副手，每月歇两个晚上、一个礼拜天。你瞧，迈克，连学好这行也得从梯子最底下爬起；谁也不能从房顶上破格而入。我讨厌好人。”

===Original===
Michael nodded.
“I hate good people, too,” he said, “if they advertise their goodness, but goodness is not hardness or sourness, it is just﻿—goodness. For example,” he went on, “I am good.”
“And I am wicked,” she said and appealed with outstretched hands to a startled duck who had waddled to the railings, “choose between us!”
He laughed but was instantly serious again.
“Your confession puts me in a dilemma. As you are a lady I cannot believe you are lying, as you are a criminal I dare not take your word. I am sufficiently acquainted with your methods to know that your presence is not essential to the committal of a crime, so I can gain nothing by pulling you in.”

===Chinese===
迈克尔点点头。
“我也讨厌好人，”他说，“要是他们把自己的好四处张扬的话。不过好不是硬邦邦，也不是酸溜溜，它就是——好。比方说，”他接着说，“我就很好。”
“而我坏。”她说着，向一只摇摇摆摆走到栏杆边、被吓了一跳的鸭子伸出双手申诉，“请在你我之间裁决！”
他笑了，随即又严肃起来。
“你这一番自白把我逼进了两难。你是位淑女，我不能相信你在撒谎；你是个罪犯，我又不敢轻信你的话。你的手段我领教得够多，知道干一桩犯罪未必非要你本人在场，所以把你抓回去也捞不着什么。”

===Original===
“Poor Mike,” she said mockingly.
“Poor Kate,” he said and the girl detected the note of sincerity in his voice.
“Kate, you can’t get away with it,” he said; “you have got to fall sooner or later. Think what it means. Think of that horrible drab life in Aylesbury, where every minute is an hour and every hour an eternity; think of the menial things they will set you to do, scrubbing floors, washing shirts and sewing sacks. Think, how you will be marshalled to church every Sunday and think how you will be stared at and jeered at by friends of the Home Secretary who come to visit the jail.”
“When that happens I shall be dead,” she said. “I believe you mean kindly, Michael Pretherston, and I will tell you this, that you nor any other human being can make me think or feel any different to what I think and feel. There is no power on earth that can tear out the foundations on which my life is built. I have read everything, all the philosophies, Christian and pagan, and all the arguments from the feeble evangelism of the tract writer, to the blatant nonsense of the professional atheist, and I am just where I began. You can’t touch me by reason or by devotion, by faith or by prayers. I am all stone﻿—here,” she laid her white hand upon her bosom and he saw the mocking laughter in her eyes. “Poor Michael!” she said. “Why, if devotion could change me, think of the chances I have had! I could have taken Ralph Sapson and made of him a snake ring for my little finger. I nearly had Flanborough on the point of proposing to me. He is rather sentimental, did you know that?”
“All people with indigestion are sentimental between paroxysms,” said Michael sagely.

===Chinese===
“可怜的迈克。”她嘲弄地说。
“可怜的凯特。”他说。姑娘听出了他嗓音里那点真诚。
“凯特，你是逃不掉的，”他说，“迟早要栽跟头。想想那意味着什么。想想艾尔斯伯里那种可怕的灰暗日子：一分钟熬成一小时，一小时熬成永恒；想想他们派你干的那些下等活计，擦地板、洗衬衣、缝麻袋。想想每个礼拜天你将被押着排队上教堂，再想想内政大臣的朋友们来参观监狱时，将怎样盯着你看、笑话你。”
“真到那时候我早死了，”她说，“我相信你是一片好意，迈克尔·普雷瑟斯顿，我也告诉你：无论你，还是世上任何人，都不能让我的所思所感改变分毫。世上没有一种力量，能把我人生赖以奠基的根基拔出去。我什么都读过，一切哲学，基督教的和异教的，一切论调，从小册子作者软弱的福音布道，到职业无神论者的喧嚷胡说，而我仍旧站在起点上。你用道理打动不了我，用痴心打动不了我，信仰不行，祈祷也不行。我全身上下都是石头——在这儿。”她把白皙的手按在胸口，他看见她眼里嘲弄的笑意。“可怜的迈克尔！”她说，“哎呀，要是痴心能改变我，想想我有过多少机会！拉尔夫·萨普森到手了，我能把他打成小指上的一枚蛇形戒指。弗兰伯勒也险些被我逼到求婚的边缘。他挺多愁善感的，你知道吗？”
“凡是消化不良的人，发作间隙都多愁善感。”迈克尔一脸智者气地说。

===Original===
He gave his hand to the girl though it was unnecessary and helped her to her feet and they walked out of the park together. Her little Mercedes was unattended and he cranked it up for her.
“Goodbye, Michael,” she said.
“Au revoir,” said Michael, “we shall meet at the sessions.”
---
At two o’clock that afternoon a constable on duty in Moorgate Street heard the first of the two explosions which agitated police circles that day. Michael was on the spot half-an-hour later and his brief examination led to the view which he afterwards communicated to Ralph. It was then he discovered that what the girl had told him was true and that both Lord Flanborough and Sir Ralph Sapson were out of town. Curiously enough, though he had been impressed at the time, he had dismissed the girl’s statement as a piece of bravado on a par with the badinage in which she usually indulged. He had cursed his folly in ignoring the warning, all the way from Baker Street to the city and it was a great relief to discover what was evident, that no attempt had been made to rifle either the safe in Bartholomew Close or the strong room in Moorgate Street. The outrages were similar in character; in both cases the steel doors had been burst open by the application of an infernal machine. In neither case had the thieves benefited by their crime. The constable who heard the first explosion said he had been admitted by the caretaker of the building within three minutes but in that time had managed to send another policeman, who came up, to guard the back of the premises. Nobody had either entered or left in that period.
The explosion in Bartholomew Close had blown a skylight into the street. The safe was in a concrete cellar in which a light had been burning day and night and although this had been extinguished by the force of the explosion, it was possible for the constable who was outside to see the safe and obtain a fairly comprehensive view of the chamber. He, too, had asserted that nobody had entered the room or left the building after the explosion.

===Chinese===
他向姑娘伸出手——虽然并无必要——扶她起身，两人一同走出公园。她那辆小小的梅赛德斯无人看管地停着，他替她摇了引擎。
“再见了，迈克尔。”她说。
“回头见。”迈克尔说，“咱们开庭时再会。”
---
当天下午两点，莫尔加特街一名值勤警察听见了两声爆炸中的第一声——这两声爆炸当天震动了警界。半小时后迈克尔赶到现场，简短查验之后形成了他后来告知拉尔夫的看法。直到这时他才发现，姑娘对他说的话是真的：弗兰伯勒勋爵和拉尔夫·萨普森爵士都不在城里。说来奇怪，他当时明明为之动容，过后却把姑娘的话当作和她惯常的打趣同一路的逞能话，丢在了脑后。从贝克街到城里这一路，他把自己忽略警告的愚蠢骂了个够；等发现那显而易见的事实时，才算松了口气：巴塞洛缪巷的保险柜和莫尔加特街的保险库，都没有被盗掠的企图。两桩暴行性质相似：都是用爆炸物炸开了钢门。两案中窃贼都一无所获。听见第一声爆炸的警察说，三分钟内管理员就放他进了楼，但这点时间里他还来得及派另一名赶来的警察去把守楼后。那段时间里没有人进出。
巴塞洛缪巷的爆炸把一扇天窗炸到了街上。保险柜在一间混凝土地下室里，室内一盏灯昼夜长明，爆炸的气浪虽把它扑灭了，外面的警察仍能看见保险柜，把地下室的情形看得七七八八。他也一口咬定，爆炸之后没有人进过那屋子，也没有人离开那栋楼。

===Original===
“It is very curious,” said Michael.
T. B. Smith had come at his urgent request and the chief was as puzzled as his subordinate.
“Did Flanborough say he would come up?”
“He is on his way now,” replied Michael.
“Do you know what I think?” said T. B. after a moment’s thought. “I think that this is a blind. That there was never any intention of rifling either the strong room or the safe. There is a big move on somewhere, Mike, call in all the reserves.”
This was an order which Michael heard with pleasure, for he had already anticipated these instructions, and detectives were at that moment flocking to Scotland Yard from every point of the compass.

===Chinese===
“真是非常古怪。”迈克尔说。
T. B. 史密斯应他的急召赶来，这位长官和他的部下一样摸不着头脑。
“弗兰伯勒说他要上来吗？”
“他已经在路上了。”迈克尔回答。
“你知道我在想什么吗？”T. B. 略一思索后说，“我认为这是个障眼法。从来就没人打算撬那间保险库或那个保险柜。别处正在进行一场大行动，迈克，把后备力量全调回来。”
这道命令迈克尔听得正中下怀，因为他早已料到会有这道指令。此时此刻，侦探们正从四面八方向苏格兰场汇拢而来。

## XIX Michael Developed a Fondness for the Criminal Classes / 十九 迈克尔迷上了犯罪阶层

===Original===
The girl rose up from the chair where she had been sitting and crossed to where Michael lay on the floor where they had thrown him.
He looked up and smiled.
“Why, Kate,” he said faintly, “always﻿ ﻿… meeting﻿ ﻿… you.”
She sat down at his side and lifting his head laid it upon her lap.
“That’s nice,” he murmured.
“Why is it nice?” she asked curiously, “because I make a softer pillow than the stone?”

===Chinese===
姑娘从她坐着的椅子上站起身，走到迈克尔躺着的地方——他们把他扔在了地板上。
他抬起头，笑了。
“哎呀，凯特，”他有气无力地说，“老是……遇见……你。”
她在他身旁坐下，托起他的头，放在自己膝上。
“真舒服。”他喃喃道。
“为什么舒服？”她好奇地问，“因为我这枕头比石头软和？”

===Original===
“That and something more,” he answered.
“What more?” she insisted.
“Oh﻿—because it is you, I suppose,” he said vaguely.
Her lips twitched in amusement.
“But it would be just the same if it were any other person,” she said, “wouldn’t it, Mike?”

===Chinese===
“这个嘛，外加一点别的。”他答道。
“外加什么？”她追问。
“噢——大概因为是你吧。”他含含糊糊地说。
她的嘴角饶有兴味地一弯。
“可要是换成随便什么人，不也一样吗？”她说，“对不对，迈克？”

===Original===
He looked up at her.
“Put your hand on my forehead,” he said.
“Like this?”
She laid her soft palm against his throbbing head.
“What does that do?” she asked after a long interval of silence.
“It just makes my head better﻿—don’t ask a lot of questions.”

===Chinese===
他抬眼望她。
“把手放在我额头上。”他说。
“这样吗？”
她把柔软的手掌贴上他突突直跳的脑袋。
沉默了好一阵，她才问：“这有什么用？”
“就是让我脑袋好受些——别问个没完。”

===Original===
Her fingers stole down his face and she gently pinched his nose.
“Oh, Kate,” he murmured sleepily, “I was just going to sleep.”
“Then don’t,” she said, “what is the use of dozing﻿—you’ll be dead soon and so will I.”
She said this very calmly, in the same matter-of-fact tone in which she might have announced that there would be a roast chicken for dinner.
“I hope they kill you first,” she said thoughtfully.
“You’re a bloodthirsty little beggar,” said Michael indignantly; “why do you wish that?”

===Chinese===
她的手指顺着他的脸悄悄往下滑，轻轻捏了捏他的鼻子。
“噢，凯特，”他昏昏欲睡地嘟哝，“我正要睡着呢。”
“那就别睡，”她说，“打盹有什么用——反正你很快就死了，我也一样。”
她说这话时口气极平静，就跟宣布晚饭有烤鸡似的，一副理所当然的腔调。
“我希望他们先杀你。”她若有所思地说。
“你这个小杀人狂，”迈克尔愤愤地说，“你为什么这么盼着？”

===Original===
She shrugged her shoulders and went on pressing back the hair from his forehead, never taking her eyes from his face.
“I don’t know,” she said at last, “only I want to make sure that you’re gone and nobody else can have you﻿—and then I shan’t care.”
He did not move; for a second she saw his eyelids quiver, but he lay still staring past her to the dingy roof of the engine house.
“Say that again,” he whispered.
“Say what again? That I want you to be killed first?” she asked innocently.
“Mike,” she said suddenly, “who was the girl?”

===Chinese===
她耸耸肩，继续把头发从他额前向后拢，目光一刻也没离开他的脸。
“不知道，”她终于开口，“我只是想确定你先走了，没有别人能得到你——那样我就无所谓了。”
他一动不动；有一瞬她看见他的眼皮颤了颤，但他躺着没动，目光越过她，怔怔望着机棚脏暗的屋顶。
“再说一遍。”他低声说。
“说什么？说我要你先被杀？”她无辜地问。
“迈克，”她忽然说，“那姑娘是谁？”

===Original===
“Which girl?”
“You know,” she said, “the girl you﻿—care about.”
“Why, you of course,” he said in surprise.
Her hands slipped down from his forehead covering his eyes.
“Say that again,” she mimicked.
“You,” he repeated. “You see I am more obliging than you were.”

===Chinese===
“哪个姑娘？”
“你知道的，”她说，“你——放在心上的那个姑娘。”
“嘿，当然是你。”他讶然道。
她的双手从他额头滑下来，捂住了他的眼睛。
“再说一遍。”她学着他的腔调。
“你。”他重复道，“瞧，我可比你听话多了。”

===Original===
“And you would not come in with us, not even for me?”
“Not even for you.”
She did not speak for some time.
“How did you know we were here?” she asked.
“I knew you could be nowhere else,” he said.
“You are an awfully arrogant young man, aren’t you? Do you know how it was all done?”

===Chinese===
“那你就是不肯跟我们合伙，连为了我都不肯？”
“连为了你也不肯。”
她半晌没有作声。
“你怎么知道我们在这儿？”她问。
“我知道你们不可能在别处。”他说。
“你可真是个傲慢透顶的年轻人，是不是？你知道这一切是怎么干成的吗？”

===Original===
He nodded.
“The train ran into the tunnel where you had a long motorcar mounted with flanged wheels and having three green lamps on the front and two red tail lamps behind. That was the ‘train’ which the signalman saw dashing through the rain and you had a horrible siren.”
She laughed softly.
“It was terrible, wasn’t it?” she admitted. “Do you remember that day you were in Crime Street? You heard it.”
He recalled the uncanny sound which had then excited his curiosity.
“When you got to the level crossing gates, the car was lifted off the rail and went on to the road. It followed the tram lines for some distance where it turned into a convenient garage, which I suppose you had already arranged for?”

===Chinese===
他点点头。
“火车开进隧道，你们在那儿备了一辆加长的汽车，装上带轮缘的车轮，车头挂三盏绿灯，车尾挂两盏红灯。信号员冒着大雨看见疾驰而过的那列‘火车’就是它，你们还配了个刺耳的警笛。”
她轻轻笑了。
“确实够吓人的，对吧？”她承认道，“记得那天你在犯罪街吗？你听见的。”
他想起了当时激起他好奇心的那阵怪异声响。
“到了平交道口，汽车就被抬下铁轨，上了公路。它顺着电车轨道走了一段，拐进一间现成的车库——那车库我猜你们早就安排妥当了？”

===Original===
“That’s right,” she nodded.
“The train went no farther than the tunnel. It then backed on to a side track. Gregori had his Italian workmen ready and fixed up the buffer which had been dropped﻿—you know the rest. The hole behind the buffer and the green scum﻿—that was your idea, I suppose.”
“It was cunning, wasn’t it, and did you see the rust I made?”
“It is a fortunate thing you are dying young, Kate,” he said; “you have a criminal mind.”
“But I haven’t a criminal mind,” she protested; “it is a game, a sort of highly complicated jigsaw puzzle. Do you ever read detective stories?”

===Chinese===
“没错。”她点头。
“火车只开进隧道，没有再往前，随后退到一条侧线上。格雷戈里先生的意大利工人早已候着，把摔落的缓冲器装了回去——其余的你都知道了。缓冲器后面的窟窿和那层绿色的浮垢——我猜是你的主意吧。”
“够妙的吧？你看见我做的铁锈没有？”
“幸好你年纪轻轻就要死了，凯特，”他说，“你天生一副犯罪头脑。”
“我才没有犯罪头脑，”她抗议道，“这是游戏，一种复杂透顶的拼图。你读侦探小说吗？”

===Original===
“Very seldom.”
“But you have read them?” she persisted.
“I have read one or two,” he confessed.
“Did the men who wrote those have criminal minds? It was a game to them. It was a game to me. I know it is all wrong, horribly wrong, but I never thought I should realize that much. I thought nothing would turn me.”
“And what has turned you?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“很少读。”
“可你读过？”她追问。
“读过一两本。”他承认。
“写那些书的人就有犯罪头脑吗？对他们而言是游戏，对我也是。我知道这一切都不对，错得可怕，可我从未想过自己会走到这一步。我以为什么都动摇不了我。”
“那是什么动摇了你？”他问。

===Original===
She hesitated.
“I don’t know what it is,” she said shaking her head. “It is a curious feeling that I get when I meet one man in the world. A feeling that makes my heart turn to ice and makes me tremble. That is all it is, Mike﻿—how do you think they are going to do it?”
Her thoughts had gone back to the approaching end.
“Heaven knows,” said Michael. “I haven’t any time to think of it. I am thinking of something else. Why do they keep the steam up in that engine?” he asked.
“It was Gregori’s idea,” she said; “he had the hole filled in today and the buffer taken down. He thought it might be useful to let the engine run on to the main line and block it. That is, if we had word that they were sending a lot of police down to search this part of the country.”

===Chinese===
她犹豫了。
“我不知道那是什么，”她摇着头说，“是我在这个世界上遇见一个男人时生出的一种怪感觉。一种让我心头结冰、浑身发抖的感觉。就这么回事，迈克——你说他们会怎么下手？”
她的思绪又转回了那步步逼近的结局。
“天知道，”迈克尔说，“我没工夫想这个。我在想别的事。他们为什么让那台机车一直烧着汽？”他问。
“那是格雷戈里先生的主意，”她说，“他今天叫人把窟窿填平了，把缓冲器也拆了下来。他想，要是得到风声，说他们要派大批警察下来搜这一带，就让机车冲上正线把铁路堵住，兴许派得上用场。”

===Original===
“Here they are,” said Michael; “help me to sit up.”
She raised him to a sitting position as the door opened and a dim figure appeared silhouetted against the dusk. It struck a match and lit a candle and Dr. Garon was revealed. He placed the candle carefully upon the floor just behind the half-closed door and passed slowly over to where Michael lay.
“Well, my young sleuth,” he said pleasantly, “the best of friends must part.”
“Fortunately,” said Michael, “I do not fall into the category of your friends.”
The doctor hummed a little tune as he took a small leather case from his pocket.
“You have seen a hypodermic syringe before, I suppose?” he held up the tiny instrument. “I am going to give you a slight dope, which won’t hurt you.”

===Chinese===
“他们来了，”迈克尔说，“扶我坐起来。”
她把他扶成坐姿，恰在此时门开了，暮色里映出一个模糊的身影。那人划亮一根火柴，点燃一支蜡烛，照出了加龙医生。他把蜡烛小心地搁在半掩的门后地上，缓步走向迈克尔躺着的地方。
“好啊，我的年轻大侦探，”他语带愉快，“朋友再好，也有分手的时候。”
“幸好，”迈克尔说，“我不在你朋友之列。”
医生从口袋里掏出一个小皮盒，嘴里还哼着小曲。
“你见过皮下注射器吧？”他举起那件小器具，“我给你打一点儿小剂量的麻醉药，不疼的。”

===Original===
“One moment,” said Michael, “do I understand that this dope is﻿—final?”
The doctor bowed. From his heightened colour and his unsteady hand Michael guessed he had been drinking, either to give himself nerve for his task or to drown the memory of his misfortune.
“Very good,” said Michael. He looked up at the girl and raised his face and Kate stooped and kissed him on the lips.
“That is it, is it?” said the doctor unpleasantly. “Gregori will be pleased.”
He caught the manacled wrists of the prisoner and pulled back his sleeve and the girl’s heart almost ceased to beat.
It was at that moment that the light went out.

===Chinese===
“等一下，”迈克尔说，“我没理解错的话，这一针是——终局的？”
医生欠了欠身。从他泛红的脸色和打颤的手，迈克尔猜他喝过酒——或是为了给这趟差事壮胆，或是为了浇熄对自身不幸的记忆。
“很好。”迈克尔说。他抬眼望着姑娘，仰起脸，凯特俯身吻在他的唇上。
“原来是这么回事，啊？”医生阴阳怪气地说，“格雷戈里先生会满意的。”
他攥住囚犯戴着镣铐的手腕，把袖子往上一撸，姑娘的心几乎停止了跳动。
就在那一刻，灯灭了。

===Original===
“Who is there?” said the doctor releasing his grip on Michael’s arm and turning quickly.
He took a groping step forward through the darkness.
“Who’s there?” he said again and they heard a soft thud followed by the sound that a body might make, when it struck the ground.
Michael caught his breath. Suddenly a beam of light danced in the room and focused upon the prostrate figure of Dr. Garon.
“Got him,” said a well-satisfied voice.

===Chinese===
“谁在那儿？”医生松开抓着迈克尔手臂的手，霍地转过身去。
他在黑暗中摸索着向前跨了一步。
“谁在那儿？”他又问了一遍。接着他们听见一声闷响，随后是人摔在地上可能发出的那种声音。
迈克尔屏住了呼吸。忽然，一束光在屋里舞动起来，定格在加龙医生趴伏的身影上。
“收拾他了。”一个心满意足的声音说。

===Original===
“Barr,” whispered Michael, “where did you spring from?”
“I came through the door,” said the voice. “Did you see it open? That is what knocked the candle over.”
He flashed the light on his superior.
“They have got the bracelets on you, sir,” he chuckled softly, took a key from his pocket and with a few deft turns released the other. His pocket knife finished the work.
Michael stretched his cramped limbs.
“I tried to get in last night but they had too many sentries﻿—I couldn’t come here or get back to a telephone. I have been lying on that hillside all last night and all today,” said Detective-Sergeant Barr. “I dared not move until it was dark. I tell you, sir, I had a bit of a fright. I thought they would get away.”

===Chinese===
“巴尔，”迈克尔低唤，“你从哪儿冒出来的？”
“我从门里进来的，”那声音答道，“看见门开了吗？蜡烛就是它带倒的。”
他把光打在上司身上。
“他们给您戴上‘手镯’了，长官。”他低声一笑，从口袋里摸出一把钥匙，灵巧地转了几下解开锁扣，剩下的小活儿由折刀包办。
迈克尔舒展开僵麻的四肢。
“昨天夜里我就想摸进来，可他们岗哨太多——既进不来，也回不去打电话。我昨晚一整夜加上今天一整天，全趴在那面山坡上，”侦探警长巴尔说，“天不黑我一步也不敢挪。跟您说，长官，我着实吓了一跳，真怕他们跑掉。”

===Original===
“Have you a revolver?” asked his chief.
The man slipped a weapon into his hand. They made their way softly back through the room where the engine was still smoking, through the little steel door of the office. It was empty save for a shrouded figure which lay beneath the table. There was a second door in the room. Michael tried this. It was locked. He heard voices and tapped at the door.
“Who is there?” said Gregori.
“Open the door,” said Michael.
“Who is there?” demanded Gregori again.
“Open, in the name of the law,” said Michael.

===Chinese===
“你带左轮枪了吗？”上司问。
那人把一支枪塞进他手里。两人轻手轻脚地折回机车仍在冒烟的机棚，穿过办公室的小铁门。屋里空荡荡的，只有桌子底下躺着一具蒙布的身形。屋里还有第二道门。迈克尔试了试，锁着。他听见人声，便敲了敲门。
“谁在那儿？”格雷戈里先生问。
“开门。”迈克尔说。
“谁在那儿？”格雷戈里先生又厉声喝问。
“以法律的名义，开门。”迈克尔说。

===Original===
He heard a shuffle of feet and an oath and stood waiting, his pistol extended but the door did not open. A sudden silence came.
“Is there any way out of here?”
“There is a door leading into the shed where the engine is,” said the girl. She was white and trembling﻿ ﻿… that shrouded figure under the table had been the last straw.
Michael dashed out into the shed but it was too late.
As his feet crossed the foothold a bullet struck the steel door and ricochetted to the roof. In the dim light offered by an oil flare he saw Mulberry and Stockmar hoisting the inanimate figure of Dr. Garon to the cab of the engine. He fired twice and Cunningham stumbled but was dragged into the cab. Then with a mighty *schuff!* which reverberated through the building the engine began to move toward the closed door. It gathered speed in the dozen yards or so it had to traverse and then with a crash it struck the gate, splintering and sending it flying.
Michael flew the length of the shed and arrived at the outer gates in time to see the engine disappearing round the edge of the bluff. Barr was at his side and the two men stood helpless, as their enemies gradually receded into the grey dusk.

===Chinese===
他听见一阵脚步拖沓声和一句咒骂，便端着手枪站在门口等，可门没有开。陡然间，一片死寂。
“这儿还有别的出口吗？”
“有一道门通到停机车的那间机棚。”姑娘说。她脸色煞白，浑身发抖……桌子底下那具蒙布的尸身，成了压垮她的最后一根稻草。
迈克尔冲进机棚，但已经太迟了。
他刚跨过立足处，一颗子弹就打在铁门上，弹上屋顶。借着油炬昏暗的光，他看见马尔伯里先生和斯托克马尔正把不省人事的加龙医生往机车驾驶室里拖。他连开两枪，坎宁安先生打了个趔趄，还是被拽进了驾驶室。紧接着，机车发出一声震彻整座厂房的巨大“嚓呼！”，朝紧闭的大门开动起来。它在仅有的十几码路上越跑越快，随即轰然撞上大门，门板碎裂，四散横飞。
迈克尔一路飞奔穿过机棚，赶到外头大门时，恰好看见机车绕着峭壁边缘消失不见。巴尔已到他身旁，两人束手无策地站着，眼睁睁看着敌人渐渐退入灰蒙蒙的暮色。

===Original===
“There is a telephone here,” said Michael quickly, “but it is probably laid for their own purpose.”
“I left my motorbike on the top of the hill somewhere, sir,” said Barr.
“Get on to it,” said Michael.
He stood listening to the sound of the locomotive going faster and faster. A hand touched his timidly.
“Did they get away?”

===Chinese===
“这儿有部电话，”迈克尔急道，“不过多半是他们自己布线专用的。”
“我把摩托车撂在山顶什么地方了，长官。”巴尔说。
“快去骑上。”迈克尔说。
他站在原地，听着机车声越去越快。一只手怯生生地碰了碰他。
“他们跑掉了吗？”

===Original===
He slipped his arm round the girl.
“I am afraid they have,” he said.
He was turning back to the shed when the roar of an explosion set the building trembling.
“What was that?” whispered the girl.
They walked back to the end of the bluff. There was no need for him to speculate as to the direction from whence the explosion had come, for a bright red glow two miles away illuminated the whole countryside.

===Chinese===
他伸手揽住姑娘。
“恐怕是跑掉了。”他说。
他正要转身回机棚，一声爆炸的轰鸣震得整座建筑直颤。
“那是什么？”姑娘低声问。
两人走回峭壁尽头。爆炸声从哪个方向来，他用不着猜——两英里外一片红光，照亮了整个乡野。

===Original===
“Something has happened to the engine,” he said.
He did not know till an hour later that running at full speed the Atlantic had dashed into a down goods train and that the blaze he witnessed was the blaze of a burning petroleum tank which the wrecked Atlantic had crushed in its death flurry.
---
“We have not been able to recognize any of them,” said T. B. “Do you think Kate Westhanger was with them?”
“Kate Westhanger is no more,” said Michael gravely, and he spoke the truth for Kate Pretherston was at that moment on her way to France, where her husband intended joining her just as soon as his resignation was accepted.

===Chinese===
“机车出事了。”他说。
直到一小时后他才知道：全速行驶的“大西洋”号一头撞上了一列下行货车，他望见的那片烈焰，是倾覆的“大西洋”号在垂死挣扎中撞破的油罐燃起的大火。
---
“我们连一个人都没认出来，”T. B. 说，“你觉得凯特·韦斯特汉杰跟他们在一块儿吗？”
“凯特·韦斯特汉杰已经不存在了。”迈克尔正色道。他说的是实话，因为凯特·普雷瑟斯顿此刻正在赴法途中，只等辞呈一获批准，她丈夫就去与她会合。

===Original===
“But why give up the work, Michael?” said T. B.
“I found, sir,” said Michael, “that it was sapping my moral qualities.”
“Your moral qualities?” said his puzzled chief. “I didn’t know that you had any. What particular form did the sapping take?”
“I found, sir,” said Michael, “that I was developing a fondness for the criminal classes.”

===Chinese===
“可你为什么放弃这份工作，迈克尔？”T. B. 问。
“我发现，长官，”迈克尔说，“它正在消磨我的道德品质。”
“你的道德品质？”上司一脸困惑，“我都不知道你有这东西。具体是怎么个消磨法？”
“我发现，长官，”迈克尔说，“我对犯罪阶层渐渐生出好感来了。”

## II Mike Said Nothing﻿—There Was Nothing to Say / 二 迈克一言不发——无话可说

===Original===
“You might do worse than lunch with me,” said Michael Pretherston.
He stood outside Felton House with the girl whose belongings in one small Gladstone bag had been deposited on the curb, pending the arrival of a taxicab.
“Why should I lunch with you?” she asked insolently. “I thought you were going to pinch me.”
“Your vulgarity is appalling!” said Michael, shaking his head in reproof. “I cannot pinch you in the vulgar sense. I have no desire to perform that operation in the corporeal sense. You had better compromise and lunch with me.”
The girl hesitated.
“Think of my reputation,” she said.

===Chinese===
“跟我吃顿午饭，也算不得委屈你。”迈克尔·普雷瑟斯顿说。
他和那姑娘一起站在费尔顿宅邸外，她的全部行装——一只小旅行提包——已经搁在路缘上，专等出租车的到来。
“我凭什么跟你吃午饭？”她没好气地问，“我还以为你要抓我呢。”
“你这话粗俗得吓人！”迈克尔摇头表示责备，“我没法以‘捏’的本义捏你，也没有从肉体意义上执行该项操作的欲望。你不如折中一下，跟我吃顿午饭。”
姑娘犹豫了一下。
“想想我的名声。”她说。

===Original===
“Thoughts of your reputation keep me awake at night,” answered Michael lightly and called a taxi.
They found a little restaurant in Soho and in an underground cellar where the bad ventilation was compensated for by a blaze of light, they ate their simple meal.
“Now, Kate, I want to ask you what your little game is,” said Michael; “and I need the information because I know it isn’t a little game.”
“I was scared sick over those pearls,” said the girl, ignoring the question. “It would have been horrible bad luck to have been taken for a job I had nothing to do with and such a paltry job, too!”
“You owe me something,” said Michael.
“I owe you more than I can ever repay you,” said the girl significantly.

===Chinese===
“你的名声可让我夜夜睡不着觉。”迈克尔轻飘飘地答道，随手唤了辆出租车。
他们在索霍找了家小馆子，在一间地下酒窖里，通风不良的缺陷被一片灯火通明补偿了回来，两人吃了顿便饭。
“好了，凯特，我想问问你葫芦里卖的什么药，”迈克尔说，“我需要这个情报，因为我知道那可不是小打小闹。”
“那些珍珠可把我吓了个半死，”姑娘答非所问地说，“为一件跟我毫不相干的活儿被抓，那运气就糟透了——何况还是那么不上台面的一件小活儿！”
“你欠我点人情。”迈克尔说。
“我欠你的，一辈子也还不清。”姑娘话里有话地说。

===Original===
“I suppose one of these days,” suggested the detective after an interval of thought, “you will instruct some of your hired pals, Gregori or the Colonel or little Stockmar, to inflict on me a painful injury.”
“You!” said the girl scornfully. “If there were not men like you in the police we should have been destroyed years ago! You are a sort of an insurance scheme and it pays us to keep you alive and well. Why, Crime Street would go into mourning the day you were buried.”
“You are not trying to be rude to me, are you?” he asked.
She looked at him slyly from under her long lashes and her eyes were dancing with fun.
“Why do you think I went to Lord Flanborough?” she asked.
He shook his head.

===Chinese===
“我猜用不了多久，”侦探思忖片刻后说，“你就会指使你那帮花钱雇来的伙伴——格雷戈里先生、上校，或者小斯托克马尔——叫我吃一番皮肉之苦。”
“就凭你！”姑娘不屑地说，“要是警界里没有你这样的人，我们早几年就完蛋了！你就像一份保险，让你活着、活得健康，对我们才划算。哼，你下葬那天，犯罪街全街都要戴孝。”
“你这不是存心对我无礼吧？”他问。
她从长长的睫毛下狡黠地瞟着他，眼睛里满是笑意。
“你猜我为什么要去找弗兰伯勒勋爵？”她问。
他摇摇头。

===Original===
“I’m blessed if I know,” he confessed. “Of course, I knew it was you the moment I heard of the rapid typewriting and the Italian songs. Now listen: I am not trying to speak to you for your good.﻿ ﻿…”
“Don’t!” she said laconically.
“But I have often wondered why a well-educated girl and a nice girl, as far as I know to the contrary, should prefer the life of a crook to.﻿ ﻿…”
“To earning £2 or £3 a week and working all day to earn it,” she finished for him; “to living my life in one little room on a top floor in Bloomsbury, waiting my turn every morning for my bath. To being made love to by the assistant manager and sacrificing my immortal soul for a half-a-crown dinner and a bottle of red wine! It is funny, isn’t it! I have had the experience for professional purposes and I don’t like it a bit, Mike.”
She looked at him straight in the eyes. She had dropped her air of flippancy, her slang; the voice that spoke was not to be distinguished from that of any other gentlewoman.
“You see, a woman is differently circumstanced to a man. She wants nice things and her attitude toward life, and indeed the whole of her conduct, depends entirely upon the degree of niceness she requires. Men don’t do things for women for nothing. They lend to their men friends all the money in the world and are grateful if they get it back. They expect nothing more than their money and are surprised when they get it. But if I were a typist in a city office and I borrowed £2 from the assistant manager or from the chief bookkeeper or a fiver from one of the partners, why, Mike, I should be booked for supper on Wednesday. Men want more from women than a quid pro quo; they want two quid pro quo. In return for the £2 I borrowed, I should pay interest well outside the range of the multiplication table. Suppose a man lent you £2 and asked you in exchange, not only to repay the money, but to renounce all your dearest principles for the sake of the loan; if he asked you to betray your friends, where you had been loyal to them, and lie, where you had been truthful; break your word where you had been faithful, be a thief where you had been honest? Would you surrender every reticence, every honourable instinct, every precious faith?”

===Chinese===
“我要是知道才见鬼了。”他承认道，“当然，一听说那手飞快的打字和意大利歌儿，我就知道是你。听着：我并不打算对你进行规劝，为你好。……
“免了！”她言简意赅地说。
“可我常常纳闷，一个受过良好教育的姑娘，一个——就我所知并无相反迹象——的好姑娘，怎么会宁可过骗子的日子，而不肯……”
“不肯挣一星期两三英镑、从早忙到晚地去挣，”她替他说完，“不肯住在布鲁姆斯伯里顶楼的一间小屋里，每天早晨排队等着轮自己洗澡。不肯被副经理纠缠，为了半克朗的一顿晚饭和一瓶红酒出卖我不朽的灵魂！好笑吧！这种日子我出于职业需要体验过，一点儿也不喜欢，迈克。”
她直视着他的眼睛。她卸下了轻浮的神气和满口俚语，说话的嗓音跟任何一位大家闺秀别无二致。
“你要明白，女人的处境和男人不同。她想要好东西，而她对生活的态度，乃至全部的行事，完全取决于她要好东西要到什么程度。男人替女人做事从来不是白做的。他们可以把世上所有的钱都借给同性朋友，钱能收回就算感恩戴德。他们指望的不过是自己的钱，钱回来了还要惊讶一番。可我若是城里某个办公室的打字员，向副经理或总账房借两英镑，或者向哪位合伙人借张五镑票子，那么，迈克，星期三的晚饭就给我订上了。男人要女人的，不止是一报还一报；他们要的是两报还一报。为借来的两英镑，我该付的利息会远远超出乘法口诀表的范围。假如有人借给你两英镑，要求的不光是还钱，还要你为了这笔借款抛弃你最珍视的一切原则；假如他要求你出卖一向忠诚相待的朋友，在从来诚实的地方撒谎；在从未失信的地方食言，在一直清白的地方做贼？你可肯交出每一分矜持、每一种荣誉的本能、每一项珍贵的信念？”

===Original===
Mike said nothing. For there was nothing to say. He paid the bill and escorted the girl to a cab.
“I am not going to be sorry for you,” he said; “you are having The Life. One of these days I shall come along and take you; but I shall hate it. Hop in, Kate!”
Kate literally hopped into the waiting taxi, waved her hand in farewell and was gone.
Michael Pretherston stood for fully five minutes on the edge of the pavement, meditating upon what the girl had said. She had struck a responsive note in his soul, for she spoke no more than was the truth, as he knew.
He went, a little sadly, back to headquarters, remembering en route that he had forgotten to write the report. Should he go back to the Yard and compose it from memory or should he return to the unsympathetic atmosphere of Felton House? He decided upon the latter and surprised Lord Flanborough in the act of taking an afternoon nap. Michael was full of apologies and was so unusually respectful that his lordship forgot to be annoyed.
“Moya’s out,” he explained.

===Chinese===
迈克一言不发。因为无话可说。他付了账，把姑娘送上出租车。
“我不打算可怜你，”他说，“你正过着‘那种生活’。哪一天我会来把你捉走；不过到时候我会恨这差事。上车吧，凯特！”
凯特真就一蹦上了待客的出租车，挥手作别，一溜烟走了。
迈克尔·普雷瑟斯顿在人行道边足足站了五分钟，回味着姑娘方才的话。她拨响了他心底一根共鸣的弦，因为正如他所知，她说的句句是实情。
他有点闷闷地回到总部，路上才想起忘了写报告。是回伦敦警察厅凭记忆赶出来，还是回到费尔顿宅邸那冷淡的气氛里去？他选了后者，正撞见弗兰伯勒勋爵在午睡。迈克尔连声道歉，又破天荒地恭恭敬敬，勋爵大人竟忘了动气。
“莫亚出门了。”勋爵解释道。

===Original===
“I will endeavour to bear up,” replied Michael, seating himself at his lordship’s desk and preparing to take a note of the circumstances which had led to his lordship’s call for assistance. He finished the report, blotted and folded it and placed the document in his pocket.
“I only want to ask you one or two questions and they concern Kate﻿—or Miss Tenby, as you call her. I’m afraid I gave you a shock this morning.”
“It was certainly a surprise,” admitted Lord Flanborough cautiously; “who is this Kate? We have made a very careful search of the house but nothing is missing so far as we can tell.”
Michael laughed.
“You needn’t worry about that. Kate is not a pilferer. Her real name is Katharine Westhanger; they call her Kate and she is the Colonel’s niece. Her age is eighteen or nineteen, and from a child she has been brought up to regard the world as her oyster. Her mother was a wholesome parson’s daughter, her father was a rascal who was kicked out of the army in ’89 for an offence against the Law of Property. Her maternal grandfather was General Sir Shaun Masserfield, the greatest strategist the British army has ever held﻿—Kate inherits his genius but has not learnt his code. Her father died when she was a child and her uncle, who is a greater scoundrel than her father was﻿—the family on the Westhanger side has a criminal history which goes back at intervals for two hundred years﻿—completed her education. Kate has been brought up to be a thief, but a big thief. She is, I believe, the brains of the biggest criminal organisation in the world. Every member of the gang has been taken, but no evidence has ever been offered against Kate. She plans the big swindles and each one is bigger than the last﻿—but never once have we traced the offence to her door.”
“Why is it that the police﻿—?” began Lord Flanborough.

===Chinese===
“我一定尽力挺住。”迈克尔回答，在勋爵的书桌旁坐下，准备记录勋爵大人求援的前后情由。他写完报告，吸干墨迹，折好，把文书放进口袋。
“我只想问你一两个问题，是关于凯特的——就是你们口中的滕比小姐。恐怕今天早上我吓着你了。”
“确实出乎意料。”弗兰伯勒勋爵谨慎地承认，“这个凯特到底是谁？我们把宅子仔仔细细搜了一遍，就我们所知并没短了什么东西。”
迈克尔笑了。
“这你不必担心。凯特不是小偷小摸的人。她的真名是凯瑟琳·韦斯特汉杰，大家都叫她凯特，是上校的侄女。年纪十八九岁，从小被教养得认定世界任她予取予求。她母亲是位品行端正的教区牧师之女，父亲是个恶棍，’89 年因触犯财产法被军队开除。她的外祖父是肖恩·马瑟菲尔德爵士上将，英军有史以来最了不起的战略家——凯特继承了他的天才，却没学到他的操守。她幼年丧父，她的叔叔——比她父亲更大的恶棍，韦斯特汉杰这一支的家族犯罪史断断续续有两百年——完成了对她的教育。凯特是被当作贼养大的，不过是大贼。我相信，她是世界上最大犯罪组织的智囊。团伙成员尽数落过网，可从来没有拿出过一件针对凯特的证据。那些惊天骗局都出自她的策划，一桩比一桩大——可我们一次也没能把罪案追到她门上。”
“那警察为什么——？”弗兰伯勒勋爵开口道。

===Original===
“The police, my dear Flanborough,” said Michael wearily, “are human beings who have to deal with human beings. They are not angels, nor thought readers, nor are they clairvoyant. The laws of this country are so framed that the criminal has six chances to every one possessed by his enemy. We know Kate was concerned in that big bank smashing exploit which took two million crowns from the treasury of the Bank of Holland. It was Kate who organised the raid upon the London jewellers in June of last year. Kate is the mother of Crime Street. You don’t know that thoroughfare, but one of these days I’ll introduce you to it, if you are curious﻿—but I warn you that if you expect to steep your soul in sordidness, you will be disappointed﻿—it is the most respectable street in London. Her ingenuity is remarkable, her patience beyond praise, and that is partly why I have come back: I want to know why she was here and what she was doing?”
“As I say﻿ ﻿…” began Lord Flanborough again.
“For Heaven’s sake,” interrupted Michael, “don’t tell me that you haven’t missed things! I tell you Kate would not touch a pin in your house. In the first place she is a well-off woman. Why in Heaven’s name should she bother her head about your belongings? I don’t suppose, if she had the full run of your house, she could find £100 worth of realisable property! No, that is not why Kate came to you. How long has she been here?”
“Nearly a month,” said Lord Flanborough, a little annoyed that the result of his own private investigations had so utterly failed to impress a representative of Scotland Yard.
“What work has she been doing?”
“Ordinary secretarial work for Moya. She came with excellent letters of recommendation.”

===Chinese===
“警察嘛，我亲爱的弗兰伯勒，”迈克尔倦倦地说，“也是人，也得跟人打交道。他们不是天使，不会读心，也没有天眼。这个国家的法律制定得让罪犯占六分便宜，他的对手只有一分。我们知道凯特跟那桩砸银行的大手笔有牵连，从荷兰银行的金库卷走两百万克朗。去年六月伦敦珠宝行遭劫，也是凯特组织的。凯特是犯罪街之母。你不知道那条街，哪天你要好奇，我领你去见识见识——不过我警告你，你要是打算让灵魂在那儿泡一泡卑污，准会失望——那是全伦敦最体面的一条街。她的巧思令人惊叹，她的耐心无可挑剔，这也多少是我回来的原因：我想知道她到这儿来干什么，图的是什么？”
“就像我说的……”弗兰伯勒勋爵又开口。
“看在老天的份上，”迈克尔打断他，“别跟我说你没丢东西！我告诉你，凯特连你家一根针都不会碰。第一，她是个有钱的女人。她究竟为什么要为你那点家当操心？我看，就算让她在整座宅子里随便翻，她也找不出值 100 英镑的可变现财物！不，凯特投到你这儿来不是为了这个。她来了多久？”
“快一个月了。”弗兰伯勒勋爵说，有点恼火，自己私下调查的成果竟丝毫没能打动苏格兰场这位代表。
“她都做了些什么？”
“给莫亚做些寻常的秘书工作。她来的时候带着顶好的推荐信。”

===Original===
“You can forget those,” interrupted Michael testily; “the gentleman who wrote them lives at No. 9, Crime Street and his name is Millet.”
“She was a wonderful typist,” began his lordship, who was seeking about in his own mind for some excuse which would explain why he had been deceived.
“That I also know. She is, as you say, one of the fastest typists in the world. In fact, no aspect of her education has been neglected. She speaks five languages and read French fluently when she was nine. What work has she done for you?”
Lord Flanborough considered for a while.
“She has copied a few letters and reports.”
“What kind of reports?”

===Chinese===
“那些信你可以忘掉，”迈克尔不耐烦地打断，“写推荐信的那位先生住在犯罪街 9 号，名叫米利特。”
“她的打字本领可了不得，”勋爵开口道，心里正搜罗着说辞，好解释自己何以受骗。
“这我也知道。她确实如你所言，是世上打字最快的人之一。事实上，她的教育没有一方面被荒疏。她会说五种语言，九岁就能流利阅读法文。她替你做了什么工作？”
弗兰伯勒勋爵寻思了一会儿。
“她抄写了一些信件和报告。”
“什么样的报告？”

===Original===
“Reports from our South African companies. You see, Michael, I still retain the direction of most of my old interests.”
“Were they very important﻿—the reports, I mean?”
“Yes and no,” replied Lord Flanborough slowly; “they were merely records of output, cost of production and projected shipments.”
“On what other work was she employed?”
“Let me think,” said Lord Flanborough.
“I *am* letting you!” replied Michael tartly. “You used to have a very private code-book if I remember rightly.”

===Chinese===
“我们南非那些公司的报告。你要知道，迈克尔，我大部分老产业的经营权还捏在手里。”
“那些报告要紧吗——我是说报告？”
“要紧也不要紧。”弗兰伯勒勋爵慢吞吞地答道，“不过是些产量、生产成本和发运计划的记录。”
“她还干过别的什么？”
“容我想想。”弗兰伯勒勋爵说。
“我正是在让你想！”迈克尔尖刻地答道，“如果我没记错，你从前有一本极其机密的密码本。”

===Original===
“That is true,” said Lord Flanborough, “but of course, she did not see that.”
“Where did you keep it?”
“In my desk,” said Lord Flanborough.
“Is it possible that she could have seen it?”
“It is possible, but wholly impossible that she could have copied it.”
“For how long a time together was she left alone?”

===Chinese===
“那是有的，”弗兰伯勒勋爵说，“不过她当然没有见过。”
“你搁在哪儿？”
“搁在我的书桌里。”弗兰伯勒勋爵说。
“她有没有可能见到过？”
“见有可能，但要抄走是绝无可能。”
“她独自一人被留在那儿，最长一次有多久？”

===Original===
“Five minutes was the longest period she was left in the library alone,” said his lordship after consideration.
Michael fingered his chin.
“Did you ever come into the library and find her in a semi-fainting condition?” he asked.
Lord Flanborough looked at him with open-mouthed amazement.
“Did she tell you?”
Michael shook his head.

===Chinese===
“她单独待在图书室，最长的一次是五分钟。”勋爵考虑之后说。
迈克尔摩挲着下巴。
“你可曾走进图书室，发现她处于半昏厥的状态？”他问。
弗兰伯勒勋爵目瞪口呆地望着他。
“她告诉你的？”
迈克尔摇摇头。

===Original===
“No, she has told me nothing. I gather from your question that there was such an occurrence?”
“It is remarkable that you should ask the question,” said his lordship. “I *did* come in one morning to find the poor girl﻿—er, the wretched girl, in a semi-fainting condition.”
“And you went out and got her a glass of water and sent for your housekeeper, I suppose,” said Michael, his lip curling.
“Yes, I did,” admitted his lordship.
“Which means, in plain language,” smiled Michael, “that you surprised her in the act of examining some of your private documents and that whilst you were getting the water and calling assistance, she was replacing whatever she was looking at where she had found it. Did she on any other occasion draw your attention, on your entering the room, to some peculiar circumstance, such as one of the pictures not hanging straight or a broken vase?”
Again Lord Flanborough looked astounded.

===Chinese===
“没有，她什么都没对我说。从你这一问，我推测确有其事？”
“亏你问得这么巧。”勋爵大人说，“有一天早晨我进去，倒真发现那可怜的姑娘——呃，那可恶的姑娘——半昏半厥的。”
“于是你就出去倒了杯水，又打发人叫管家太太，我猜得对吧。”迈克尔说，嘴角挂着讥诮。
“对，我是这么办的。”勋爵大人承认。
“用大白话说，”迈克尔微笑道，“就是撞见她正在翻你的某些私人文件，而你出去取水、叫人的当口，她正好把看过的东西原样放回原处。除此之外，可还有别的什么场合，你一进屋，她就引着你去注意什么异样的事情——比如哪幅画挂歪了，或者花瓶碎了？”
弗兰伯勒勋爵又露出骇然之色。

===Original===
“Yes, once she pointed to the china cupboard and asked me who cracked the glass. As a matter of fact, the glass was not cracked at all,” he explained.
“But you went over and examined it?”
“Naturally,” said his lordship.
“That was exactly the same trick,” said Michael; “whilst you were making your inspection she was able to replace any documents she had been examining and close the drawer﻿—if they were in a drawer. Now, I wonder what her game is?”

===Chinese===
“有，有一回她指着瓷器柜，问我是谁把玻璃碰裂了。其实那玻璃根本没有裂。”他解释道。
“可你走过去查看了一番？”
“那自然。”勋爵大人说。
“这正是同一路的把戏，”迈克尔说，“你查验的时候，她正好可以从容把她翻过的文件放回去，再关上抽屉——如果文件在抽屉里的话。如今我倒要琢磨琢磨，她玩的是哪一出？”

===Original===
“You don’t suggest,” began his lordship in alarm, “that she is scheming to rob me?”
“I hope not,” said Michael gravely; “from the idea of your being robbed, the imagination reels.”
“I wish you wouldn’t be so sarcastic. I am afraid you have never quite forgiven Moya﻿—”
“I bless Moya every time I think of her,” said Michael quickly; “she rendered me the greatest service that one human being can render to another, when she refused me. I hope to do better than Moya. As Moya’s father, you utter a pained protest. I know, I know,” said Michael, and he waved his hand cheerfully from the door.

===Chinese===
“你该不是想说，”勋爵大人惊慌地开口，“她在密谋抢我？”
“但愿不是，”迈克尔一本正经地说，“一想到你遭抢劫的场面，想象力都会打晃。”
“拜托你别这么挖苦人。我看你对莫亚到现在也没消气——”
“我一想起莫亚就要为她祝福，”迈克尔赶紧说，“她拒绝了我，这是一个人能给另一个人的最大的恩惠。我希望娶个比莫亚更好的。作为莫亚的父亲，你这就要发出痛心的抗议了。我知道，我知道。”迈克尔说着，在门口愉快地挥了挥手。

## XVI On the Unmorality of Professional Thieves / 十六 论职业盗贼的非道德性

===Original===
The main building of what had once been Boltover’s Cement Works consisted of four high walls and a slate roof. Here had stood the wash mills and the revolving knives which had reduced the clay and mud from the nearby river into slurry. Leading therefrom was the heating chamber and the kiln house. There was no trace of mill, though the kilns still stood.
All the machinery had been removed, the concrete floor strengthened and the only engine visible was a great Atlantic locomotive which had stood with steam up day and night before the wreckage of two trucks. In each of these was a rough circular hole and the blistered paint and the drops of metal which hung upon the edge or had trickled down its blackened side, told of the terrific heat which had been employed to break through the steel walls.
Near one wall were a number of small packages neatly stitched in canvas and ready for removal, and on these sat Mr. Mulberry, the benignity of whose countenance was somewhat discounted by the fact that a loaded rifle lay across his knees. Leading from the main building was a small office approached through a steel door and in this were seated the seven guiding spirits of the great raid, Francis Stockmar, Gregori, Colonel Westhanger, Colling Jacques, Thomas Stockmar, Mr. Cunningham and Kate.
Gregori was talking. He leant across the table, his hands lightly clasped, his head on one side turned to the girl who sat opposite to him and a little to his right.
“I think, Kate, we finish here,” he was saying. “Crime Street is getting a little too warm.”

===Chinese===
博尔托弗水泥厂旧址的主厂房，只剩四面高墙和一顶石板瓦屋顶。这里曾安装着淘泥机和旋转刀具，把附近河里挖来的黏土和泥浆捣成浆液。再往里连着加热室和窑房。磨机已无影无踪，窑倒还立着。
机器设备已尽数搬走，水泥地面做了加固，唯一见得到的引擎，是一台庞大的“大西洋”式机车，昼夜烧着汽，停在两节货车残骸前。车厢上各开着一个粗糙的圆洞，鼓泡脱落的油漆、挂在洞沿或顺着烧黑的厢壁淌下的一滴滴金属，都在诉说当初为烧穿这钢壁动用了何等骇人的高热。
靠着一面墙堆着若干帆布缝得整整齐齐、随时可以搬走的小包裹，马尔伯里先生就坐在包裹上，只是膝上横着一支上了膛的步枪，脸上那副慈祥相便大打了折扣。主厂房连着一间小办公室，要穿过一道铁门进去，里面坐着这场大劫案的七位主脑：弗朗西斯·斯托克马尔、格雷戈里先生、韦斯特汉杰上校、科林·雅克、托马斯·斯托克马尔、坎宁安先生和凯特。
格雷戈里先生正在说话。他俯身向着桌子，两手轻轻交握，头侧向坐在他对面偏右一点的那位姑娘。
“我看，凯特，咱们到这儿就收场吧。”他说，“犯罪街那边有点太热了。”

===Original===
“I didn’t expect you to lose your nerve,” she said.
“I’m not losing my nerve,” he said with a scowl. “I am afraid of losing my life, if you want to know the truth. We are watched all the time. They know you are out of town and are searching for you.”
“They found me,” said the girl coolly. “I am staying at Brighton.”
“We have made a big haul and it will take us a year to get rid of it,” Gregori went on, “but when we *have* got rid of it, we shall have enough to settle down.”
“But why do you want to settle down?” she asked.
“My dear Kate,” said her uncle querulously, “don’t ask absurd questions. You know there is no reason in the world why we should not settle down. We have enough money.”

===Chinese===
“我没想到你会吓得没了胆气。”她说。
“我不是没了胆气，”他皱着眉说，“要说实话，我是怕丢了性命。我们随时被人盯着。他们知道你出了城，正在搜你。”
“他们已经找到我了，”姑娘淡淡地说，“我就住在布莱顿。”
“我们已经干了票大的，要脱手得花上一年工夫，”格雷戈里先生接着说，“不过等真脱了手，我们就够安顿下来了。”
“可你们为什么想安顿下来？”她问。
“亲爱的凯特，”她叔叔不耐烦地说，“别问这种荒唐问题。你很清楚，这世上没有任何理由能拦着我们安顿下来。钱已经够了。”

===Original===
“Exactly what do you mean by settling down?” she insisted. “I am not being sarcastic. I merely want information. You have taught me that it is the game and not the prize that is worth while. That has been my life’s teaching. Why, you told me if you were a millionaire,” she looked at her uncle under her bent brows, “nothing would induce you to be ‘dull and honest.’ Those were your words.”
“My dear child,” said Colonel Westhanger, “I have told you lots of things which have to be interpreted in a liberal spirit. We have had all the fun we want and now we will﻿—”
He was at a loss in his desire to avoid a tautological repetition of a certain phrase.
“Settle down,” she suggested; “be dull and honest?”
“But, surely, Kate,” said Gregori impatiently, “you don’t want to be a hunted beast all your life?”
“Why not?” she asked in astonishment. “It is just as much fun being hunted as hunting. You have said that a score of times. Does Michael Pretherston﻿—”

===Chinese===
“你说的安顿下来，究竟是什么意思？”她追问，“我不是挖苦人，我只是想弄明白。你教过我：值得要的是这局游戏，不是那份彩头。这是我受的一辈子的教育。对了，你说过，你要是当了百万富翁，”她垂着眼帘看叔叔，“什么也诱使不了你去‘又乏味又老实’。这是你的原话。”
“亲爱的孩子，”韦斯特汉杰上校说，“我讲过的许多话，得本着宽宏的精神去理解。我们要的乐子已经享够了，如今咱们要——”
他一时语塞，一心想避开对某个词组的同义反复。
“安顿下来，”她替他说完，“做个又乏味又老实的人？”
“不过，说真的，凯特，”格雷戈里先生不耐烦地说，“你总不想一辈子当被人追猎的野兽吧？”
“为什么不想？”她讶然反问，“被追猎跟追猎一样有乐趣。这话你说过二十遍了。迈克尔·普雷瑟斯顿他——”

===Original===
“Oh, hang Michael Pretherston,” said Gregori.
“Does Michael Pretherston,” she went on, “get as much fun out of chasing me, as I get out of escaping him? Does Michael Pretherston find the same exhilaration of mind in following on my tracks as I find in keeping ahead of him?”
“Anyway,” said Gregori. “I have had enough of it and I want to go out of the business and I advise you to do the same. And there is another thing, Kate﻿—”
He looked at the Colonel for support, but Colonel Westhanger found it convenient at that moment to be staring at the skylight.
“What is the other thing?” she asked.
“Well, you know I am fond of you,” he said, “and I want to﻿—” he floundered.

===Chinese===
“噢，让迈克尔·普雷瑟斯顿见鬼去吧。”格雷戈里先生说。
“迈克尔·普雷瑟斯顿追我，”她继续说，“能像我甩他那样得到乐趣吗？迈克尔·普雷瑟斯顿循着我的踪迹穷追，能像我抢在他前头那样心花怒放吗？”
“总之，”格雷戈里先生说，“我是干够了，我要退出这一行，也劝你照办。还有一件事，凯特——”
他向上校投去求助的一瞥，可韦斯特汉杰上校偏巧在这一刻专注于天窗。
“另一件事是什么？”她问。
“呃，你知道我喜欢你，”他说，“我想——”他支吾起来。

===Original===
“Settle down,” she suggested innocently; “what is all this ‘settling down’ that everybody loves so much? Does it mean we shall never plan another great coup?” She leant her elbows on the table. “Honestly, I am not being wilfully dense. I know money is useful, because it helps one to prepare the way for making more money, but I have not been in this,” she waved her hand, “in all these things for money. I told Michael Pretherston so and he believed me.”
“What have you been telling Michael Pretherston?” asked Gregori suspiciously.
“I told him that,” she said simply.
“But, my dear girl,” said her uncle, “fun and excitement and all that sort of thing are well enough in their way, but you don’t mean to tell me, at this hour, that you have not been working for the ‘stuff’?”
“I will tell you as much at this or any other hour,” she answered immediately.
“I see,” said Gregori with a faint smile, “then really you are what I would call a criminal artist﻿—art for art’s sake, eh?”

===Chinese===
“安顿下来，”她无辜地接话，“这人人宠爱的‘安顿下来’到底是怎么回事？是不是说，我们从此再也不能策划一场漂亮的大行动了？”她把胳膊肘支在桌上，“说正经的，我不是存心装糊涂。我知道钱有用，因为它能帮人铺路去赚更多的钱，可我卷进这个，”她一挥手，“卷进这所有的事情里，从来不是为了钱。我这样对迈克尔·普雷瑟斯顿说过，他信了我。”
“你都跟迈克尔·普雷瑟斯顿说了些什么？”格雷戈里先生疑心地问。
“我就把这些告诉了他。”她答得干脆。
“可是，亲爱的姑娘，”她叔叔说，“玩乐啊刺激啊这一类东西，自有其好处，只是事到如今，你总不至于要告诉我，你一直不是为‘那玩意儿’干的吧？”
“此时也罢，任何时候也罢，我都可以这样告诉你。”她当即回答。
“我明白了，”格雷戈里先生淡淡一笑，“那你就真是我所说的那种犯罪艺术家——为艺术而艺术，嗯？”

===Original===
“I mean that,” she said again. “One must not judge one’s successes by the amount of money one has made.”
“That is how I joodge it,” said the thick voice of Francis Stockmar; “so much mooney, so much sugsess, isn’t it?”
“I tell you frankly,” said Gregori. “I am in this for the money and so is your uncle. We have taken many risks, some of us have been caught and some of us,” he said significantly, “have been lucky. I’ve got thirty years in front of me, with any luck, and so I am going to﻿—”
“Settle down,” suggested Kate ironically.
“I am going to quit.”
“Come, come, be sensible, Kate,” said the Colonel, patting her on the shoulder. “You have been a very good girl and we owe you almost everything we have. I am sure everyone agrees that you have been the brains of our﻿—er﻿—association. The only time when any of us have been caught is when we have gone out on a side line of our own. Now leave well alone.”

===Chinese===
“我就是这个意思。”她又说了一遍，“衡量成败，不能看挣了多少钱。”
“俺就是这么看的，”弗朗西斯·斯托克马尔瓮声瓮气地说，“多多少钱，就多多成功，不斯吗？”
“坦白告诉你，”格雷戈里先生说，“我是为钱干这一行的，你叔叔也是。我们冒过许多险，有人栽过跟头，也有人，”他意味深长地说，“运气好。运气不坏的话，我还有三十年好活，所以我打算——”
“安顿下来。”凯特讥诮地替他接上。
“我打算金盆洗手。”
“好了好了，讲点道理，凯特，”上校拍拍她的肩膀，“你一直是个好姑娘，我们如今的家当几乎全亏了你。我敢说大家都承认，你一直是我们这个——呃——协会的智囊。我们中间唯一失手的几次，都是各人自己出去单干岔了道。如今见好就收吧。”

===Original===
“When hunters have caught the fox,” she said, “do they leave well alone and never hunt again? In war, when a soldier comes through a battle safely, does he leave well alone and never go into action again? Does the huntsman who is nearly caught by a lion leave well, and lions, alone?”
“This is different,” said her uncle doggedly.
“But I don’t understand it. If what you say is right, then I am wrong and have been wrong all my life. I am wrong and the police are right.”
“Of course, they’re right,” said Gregori; “what rubbish you are talking.”
“The police are right?” she asked in open-eyed astonishment.
“Of course they are right. They must protect society. In five years’ time, when I am settled on my little estate in Spain and my house is burgled do you imagine I shall not call in the police?”

===Chinese===
“猎人抓到过狐狸之后，”她说，“难道就见好就收，从此不再打猎？打仗时，士兵平安打完一仗，难道就见好就收，从此不再上阵？猎人险些被狮子逮住，难道就见好就收，连狮子也不碰了？”
“这回不一样。”她叔叔固执地说。
“可我不明白。要是你们说得对，那我就是错的，一辈子都错了。我错了，警察倒对了。”
“他们当然是对的，”格雷戈里先生说，“你这是说的什么胡话。”
“警察是对的？”她睁大眼睛，满脸惊讶地问。
“他们当然对。他们必须保护社会。过五年，等我在西班牙的小庄园里安顿下来，家里遭了贼，你难道以为我不会去报警？”

===Original===
“I know they are right in their way,” she said, as if she were speaking her thoughts aloud, “but we are right, too.”
“We cannot both be right,” said Colonel Westhanger.
“I asked you some time ago,” she said, turning to him, “which was the better life﻿—the dull life or ours. They cannot both be better. The elementary conditions cannot change. That life must be the best, or ours.”
“That life is best,” said the Colonel decisively.
She looked at him steadily.
“Then why have you let me live this?” she asked. “You cannot change me. I cannot change. I cannot!” she said with vehemence and the men noted with amazement the emotion she displayed. “Nothing can change me!”

===Chinese===
“我知道他们有他们的道理，”她说，像是在把心里话讲出声来，“可我们也有我们的道理。”
“我们不能两边都对。”韦斯特汉杰上校说。
“我从前问过你，”她转过身向着上校，“哪种日子更好——乏味的日子，还是我们的日子。不可能两种都更好。根本的道理不会变。要么那种生活最好，要么我们的最好。”
“那种生活最好。”上校斩钉截铁地说。
她定定地看着他。
“那你为什么让我过这种日子？”她问，“你们改变不了我。我变不了。变不了！”她说得激烈，男人们都惊异地注意到她流露出的情感。“什么都改变不了我！”

===Original===
Gregori reached out and took her hand, but she snatched it away.
“I will tell you what can change you, little girl,” he said undeterred by the rebuff, “love can change you. Give me a chance.”
She looked at him and laughed in his face.
“Will you be good or bad, honest or dishonest? You will only be a half man, living two lives. Marry you! And am I to go into witness boxes to testify against your burglar? And prosecute your poachers? I am living now, what I believe to be the truth. I believe I have the right to match my wits against the world and take, by my intelligence, what the old robber barons took by brutal strength. If I pass to the other side I should be a liar, living a life in which I did not believe. I am going on.”
“Then you will go on by yourself.”
“Will I?” she asked softly.

===Chinese===
格雷戈里先生伸手去握她的手，却被她一把抽开。
“我告诉你什么能改变你，小姑娘，”他碰了钉子仍不气馁，“爱情能改变你。给我一个机会。”
她看着他，当面笑了起来。
“你会学好还是学坏，诚实还是不诚实？你只会做个半拉子人，过两重日子。嫁给你！难道要我日后站上证人席，指证偷你家的贼？起诉到你庄园偷猎的人？我如今过的，是我信以为真的日子。我相信我有权利拿自己的智谋跟整个世界较量，凭智力去取从前强盗男爵们凭蛮力攫取的东西。我若是倒向另一边，我就成了说谎的人，过着我自己都不信的生活。我要继续下去。”
“那你就一个人干下去吧。”
“是吗？”她轻声问。

===Original===
“Go out and find somebody who thinks as you think if you can,” sneered Gregori; “you will be obliged to live a lie, anyway. You will never meet a man who believes in stealing, who believes in fraud and who will go on so believing, until he is an old man. You will never meet a man on the other side of life who would trust you if he knew you, and he *would* know you unless you﻿—went on lying.”
He laughed.
“You are in a cleft stick, my little friend, and if you take my tip you will stick to the friends who know you.”
He laughed again.
“Suppose I come down into Spain and burgle your house﻿—” her eyes lit up﻿—“and I would do it! Or, suppose, when you have﻿—settled down﻿—and when you have all deposited your symbols of success in your banks, I planned a little coup and smashed your banks? I could do it easily and I would do it,” she said. “What would you do?”
Their faces were a study. The Colonel was stroking his white moustache. Francis Stockmar was scowling horribly. Mr. Cunningham was staring blankly at the opposite wall.

===Chinese===
“有本事就出去找一个跟你想法一样的人，”格雷戈里先生讥笑道，“反正你将不得不靠谎言过活。你永远遇不到一个信奉偷窃、信奉欺诈、而且信到老死的男人。你也永远遇不到一个正道上的人，在识破你的底细后还信得过你——而他必然识破你，除非你——继续把谎撒下去。”
他笑了。
“你可是进退两难了，我的小朋友，听我一句劝，还是守住这些了解你的老朋友吧。”
他又笑了。
“比方说，我跑到西班牙去，把你家给偷了——”她眼睛一亮——“我真干得出来！再比方说，等你们——安顿下来——把你们的成功标志统统存进银行，我策划一场小小的行动，把你们的银行砸了？我轻而易举就能办到，也会去办，”她说，“你们会怎么样？”
几个人的脸色各有千秋。上校捋着他的白胡子。弗朗西斯·斯托克马尔阴沉地板着脸。坎宁安先生茫然瞪着对面的墙。

===Original===
“Naturally you would not play such a low-down trick upon your old friends,” said the Colonel soothingly; “nobody believes you would, Kate. I mean, it would be tragic for some of us, after spending years of our lives accumulating a little nest egg to find we had become beggars in a night. Of course, speaking personally, I should consider myself exonerated from any responsibility I had in regard to our relationship and I should have to tell the police﻿—”
“You would call the police, too, would you? Would you, Stockmar?”
“Yas,” said the stolid Austrian, “of goorse. The mooney to recover, ain’t it?”
“And you?”
“I don’t think you would do anything so treacherous,” said Mr. Cunningham; “naturally, we would not take that sort of thing lying down.”
“Naturally,” said Colling Jacques, “the whole matter is this, when we go back to the respectable world and obey the laws, we, as citizens, are entitled to the protection which the laws give us.”

===Chinese===
“你自然不会对老朋友们使这种下三滥的招数，”上校打圆场说，“谁也不信你会，凯特。我是说，我们有些人耗了半辈子攒下一点养老钱，一夜之间沦为乞丐，那就太惨了。当然，就我个人而言，我自会认为我对咱们这层关系负的一切责任都已勾销，我就只好去报警——”
“你也会报警，是吗？你呢，斯托克马尔？”
“系啊，”木然的奥地利人说，“当燃。总得把钱追回来，不斯吗？”
“还有你？”
“我不信你会干出这么背信弃义的事，”坎宁安先生说，“不过真有那种事，我们自然也不会善罢甘休。”
“那自然，”科林·雅克说，“整件事就是这么回事：等我们回到体面人的世界，遵纪守法，我们作为公民，就有权享受法律给予公民的保护。”

===Original===
“I see. You are, so to speak, touching wood. The wood is the law.”
“That is it,” he said.
Kate got up and walked to the one window of the room and looked out upon the dreary yard with its tangle of twisted machinery, its rusted boilers, its chaos of rotting cement bags.
“Well, you can all do as you like,” she turned on them, “but I tell you this, that if you think you are going to﻿—settle down﻿—at my expense, and if you think I have been planning and scheming and playacting and lying in order that you might all become respected parish councillors, you have made a mistake. You talk about my friends, if you are my friends, God help me! There is one man in the world who is worth the whole crowd of you.”
She was interrupted by a crash as though a heavy body had been thrown against a door. Somebody fumbled with the lock and Gregori jumped up and threw it open. They half carried, half pushed a gagged and bound man through the doorway. Behind him peered the saturnine, malignant face of his captor, Doctor Garon.

===Chinese===
“我懂了。打个比方，你们是在摸着木头求保佑。这木头就是法律。”
“正是如此。”他说。
凯特起身走到屋里唯一的窗前，望向那荒凉的院子：绞成一团的扭曲机器、锈死的锅炉、乱堆着腐朽的水泥袋。
“好吧，你们爱怎么办就怎么办，”她猛地转身向着他们，“可我把话放在这儿：你们要是以为能踩着我——安顿下来，以为我筹划、密谋、演戏、撒谎，就为了让你们统统当上受人尊敬的教区议员，那你们就大错特错了。你们口口声声我的朋友——你们要是也算朋友，上帝保佑我！这世上有一个男人，抵得上你们这一整窝人。”
话音未落，只听哗啦一声巨响，像是沉重的人体撞在了门上。有人摸索着开锁，格雷戈里先生跳起来把门拉开。众人半抬半推，把一个堵着嘴、捆着绑的男人弄进门来。他身后探出抓他的人那张阴沉恶毒的脸——加龙医生。

===Original===
“Got him,” he said triumphantly.
“Who is it?” asked Gregori, staring at the half conscious man.
The girl did not ask. She went suddenly cold, for she knew it was Michael Pretherston.

===Chinese===
“抓到他了。”他得意洋洋地说。
“是谁？”格雷戈里先生盯着那个半昏迷的人问。
姑娘没有问。她周身倏地发冷，因为她知道，那是迈克尔·普雷瑟斯顿。

## III Other Eyes Watched Michael / 三 另一些眼睛注视着迈克尔

===Original===
Michael Pretherston was back at the Yard in time to catch his chief before he departed for the day.
Commissioner T. B. Smith, to whose recommendation this young scion of the aristocracy owed his promotion, was not helpful.
“If we took Kate on any charge it would not prevent the swindle going forward,” he said; “you may be sure she has mobilized all her resources and her little army is ready to the last button of the last gaiter. There is supposed to be a fellow watching her all the time, but he seems to have missed her rather cleverly. Anyway, I don’t think there is much to be gained from shadowing her, because she knows she is under observation and acts accordingly. But I have a word of advice to you, my young Hibernian friend, and that is to keep a sharp eye on your own precious life. Kate is afraid of you.”
“She didn’t give me that impression this afternoon,” said Michael sadly.
“Kate is a bluff; you mustn’t take any notice of what she says. You accept a friend’s advice and go very carefully to work. I am not so sure that you didn’t behave indiscreetly this afternoon.”
“That is impossible!” said Michael stoutly, and T. B. Smith laughed.

===Chinese===
迈克尔·普雷瑟斯顿赶回警察厅，正好在长官下班前逮住了他。
警察总监 T. B. 史密斯——这位贵族出身的年轻人多亏他的举荐才得升迁——却帮不上什么忙。
“就算我们随便安个罪名把凯特抓起来，也拦不住那桩骗局继续推进，”他说，“你可以放心，她已经把手头的一切都动员起来了，她那支小小的部队已是万事俱备，连最后一副绑腿的最后一颗扣子都扣好了。按说一直有个人盯她的梢，可她甩起他来相当利落。总之，我认为盯她的梢捞不到什么好处，因为她知道自己被监视，行事也就处处提防。不过我有一句忠告送给你，我年轻的爱尔兰朋友：把你自己的宝贝性命看紧点。凯特怕你。”
“今天下午她可没给我这个印象。”迈克尔悻悻地说。
“凯特惯会虚张声势；她说的话你千万别当真。听朋友一句劝，办事小心再小心。今天下午你有没有失于轻率，我可说不准。”
“那不可能！”迈克尔斩钉截铁地说，T. B. 史密斯笑了。

===Original===
“The thing to have done was not to have recognized her and to have kept her under observation, pursuing your enquiries in the usual way.”
“If you can suggest any method by which I could have prevented her from recognizing me and recognizing the fact that I recognized her I will admit that I was wrong,” and T. B. Smith agreed.
“You may be right,” he said; “anyway, look after yourself.”

===Chinese===
“当时上策是装作没认出她，把她置于监视之下，再按老规矩查你的案。”
“你要能想出什么法子，叫她既认不出我、又认不出‘我认出了她’这回事，我就认错。”T. B. 史密斯表示同意。
“也许你是对的，”他说，“总之，照顾好自己。”

===Original===
Michael promptly forgot his chief’s advice and spent his evening making a solitary reconnaissance of Crime Street. Crime Street does not appear upon any plan of London, but if you will look at any large survey of the Hampstead district, you will find in a somewhat irregular tangle of buildings within a stone’s throw of the Heath, a curious oval which is conspicuous on the plan, not only by its own symmetry but by the graceful lines of the thoroughfares which radiate therefrom.
This is Amberscombe Gardens. The centre of the oval is occupied by four houses, Numbers Two, Four, Six and Eight; the northern side of the gardens by five houses, Numbers One, Three, Five, Seven and Nine.
Into Amberscombe Gardens from the north run three roads, the first of which (opening into the oval between Numbers One and Three) being called The Approach; the second, dividing Numbers Five and Seven, called Bethburn Avenue; the third between Numbers Seven and Nine, Coleburn Avenue. On the south side of the oval the arrangement of the streets is very similar. Originally, the central space had been occupied by nine houses but these had been pulled down by the proprietors of the remaining four and a private garden, common to all four houses, had been laid out by the owners of these properties. So that on the southern side of the central oval, there were no buildings, but a wall bisected at regular intervals by plain garden doors which form such a common feature of London suburban residences.

===Chinese===
迈克尔转身就把长官的忠告忘了，独自把一晚上花在侦察犯罪街上。犯罪街在任何伦敦地图上都找不到，可你只要翻开汉普斯特德区任何一幅大测绘图，就会在离荒野公园一箭之地、纵横交错的楼群中间，发现一个古怪的椭圆形。它在图上非常显眼，不光因为它自身匀称，还因为从它辐射出去的条条街道线条优雅。
这就是安伯斯库姆花园。椭圆形的中心是四座房子：2 号、4 号、6 号、8 号；花园北侧是五座：1 号、3 号、5 号、7 号、9 号。
从北面通进安伯斯库姆花园的有三条路：第一条（在 1 号和 3 号之间开进椭圆）名叫“引道”；第二条隔开 5 号和 7 号，叫贝思伯恩大道；第三条在 7 号和 9 号之间，叫科尔伯恩大道。椭圆南侧的街道布局也大体相仿。原先中央这块地上有九座房子，被余下四座的业主拆掉了，由这几位业主辟出一座四宅共享的私家花园。于是中央椭圆的南侧没有建筑，只有一堵墙，每隔一段便开一扇素净的园门——这在伦敦郊外的宅子里是常见的格局。

===Original===
In reality, the roadway to the north and south of the plot is all Amberscombe Gardens, but the oval which curves round to the north was, at the period this story covers, known to the police as “Crime Street,” and in this description the nine houses on both sides of the northern curve were involved.
Number One, the most modest of all the buildings, was in the occupation of Dr. Philip Garon, an American practitioner who made frequent visits across the Atlantic and invariably returned to deposit a very handsome surplus in the local branch of the London and Western Counties Bank. Dr. Garon was successful as a result of the sublime assurance of all oceangoing passengers, that the notice, conspicuously displayed in the smoking-room warning passengers not to play cards with strangers, did not apply to them.
Number Three, a pretty house smothered in clematis in the proper season of the year, with its white window sashes and its sober red front, was the town house of Mr. Cunningham, who, apparently, had no initial and no Christian name. He was known to his intimate friends as Mush, the derivation of which is a little obscure. Mr. Cunningham described himself as independent, which meant no more than that he was independent of the ordinary necessities of making an honest living. In a sense, he was by far the best known of the Colony, for Mush had served two terms of penal servitude, one in an English and one in a French prison. He had the reputation of being able to cut holes in steel safes with a greater rapidity than any other gentleman in his profession, and it is said, probably with truth, that he had improved upon the oxy-hydrogen jet and had introduced a new element which shortened the work by half.

===Chinese===
实际上，这块地南北两面的路都叫安伯斯库姆花园，但那个向北弯的椭圆，在我们这个故事发生的年代，警方称之为“犯罪街”，这个称呼涵盖了北面弧线两侧的九座房子。
1 号是全部房子里最不起眼的一座，住着菲利普·加龙医生，一位美国执业医师，常在大西洋两岸往返，每次回来必定往伦敦西区郡银行的本地分行存进一笔可观的外快。加龙医生的成功，全赖远洋乘客们那份崇高的自信——他们坚信吸烟室里醒目张贴的“请勿与陌生人玩牌”的告示，说的绝不是自己。
3 号是一座漂亮房子，到了时节便爬满铁线莲，白色窗框，暗红门面，是坎宁安先生在城里的宅邸——此人名前似乎没有缩写字母，也没有教名。至交们都叫他“蘑菇”，这个绰号的来历有点说不清。坎宁安先生自称“独立人士”，意思不过是：靠老实谋生糊口这种寻常营生，他已经独立在外了。从某种意义上说，他是这殖民地里名气最大的一个，因为蘑菇服过两期苦役，一期在英国监狱，一期在法国监狱。他有项响亮的名声：在钢制保险柜上开洞，比他这一行的任何别的绅士都利索。据说——多半是真的——他还改良了氢氧喷焰，添了一种新元素，把工时缩短了一半。

===Original===
The tenant of Number Five was a gentleman, benign of countenance and very good to the poor. He was called the Bishop by friends and foes alike. His real name was Brown and he had been concerned in more bank swindles than any of the other colonists, though he had only one conviction to his discredit and that a comparative flea-bite of nine months’ hard labour.
The owner of Number Seven was described as “Mr. Colling Jacques, Civil Engineer,” in the local directories. The official police “Who’s Who” noted that he was a wonderful pistol shot, and recorded, in parenthesis, that on the occasion of his arrest in connection with the smashing of the Bank of Holland, no weapon was found upon him. It was also added that there was no conviction against him in England, though he, too, had seen the inside of a French prison.
Number Nine was pointed out to sightseers, with a certain amount of local pride by the guide, as the home of Millet the forger, who had received on one occasion a fifteen years’ sentence, but had been released after serving two years, an act of grace on the part of the authorities which earned for him a certain unpopularity with his peers and was held to be not unconnected with the subsequent arrest of a few of his former associates, the suggestion being that Mr. Millet had turned King’s evidence.

===Chinese===
5 号的房客是位面目慈祥、乐善好施的先生。朋友和仇家都管他叫“主教”。他的真姓是布朗，卷入过的银行骗局比殖民地里任何人都多，只是定罪记录仅有一条，而且不过是九个月苦役，算是虼蚤咬一口的小意思。
在当地的行名录上，7 号的业主写作“科林·雅克先生，土木工程师”。警方的官方“名人录”注明他是了不得的枪手，并加括号记录：因荷兰银行倒闭案被捕时，他身上没有搜出武器。还补了一句：他在英格兰虽无前科，却也进过法国监狱的铁窗。
导游领着游客参观时，会带几分本地自豪地指给他们看 9 号——伪造犯米利特先生的府上。此人曾一次被判十五年，却只服了两年刑就获释。当局这项恩典，让他在同行里颇不得人心，而且人们认为，他几个老伙伴后来相继落网，与此事不无关系——言下之意是，米利特先生当了污点证人。

===Original===
At Number Two, on the “oval” side of the street, lived H. Mulberry, a respectable and methodical man, who went to his little office in Chancery Lane every morning of his life by the 9:15 and returned to his home at exactly 5:30 p.m. year in and year out. Mulberry was a begging letter writer on a magnificent scale. He had a wonderful literary style which seldom failed to extract the necessary emolument which he sought.
Number Four, a much larger house, indeed the second largest in Crime Street, was the habitat of “Señor Gregori, a teacher of languages.” Unfortunately for him, he had in the course of his thrilling career taught other things than the liquid tongue of Spain. For example, he had taught the Bank of Chile that their “unforgeable” notes which, it was boasted, defied photographic reproduction could be turned out by the tens of thousands and that the six tints in which a gold bond was printed offered no insuperable difficulty to a clever craftsman with an artist’s eye and a sense of colour.
In Number Eight lived the two brothers Thomas and Francis Stockmar of Austrian extraction, who were described as political refugees but were undoubtedly criminals of a peculiarly dangerous type. The Stockmars were dour, white-faced men with short bristling hair and were certainly the least presentable of all the colonists.

===Chinese===
在街的“椭圆”一侧，2 号住着 H. 马尔伯里，一位体面而一丝不苟的先生，一年到头，每天早上坐 9 点 15 分的车去他在大法官巷的小办公室，下午 5 点 30 分整准时回家。马尔伯里先生是个大规模经营乞讨信的写手。他有一手漂亮文章，要提取他谋求的那笔润资，很少落空。
4 号的房子大得多，其实是犯罪街第二大宅，住着“语言教师格雷戈里先生”。遗憾的是，在他那段惊险的生涯里，他教过的可不止西班牙那门流利的语言。比如说，他教给了智利银行一个道理：他们那号称照相机也奈何不得的“不可伪造”钞票，可以成千上万地造出来；金券上印的六种颜色，在一位有画家眼力和色彩感的巧匠手里，也算不上什么翻不过去的难关。
8 号住着托马斯、弗朗西斯·斯托克马尔兄弟，奥地利裔，名义上是政治流亡者，实际上无疑是特别危险的一类罪犯。斯托克马尔兄弟生性阴郁，脸色苍白，头发短硬，在这些殖民者里无疑是最拿不出门的两位。

===Original===
Number Six has been left to the last, for this was the most important house in Crime Street. It was a story higher than any other, built squarely, with no attempt at beauty. It is said that the third floor consisted of one room and that from its many windows it was possible to command, not only all the approaches to the northern side of the gardens, but those to the south; it has even been suggested that it was so planned, that, in case of necessity, the house could be converted into a fortress, from the third floor of which a last desperate stand might be made. This then was Number Six, the abiding place of Colonel Westhanger and his brilliant niece.
Michael Pretherston was no stranger to Crime Street. He had made many visits to this locality, and it had been at his initiative that the roadway of Amberscombe Gardens had been dug up one fine morning by a gang of road-breakers and there had been revealed that remarkable subterranean passage which connected the one side of the street with the other. The passageway led from the summer house in the gardens of the oval to a stable in Number Three.
The Colonists, however, swore stoutly that they knew nothing whatever of the existence of this passage and that it must have existed years before they came to the street. The civil engineer, Colling Jacques, pointed out to the district surveyor that the very character of the passage suggested that this was some storm water drain which had been laid down and forgotten by the contractor. Or else it had been laid down in error and the contractor had been either too lazy or too rushed to break it up. There were many other explanations, none of which was wholly acceptable.

===Chinese===
6 号留到最后才讲，因为它是犯罪街最重要的一座房子。它比谁家都高出一层，方方正正，全不求美观。据说三楼是一整间屋子，凭它那许多扇窗子，不但能俯瞰花园北侧的所有通道，连南侧的也在眼底；甚至有人断言，这宅子是照着必要时刻可以改作堡垒的图样盖的，从三楼还可以作最后一搏。这便是 6 号，韦斯特汉杰上校和他那位聪明绝顶的侄女的居所。
迈克尔·普雷瑟斯顿对犯罪街并不陌生。他来这一带勘查过许多回，正是由于他的提议，某个晴朗的早晨，一队筑路工把安伯斯库姆花园的路面刨开，挖出了那条把街两边连通起来的著名地下通道。通道一头是椭圆花园里的凉亭，另一头通到 3 号的马厩。
然而殖民者们赌咒发誓，说这条通道的存在他们压根不知情，还说它必定在他们搬来之前好些年就有了。那位土木工程师科林·雅克向区测绘官指出：单看这条通道的构造，它就该是一条雨水排水管，是包工头埋下后忘掉的。要不然就是埋错了地方，包工头不是懒得拆，就是忙得顾不上拆。旁的解释还有很多，没有一条能完全站得住。

===Original===
Michael, swinging his stick, passed that portion of the road in which the passage had run and wondered with a reminiscent smile where the new tunnel was, for that there was a new one, he did not doubt.
Night was falling, and Dr. Philip Garon’s dining-room windows blazed with light. Mr. Mulberry’s, on the right, was more modestly illuminated. Mr. Cunningham’s house was in darkness, as also was “The Bishop’s.” There were lights in the bedroom at Number Seven but Number Six was black as also was Number Eight.
He saw Millet standing at his garden gate, smoking, and crossed the road toward him, realizing that the keen-eyed gentleman had already observed his presence. Millet, a florid man with a genial, almost fulsome, manner met him with a friendly nod.

===Chinese===
迈克尔挥着手杖，走过通道原先经过的那段路，带着追忆的微笑暗自琢磨：新的地道会在哪儿呢——至于有没有新地道，他毫不怀疑。
夜幕四合。菲利普·加龙医生家饭厅的窗户灯火通明。右首马尔伯里先生家的灯就收敛得多。坎宁安先生家一片漆黑，“主教”家也是如此。7 号的卧室里有灯光，6 号却黑着，8 号也黑着。
他看见米利特先生站在自家园门口抽烟，便穿过马路向他走去，心里明白这位眼尖的先生早已发现了自己。米利特先生满面红光，态度殷勤得近乎过头，友好地点头招呼他。

===Original===
“Good evening, Mr. Pretherston,” he said. “I hope you are not looking for trouble.”
Michael leant on the top bar of the gate and shook his head.
“I shouldn’t come here for trouble,” he said; “this is the most law-abiding spot in London.”
Mr. Millet sighed and murmured something about misfortunes which overtake mankind and added a pious expression of his desire to forget the past and to end his days in that security and peace which sin denies its votaries.
“Very pretty,” said Michael blandly, “and how are all our good neighbours? I was thinking of taking a house here myself. By-the-way,” he added innocently, “I suppose you don’t know any that are to be let?”
Mr. Millet shook his head.

===Chinese===
“晚上好，普雷瑟斯顿先生，”他说，“但愿您不是来找麻烦的。”
迈克尔倚着园门最上一道横档，摇了摇头。
“我不会到这儿来找麻烦，”他说，“这是全伦敦最守法的一角。”
米利特先生叹了口气，喃喃说了几句人生难免灾祸之类的话，又虔诚地表露了一番心愿：但愿忘掉过去，在安宁与平静中了此残生——毕竟罪恶是不肯让它的信徒得到这两种东西的。
“说得真动听，”迈克尔淡淡地说，“咱们那些好邻居们都好吗？我还琢磨着自己也来这儿赁座房子呢。顺便问一句，”他一脸无辜地补充道，“您可知道哪家要出租？”
米利特先生摇了摇头。

===Original===
“I am all alone here,” he said, “if you were really serious about wishing to live in this neighbourhood, I should be honoured to act as your host, Mr. Pretherston.”
“And how is Kate?” demanded Michael, ignoring the invitation.
“Kate?” asked the puzzled Mr. Millet; “oh, you mean, Miss Westhanger. I haven’t seen her for several days﻿—I think it was last Tuesday afternoon I saw her last.”
“Yes, at 2:30 in the afternoon,” mocked Michael, “she was wearing a blue dress with white spots and a green hat with an ostrich feather. You remember her distinctly because she dropped her bag and you crossed to pick it up. You needn’t start the alibi factory working, Millet; I have nothing against Kate for the moment.”
Mr. Millet laughed softly.
“You will have your joke,” he said.

===Chinese===
“我在这儿孤零零一个人，”他说，“您要是真想住到这一带来，能给您当个房东，我可就荣幸了，普雷瑟斯顿先生。”
“凯特怎么样？”迈克尔问道，把邀请晾在一边。
“凯特？”米利特先生一脸茫然，“哦，您是说韦斯特汉杰小姐。我好几天没见着她了——上回见着，我想是上星期二下午。”
“不错，下午 2 点 30 分，”迈克尔讥讽道，“她穿一件白点蓝裙，戴一顶缀着鸵鸟毛的绿帽子。您记得清清楚楚，因为她掉了手提包，是您穿过马路帮她捡的。您不必开动不在场证明工厂了，米利特；眼下我手里没有针对凯特的东西。”
米利特先生轻轻笑了。
“您就爱说笑。”他说。

===Original===
“I will,” said Michael with grim emphasis, “but it is going to be a long time developing. I haven’t seen the Stockmars lately either.”
“I never see them at all,” Mr. Millet hastened to state. “I have very little in common with foreigners. Whatever there is against me, Mr. Pretherston, I am a patriot through and through. I am proud to be English and I don’t take kindly to foreign gentlemen and never will.”
“Your patriotism does you credit, Millet,” said the detective dryly as he prepared to move on. “I wish you would be patriotic enough to give me a tip as to what game is on,” he lowered his voice. “You know all that is happening here and you might do yourself a little bit of good.”
“If I knew anything,” said the other earnestly, “I would tell you in a moment, Mr. Pretherston, but here I am, out of the world, so to speak. Nobody ever consults me and I am glad they don’t. I want to be left alone to forget the past﻿—”
“Cut all that Little Eva stuff out, Uncle Tom,” said Michael coarsely.
Other eyes had watched Michael, from behind blinds, through unsuspected peepholes, a dozen pairs of eyes had followed him as he took his slow promenade along Crime Street.

===Chinese===
“我是爱说笑，”迈克尔冷冷地加重语气，“不过这个笑话要酝酿很久才能成型。斯托克马尔兄弟我近来也没见着。”
“我跟他们素无往来，”米利特先生连忙声明，“我跟外国人少有共同语言。不管我身上有什么罪名，普雷瑟斯顿先生，我是个彻头彻尾的爱国者。我以自己是英国人为荣，外国先生们我看着就不亲热，以后也亲热不起来。”
“你这颗爱国心给你增光，米利特，”侦探淡淡说着，准备动身，“你要是能爱国到给我透个风、说说眼下在玩哪一出就好了，”他压低嗓音，“这儿的一举一动你全知道，兴许也能给自己捞点好处。”
“我要是真知道什么，”对方一脸恳切，“立时三刻就告诉您了，普雷瑟斯顿先生。可我如今，可以说是与世隔绝。谁也不来讨教我，我也乐得没人来。我只求别来打扰，让我忘掉过去——”
“收起你那套小伊娃的煽情戏吧，汤姆大叔。”迈克尔毫不客气地说。
另一些眼睛一直在注视着迈克尔——从百叶窗后头，从无人察觉的窥孔里，十几双眼睛跟随着他沿犯罪街缓缓踱步。

===Original===
Colonel Westhanger, a tall, grey man, stood in that big room on the third floor of his house, his hands folded behind him, his chin upon his breast, following every movement of the detective. Gregori, handsome and lithe, stood at his elbow, shading the glow of his cigarette in the palm of his hand.
“Colonel *mio*,” he said softly, “I would give much for an opportunity of meeting that gentleman in a nice dark passage, in one of those old Harrison Ainsworth houses which were providentially built over a river.”
“You will have your wish one of these days,” said the Colonel gruffly; “I don’t like that fellow. He is not one of the ordinary run of policemen. They are bad enough, but this fellow knows too much.”
He nibbled his white moustache, shook his head and turned away from the window as Michael took his farewell of the forger.
“Watch him on the other side,” he said, “and send one of the boys out to follow him.”
He descended the thickly carpeted stairs to the first floor, which was the living suite. The drawing-room in which he turned was a beautifully furnished apartment, and the girl who had been sitting at the piano, her nimble hands running over the keys, looked up as he entered.

===Chinese===
韦斯特汉杰上校是个瘦高的灰发男人，站在自家三楼那间大屋子里，双手交叠背后，下巴抵着胸口，追踪着侦探的一举一动。格雷戈里先生英俊利落，立在他肘边，用手掌掩着香烟的火光。
“我亲爱的上校，”他柔声说，“要是能有机会，在某条漆黑合宜的过道里会一会那位先生，要我付出什么代价都行——那过道还得在哈里森·安斯沃思笔下那种老宅子里，老天有眼，那些宅子偏偏都盖在河上。”
“迟早有一天会让你如愿。”上校粗声粗气地说，“我不喜欢那家伙。他不是警察里寻常的路数。寻常警察已经够难缠了，这家伙偏偏懂得太多。”
他咬着白胡须，摇了摇头，在迈克尔与那位伪造犯道别时，从窗前转过身去。
“到那头盯着他，”他说，“派个小子出去跟他的梢。”
他顺着铺着厚地毯的楼梯下到二楼——那是起居的套间。他转身走进的那间客厅陈设精美，原本坐在钢琴前的姑娘正让灵巧的双手在琴键上跑动，见他进来，抬起了头。

## X Sir Ralph Lost a Princess and Found a Policeman / 十 拉尔夫爵士丢了位公主，找了位警察

===Original===
Michael took the card from the uniformed constable and raised his eyebrows in surprise.
“Sir Ralph Sapson,” he said, “what the dickens does he want?”
The constable made no reply, for he was neither thought-reader nor inquisitive.
“Show him in,” said Michael.

===Chinese===
迈克尔从制服警员手里接过名片，惊奇地扬起了眉毛。
“拉尔夫·萨普森爵士。”他说，“他葫芦里卖的什么药？”
警员没有应声——他既不会读心术，也没有好奇心。
“请他进来。”迈克尔说。

===Original===
Sir Ralph Sapson had never before called at Scotland House or showed the slightest desire to improve his acquaintance with Michael and the visit was therefore a little puzzling. Ralph bustled in, less important than usual and probably somewhat overawed by the difficulty he had experienced in reaching his objective.
